# MacroFit Mobile — UI/UX Audit

Generated by an 18-agent audit: 8 auditors read every route and component in full, 8 adversarial verifiers re-opened each cited file and voted CONFIRMED / ADJUSTED / REFUTED, and 2 synthesis agents produced the ADRs and the fix plan. Every finding below survived verification against real code.

| | |
|---|---|
| Findings after verification | **231** |
| Refuted and dropped | 2 |
| P0 — data loss, crash, or core task impossible | **7** |
| P1 — core task materially broken or misplaced | **43** |
| P2 — friction a real user notices | 130 |
| P3 — nits | 51 |
| Baseline | `npx tsc --noEmit` exits 0 — no type errors |

Severity: **P0** the user cannot complete a core task, loses data, or the app crashes. **P1** the task is completable but materially broken, misplaced, or misleading. **P2** friction a real user notices. **P3** nit.

## What the findings are actually about

| Category | Count |
|---|---|
| data-correctness | 54 |
| broken-flow | 39 |
| information-architecture | 32 |
| performance | 30 |
| design-system | 22 |
| feedback-state | 17 |
| accessibility | 16 |
| discoverability | 7 |
| empty-error-state | 6 |
| copy | 6 |
| crash | 1 |
| layout | 1 |

The largest bucket is **data correctness**, not visual polish. That matters for how this is read: the app looks finished, and most of what is wrong is invisible until it has already cost the user something.

## P0 — fix before anything else

The 7 P0 findings are four distinct defects; three of them were found independently by more than one auditor, from different screens.

### P0-1 — Health Connect import destroys hand-entered weight history and rewinds current weight by up to a year

_Found independently by 3 auditors (ia-navigation, progress-goals, profile-auth)._

**Google Fit / Health Connect import is implemented twice, on two screens, with contradictory overwrite guarantees** — [app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1073](app/(tabs)/progress.tsx#L1073) · verdict `ADJUSTED` · effort M

- **Code:** `app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1073-1137` renders an 'Import from Google Fit' card that calls `getHistoricalData({ daysBack: 365 })` and then `handleConfirmImport` (line 672) loops `addWeightEntry({ date: w.date, weight: … })` for every record. `addWeightEntry` in `src/core/store/appState.ts:321` does `state.weightLog = [entry, ...state.weightLog.filter(w => w.date !== entryData.date)]` — it replaces any existing entry for that date. `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:360-409` renders a second Health Connect section calling `health.importWeightHistory()` whose adjacent copy promises "days you already logged yourself are never overwritten" (lines 371-373).
- **What the user experiences:** Two controls with the same name in two tabs behave differently. The Progress one silently replaces up to a year of hand-entered weigh-ins with scale/phone values the user may not trust, after a 'Confirm & Import' button that reads like an additive action. The Profile one does not. Whichever the user finds first determines whether their own data survives.
- **Fix:** Delete the import Surface at app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1073-1137 together with handleHealthSync/handleConfirmImport and their three state hooks (632-689), and let useHealthSync().importWeightHistory() on Profile be the only importer — it already dedupes by date. If the Progress entry point must survive short-term, at minimum reuse the same dedupe filter before the addWeightEntry loop at 675-681.

**Google Fit import sets "current weight" to the user's weight from a year ago** — [app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:675](app/(tabs)/progress.tsx#L675) · verdict `CONFIRMED` · effort S

- **Code:** handleConfirmImport loops `for (const w of healthSyncResult.weights) addWeightEntry({...})`. health.sync.ts:33 returns that array sorted NEWEST FIRST (`.sort((a, b) => b.date.localeCompare(a.date))`), and appState.ts:319-330 `addWeightEntry` unconditionally does `state.currentWeightKg = latestKg` from whatever entry it was just handed. The final loop iteration is therefore the OLDEST record in the 365-day window (`getHistoricalData({ daysBack: 365 })`, line 661).
- **What the user experiences:** A user taps "Confirm & Import" to bring in their Google Fit history and their current weight silently becomes their weight from up to a year ago. Every downstream number is then wrong: BMR/TDEE and the three preset calorie anchors on Goals (goals.tsx:344-348, 729), BMI and the profile header (profile.tsx:219, 333), coach protein g/kg (coachAlerts.ts:298), the local plan's protein (localRecommendation.ts:123), and the whole weight-target card's remaining/fraction/ETA (weightTarget.ts:187-194).
- **Fix:** In handleConfirmImport, iterate oldest-first (`[...healthSyncResult.weights].sort((a,b) => a.date.localeCompare(b.date))`), or leave the loop alone and follow it with an explicit `setCurrentWeight()` derived from the newest-dated entry. Longer term, appState.addWeightEntry should set currentWeightKg from the newest entry in the log, not from the entry it was just given (upstream web-repo change).

**'Sync from Google Fit' destroys hand-logged weigh-ins and rewinds current weight to the oldest imported record, with no warning and no undo** — [app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:675](app/(tabs)/progress.tsx#L675) · verdict `ADJUSTED` · effort M

- **Code:** handleConfirmImport loops `for (const w of healthSyncResult.weights) addWeightEntry({...})` with no dedupe against existing entries (lines 675-681). addWeightEntry does `state.weightLog = [entry, ...state.weightLog.filter(w => w.date !== entryData.date)]` — same-date entries are replaced (appState.ts:321) — and then unconditionally sets `state.currentWeightKg` from the entry just added (lines 324-329). health.sync.ts sorts weights `(a, b) => b.date.localeCompare(a.date)` (line 33), newest first, so the final loop iteration is the OLDEST record. The sibling path in src/hooks/useHealthSync.ts:92-94 explicitly guards this ('Existing days win'); this one does not.
- **What the user experiences:** A user who has hand-logged weigh-ins for two months taps 'Sync from Google Fit' and confirms. Every hand-entered day that also exists in Health Connect is silently replaced by the device reading, and current weight jumps to whatever they weighed 365 days ago. BMI, TDEE, the weight-goal countdown and every 'on track' verdict are now computed from a year-old number, with no undo.
- **Fix:** Reuse the guarded path: filter the incoming weights against `new Set(weightLog.map(e => e.date))` before writing, and add the entries oldest-first so currentWeightKg ends on the newest. Better, delete src/lib/health/ entirely and route the Progress button through useHealthSync.importWeightHistory. Also fix useHealthSync so currentWeightKg is only updated when the newest imported date is newer than the newest existing one.

### P0-2 — Sign-out never clears local state; the next account inherits the previous user's health record

_Found independently by 3 auditors (ia-navigation, profile-auth)._

**Signing out never clears the persisted store, so the next account on the device inherits the previous user's diary and skips onboarding** — [src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:201](src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx#L201) · verdict `CONFIRMED` · effort M

- **Code:** `signOut` (`src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:201-204`) calls `flushSave()` then `supabase.auth.signOut()` — it never resets zustand nor clears the `macrofit-storage` AsyncStorage key created in `src/store/useStore.ts:26-27`. On the next sign-in, `loadUserData` returns early for an account with no saved row: `if (!result || !('data' in result) || result.error || !result.data?.data) return` (line 67), so `hydrateStore` never runs. `onboardedAt` is in `SYNC_FIELDS` (line 44) and is what the router gates on (`app/_layout.tsx:43` `const needsSetup = onboardedAt === null`).
- **What the user experiences:** User A signs out, user B signs in on the same phone. B is routed straight past onboarding into the tabs and sees A's diary, A's weigh-ins, A's targets and A's name on the Profile header — and the debounced auto-save at `AuthProvider.tsx:137-144` then writes all of A's data up to B's account.
- **Fix:** Reset state on sign-out in `src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx`: after `supabase.auth.signOut()`, call `useStore.persist.clearStorage()` and re-seed defaults (an upstream web-repo change is needed if a `resetStore` action is wanted in `src/core/store/appState.ts`, which is generated). Also make `loadUserData`'s no-row branch explicitly reset rather than silently `return`.

**Sign-out never clears local state, so the next account inherits the previous user's diary, weights and profile** — [src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:201](src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx#L201) · verdict `CONFIRMED` · effort M

- **Code:** signOut is exactly `flushSave()` then `await supabase.auth.signOut()` (lines 201-204). A repo-wide search for SIGNED_OUT, clearStorage, AsyncStorage.clear, removeItem and `persist.` returns zero matches, so nothing resets the store. The zustand persist key 'macrofit-storage' (src/store/useStore.ts:26) survives sign-out with profile, diary, weightLog and onboardedAt intact. When user B signs in, loadUserData(B.id) uses `.single()`, which for a user with no row returns an error, and the guard `if (!result || !('data' in result) || result.error || !result.data?.data) return` (line 67) bails without hydrating.
- **What the user experiences:** User A signs out on a shared or handed-down phone. User B signs in and lands straight in the tabs (A's onboardedAt is non-null so onboarding is skipped entirely), sees A's name, A's weight log, A's BMI and A's food diary. Worse, the debounced auto-save (line 137-144) then uploads A's data into B's user_data row, permanently attaching A's health record to B's account.
- **Fix:** Add a `resetStore()` action to the store (upstream web repo change in src/core/store/appState.ts) that reassigns every SYNC_FIELDS key to its default, then call it plus `useStore.persist.clearStorage()` in AuthProvider.signOut before `supabase.auth.signOut()`, and again in onAuthStateChange when event === 'SIGNED_OUT'. Also reset when the SIGNED_IN user id differs from the previously stored one. Await flushSave() first so the outgoing user's last edits are actually written.

**A slow or failed cloud load silently falls through to defaults, and the auto-save then overwrites the account's server data** — [src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:63](src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx#L63) · verdict `CONFIRMED` · effort L

- **Code:** loadUserData races the query against `new Promise<null>(resolve => setTimeout(() => resolve(null), 8000))` and on timeout returns without hydrating and without setting any error state (lines 63-78). isHydratingRef stays false. The store subscription at lines 137-144 then saves on any change: `saveUserData(userRef.current.id, collect())` upserts the whole `data` blob built from the current in-memory store.
- **What the user experiences:** A returning user installs the app on a new phone on hotel wifi. getSession succeeds, the data fetch times out at 8s, loading flips false, onboardedAt is null locally so the gate routes them to onboarding. They complete the 5 steps; completeOnboarding/updateProfile/updateGoals mutate the store; 1.5s later the debounced save upserts the fresh default state over their real row. Months of diary, weight log and workouts are gone from the server with no warning and no error shown.
- **Fix:** Track a `hydrationOutcome` of 'ok' | 'empty' | 'failed' in AuthProvider. Distinguish a genuine no-row result (PostgREST code PGRST116) from a network/timeout failure; on 'failed' set syncStatus to 'error', block all saves for that session, and render a blocking retry banner rather than routing on a store that never loaded. Never let a save fire for a user whose load did not complete.

### P0-3 — Weight persists in the display unit, so flipping kg/lbs reinterprets every historical weigh-in


**Weight log is persisted in the display unit, so switching kg/lbs corrupts every historical entry** — [app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:735](app/(tabs)/progress.tsx#L735) · verdict `CONFIRMED` · effort L

- **Code:** progress.tsx:735-742 comments "weightLog stores each entry in the unit the user was using — no conversion here" and plots `w.weight` raw, while the axis caption and every label use `unitLabel` derived from the CURRENT setting (line 691, 982, 1018, 1041). WeightTarget.tsx:82-83 writes the raw typed number ("Stored in the user's display unit"). src/core/types/index.ts:89 declares `weight: number // in user's preferred unit`. Meanwhile profile.tsx:510 comments "Only the display unit changes; stored values stay metric" and renders `${entry.weight} ${unit}` (line 552). The design rule is "Weights persist in kg always; convert only at input/display boundary."
- **What the user experiences:** A user logs six months at 80 kg, then flips Weight unit to lbs in Profile. Every chart point, the weight-log list, the Latest tile and the target card now read "80.0 lb". weightTarget.ts:60-61 and coachAlerts.ts:103-104 then divide the entire history by 2.20462, so the trend, the on-track status, the ETA and the coach alerts are all computed from a body that never existed. Nothing warns them and there is no way to undo it.
- **Fix:** Upstream web-repo change to src/core: store WeightEntry.weight in kg always and convert at the input boundary. On the mobile side that means WeightTarget.tsx:79-84 converts the typed value to kg before calling addWeightEntry, progress.tsx:736-742 converts kg→display when building weightPoints, and profile.tsx:552 formats through kgToLbs. Ship a one-time migration keyed on the unit stored at write time, otherwise existing accounts are re-interpreted on upgrade.

## Findings by area

### Information architecture & navigation

#### P1 (10)

<details><summary><b>The one global floating action button opens the AI chat; logging food has no global affordance at all</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:151</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `AssistantButton` is mounted once outside `<Tabs>` (`app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:189-201`) and its only action is `router.push('/chat')` (line 151). It is a 56pt jade FAB pinned at `bottom: Math.max(insets.bottom, 8) + 74`, visible on all five tabs. The comment at lines 132-138 states it exists because "app/chat.tsx ... was unreachable". Meanwhile `/food-search` is pushed from exactly one place in the entire app — `app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:563` — and `/lift-picker` from exactly one — `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:713`.

**User impact.** The single most prominent, always-reachable control in the app is given to a feature a user touches occasionally, while the action they perform 3-6 times a day (log a food) is four taps and a scroll away behind a tab switch. From a cold open on the Dashboard, logging breakfast is: Diary tab (1) → scroll past DateNavigator + DayTotals → 'Add food' on the Breakfast card (2) → tap a result row (3) → 'Add to Breakfast' (4). Logging a set is worse: Workout tab (1) → 'Start workout' (2) → 'Add exercise' (3) → tap a lift (4) → 'Add set' (5) → type weight + reps → tick (6).

**Fix.** Repoint the FAB in `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` at logging, not chat: on the Dashboard/Diary push `/food-search` with `{ meal: inferredFromClock, date: getTodayString() }`; on the Workout tab, when `activeWorkoutId` is set, push `/lift-picker`. Chat keeps the header IconButton it already has on three screens. Alternatives considered: (a) leave the chat FAB and add a second FAB for logging — rejected, two competing FABs in the same corner is the classic thumb-conflict and the design doc restricts glass FABs to one; (b) turn the FAB into a speed-dial (Log food / Log weight / Ask AI) — rejected because it adds a tap to the highest-frequency path to save a tap on the lowest, and a menu on press is a poor fit for the 250ms enter motion budget; (c) make the FAB context-sensitive per tab — this is the recommendation, because the primary verb genuinely differs by tab (log food on Dashboard/Diary/Progress, add lift on Workout, nothing on Profile) and it costs one `useSegments()` read.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:189-201 mounts <AssistantButton /> once, as a sibling of <Tabs>, so it renders over all five tabs; its onPress at 149-152 is Haptics + router.push('/chat') and nothing else. Geometry at 153-174 is exactly as described (right:18, bottom: Math.max(insets.bottom,8)+74, 56x56). I grepped the whole repo: router.push to '/food-search' appears only at app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:563 and to '/lift-picker' only at app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:713. I also walked both counts: from the Dashboard, Diary tab -> MealCard 'Add food' (diary.tsx:559-564) -> FoodRow (food-search.tsx:478) -> 'Add to {meal}' (food-search.tsx:663-669) is 4 taps; the workout count of 6 is right too (workout.tsx:1181 Start workout, 779/820 Add exercise, lift-picker LiftRow, workout.tsx:601 Add set, then the check Pressable at 319). The app's single most prominent global control is spent on the lowest-frequency verb.

</details>

<details><summary><b>/goals — the coach plan and the only place calorie/macro targets can be edited — is reachable from one card on one screen</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:350</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** A repo-wide grep for `/goals` across `app/` returns exactly two hits: the comment at `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:336` and `<Link href="/goals" asChild>` at line 350. Nothing in Profile, Progress, Diary or Workout links to it. `app/goals.tsx` is the sole owner of `updateGoals` (line 409), of the coach `Accept plan` / `Refresh` buttons (lines 597-612), of the predicted-vs-measured TDEE panel (625-691) and of the manual macro-gram fields (767-847). The Dashboard card that reaches it is styled as a passive summary — a `Pressable` wrapped `GlassSurface` whose only affordance cue is a `ChevronRight` (line 406).

**User impact.** A user who wants to change their calorie target goes to Profile — the screen literally labelled 'Targets' at `profile.tsx:411` — and finds only a goal-weight field. The actual targets screen, and the AI coach that is the product's differentiator, are behind an unlabelled chevron on a card most users will read as a status readout, not a link.

**Fix.** Add a second entry from `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`: convert the existing 'Targets' Section (line 411) into a row that pushes `/goals`, keeping goal weight there but linking calories/macros out. Alternatives considered: (a) promote Goals/Coach to a sixth tab — rejected, six tabs breaks the 5-slot bar and Coach is weekly-frequency, not daily; (b) move the whole of goals.tsx into the Profile tab as another `<Section>` — rejected, it is a 900-line screen with its own hero, alerts and two-column figure grid, and burying it under a disclosure triangle in a nine-section accordion makes discoverability worse; (c) keep the Dashboard card as the hero entry AND add a Profile row — recommended, because the Dashboard card is the right *contextual* entry (it shows the current plan) while Profile is where users go hunting for settings, and both cost one line.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Repo-wide grep for '/goals' outside src/core returns exactly two hits, both in app/(tabs)/index.tsx (the comment at 336 and <Link href="/goals" asChild> at 350). No Link, router.push or href to /goals exists in profile.tsx, progress.tsx, diary.tsx, workout.tsx or chat.tsx. goals.tsx really is the sole owner of updateGoals (409), Accept plan / Refresh (597-612), the predicted-vs-measured burn panel (625-691) and the manual calorie + macro-gram fields (708-847). Profile's Section titled 'Targets' (profile.tsx:411-435) contains one goal-weight Field and nothing else. The Dashboard card does carry accessibilityRole="link" and a spoken label ending 'Open goals.' (index.tsx:344-347), so it is not literally unlabelled for screen readers — but visually it is a summary card with a ChevronRight (406), and it is the only door. Corroborated by onboarding.tsx:670, which tells the user 'every number is editable under Profile.'

</details>

<details><summary><b>Two screens both claim to own 'targets', each holding half of them</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:411</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:411-435` renders `<Section title="Targets" icon={<Target …>}` whose entire content is one goal-weight `Field` (line 421) writing `profile.targetWeightKg`. `app/goals.tsx` is titled 'Goals' with subtitle 'Your coach plan and your own numbers' (lines 426-427) and owns calories, protein, carbs, fat, fiber and the phase presets. Separately, Profile's 'About you' section mutates `profile.goal` and `profile.activityLevel` and calls `recalculateGoals()` (lines 475-491) — which silently overwrites the calorie/macro numbers the user set on the Goals screen, with no warning on either screen.

**User impact.** Changing 'Activity level' on Profile silently rewrites a calorie target the user carefully typed on /goals or accepted from the coach; nothing on Profile says so. And a user looking for 'my targets' has to know that goal *weight* is on Profile while calorie *targets* are on a screen Profile does not link to.

**Fix.** Make /goals the single owner of every numeric target: replace the body of profile.tsx's 'Targets' Section (411-435) with the goal-weight Field plus a navigation row to /goals. Then gate the silent recalculation — recalculateGoals() is called from profile.tsx:249 (commitBasics), :477, :490 and :504; at minimum add an inline note beside the Choice controls and, better, only recalculate when state.goals still equals the formula output, otherwise ask first.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** profile.tsx:411-435 is a Section titled 'Targets' whose whole body is the goal-weight Field (421) writing profile.targetWeightKg; goals.tsx:426-427 is titled 'Goals' / 'Your coach plan and your own numbers' and owns calories, protein, carbs, fat, fiber and the Cut/Maintain/Bulk presets. The silent-overwrite claim is not just true, it is worse than stated: recalculateGoals (src/core/store/appState.ts:226-239) does Object.assign(state.goals, calculateMacroGoals(...)), and calculateMacroGoals (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:36-45) returns calories, protein, carbs, fat, fiber, sugar, sodium AND water — so every numeric target the user typed on /goals is rewritten, including sodium and water which the coach's own acceptRecommendation (appState.ts:451-470) deliberately preserves. Nothing on either screen warns about it.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The rest timer is an inline list item at the top of the workout scroll, so it is off-screen exactly when it is running</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:770</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `<RestTimer triggerKey={restKey} onDismiss={() => setRestKey(0)} />` is rendered at `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:770`, immediately after the session `GlassSurface` and *before* the exercise cards (lines 772-817). It is a plain `<View>` in the `Screen`'s ScrollView — no `position: 'absolute'`, no sticky header. The trigger fires from `onChangeSet` at lines 796-804 when a working set is ticked off.

**User impact.** You tick off set 3 of your fourth exercise — which is several screens down — and a countdown starts at the very top of the page where you cannot see it. To read your remaining rest you scroll up, losing your place in the set grid; to log the next set you scroll back down. The one component that must be visible while lifting is the one that is not.

**Fix.** Pin it: render `<RestTimer>` in `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx` as an absolutely-positioned overlay anchored above the tab bar (mirroring the FAB's `bottom: Math.max(insets.bottom, 8) + 74` geometry from `(tabs)/_layout.tsx:157`), or move it inside the session `GlassSurface` and make that surface sticky. Either way it must not depend on scroll position.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:770 renders <RestTimer triggerKey={restKey} onDismiss={...} /> as a plain sibling between the session GlassSurface (719-767) and the exercise cards (772-817), inside the Screen's ScrollView (src/components/Layout.tsx:99-109). src/components/RestTimer.tsx:76-115 is an ordinary <View> — no position:'absolute', no sticky/stickyHeaderIndices anywhere. The trigger is at workout.tsx:796-804, fired from a set-completion tick on any exercise, including ones several screens below. So the countdown starts off-screen precisely when it is running, and the +30/-30/dismiss controls (RestTimer.tsx:95-106) are unreachable without losing your place in the set grid.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard meal rows say 'Add food' but navigate to the top of the Diary screen instead of the food search</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429` wraps each meal row in `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` — no params. The visible CTA on an empty meal is the text 'Add food' (lines 477-479) and the spoken label is `'${meal}, nothing logged. Open diary to add food.'` (line 426). Both `meal` and `today` are in scope at that point, and `/food-search` already accepts them: `router.push({ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date } })` at `diary.tsx:563`.

**User impact.** The user taps 'Add food' next to Dinner and lands at the top of the Diary looking at the date navigator and the day-totals card, then has to scroll past Breakfast, Lunch and Dinner's entries to find that meal's own 'Add food' button. A labelled action performs a navigation, not the action.

**Fix.** Change the `Link` at `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429` to `router.push({ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date: today } })` when the row's CTA is 'Add food', and keep `/diary` only for rows that already have entries (where 'review' is the real intent). One line, removes two taps and a scroll from the most frequent flow in the app.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429 is `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` with no params, wrapping the whole row; the visible CTA on an empty meal is the text 'Add food' at 477-479. Both `meal` (from the MEALS.map at 420) and the day are in scope. The destination /diary has no meal or date param handling at all — diary.tsx:670-671 seeds its own state and never reads useLocalSearchParams — so the tap lands at the top of the Diary above DateNavigator (695) and the tall DayTotals card (696). Meanwhile diary.tsx:563 shows the exact call that would have worked: router.push({ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date } }). A control labelled with a verb performs a navigation instead, on the most frequent path in the app.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Finishing a workout deletes the session card and shows no summary of what was just done</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:766</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `<Button label="Finish workout" full haptic onPress={endWorkout} />` at `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:766`. `endWorkout` (`src/core/store/appState.ts:498-502`) sets `endedAt` and nulls `activeWorkoutId`. `activeSession` at line 1126 then becomes null, so the whole `<ActiveWorkout>` subtree unmounts and the screen re-renders as the 'Ready to train?' card (line 1173). The finished session's volume, set count, duration and any PRs are only visible by scrolling to the History list (line 1291) and expanding its row.

**User impact.** You finish a hard session, tap the big button, and the screen you were looking at vanishes and is replaced by an invitation to start another workout. No total volume, no duration, no 'you set 2 PRs' — the payoff for 45 minutes of logging is a blank slate. There is also no undo if Finish was tapped by accident.

**Fix.** In `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx`, hold the just-finished session id in local state after `endWorkout()` and render a summary card in place of `ActiveWorkout` — volume (`sessionVolume`), sets (`sessionSetCount`), duration (`formatDuration`) and the `prSetIds` set already computed at line 684 — with 'Save as template' and 'Done'. All four helpers are already imported in this file.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** workout.tsx:766 is `<Button label="Finish workout" full haptic onPress={endWorkout} />` with no confirmation step — contrast Cancel at 859-866, which deliberately asks twice. endWorkout (src/core/store/appState.ts:498-502) stamps endedAt and nulls activeWorkoutId, so activeSession (1126) becomes null, the whole ActiveWorkout subtree unmounts, and 1173 renders 'Ready to train?' in its place. I checked for a resume path: no action anywhere sets activeWorkoutId back to an existing session (deleteWorkout at 553-556 only clears it), so an accidental Finish is unrecoverable — you would have to start a new session and re-add every exercise. The finished session's volume, duration, set count and PRs are only reachable by scrolling to History (1291) and expanding a row. P1 stands, driven more by the irreversibility than by the missing summary.

</details>

<details><summary><b>There is no path to create a custom food, yet Profile has a section for managing them</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:690</code> · broken-flow · effort L</summary>

**Code.** A repo-wide grep shows `addCustomFood` is never called outside `src/core`. `app/food-search.tsx`'s no-results footer (lines 690-702) offers only advice — "Try a shorter word, a brand name, or the plain ingredient" — with no action, in direct contrast to `app/lift-picker.tsx:499` which offers a 'Create custom lift' Button in the same situation and again in its footer (line 509). `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:645-677` renders a 'Custom foods' Section whose empty copy reads "Foods you create while logging show up here." The chat screen builds `isCustom: true` foods (`app/chat.tsx:216-236`) but attaches them to the entry only — it never pushes to `customFoods`.

**User impact.** Search for a homemade meal or a local brand, get nothing, and the flow dead-ends: the user must either give up or ask the AI to invent macros. Meanwhile Profile shows a permanently empty 'Custom foods (0 saved)' section describing a feature that does not exist.

**Fix.** Add a 'Create this food' action to the `ListFooterComponent` empty state in `app/food-search.tsx:690` and to the results footer, opening an inline form that calls `addCustomFood` and then `openServingStep(food)` — mirroring `startCreating`/`handleCreate` in `app/lift-picker.tsx:193-212`. Until then, `profile.tsx:652` copy is a lie and should be corrected.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Repo-wide grep: `addCustomFood` appears only at src/core/store/appState.ts:64 (type) and :336 (implementation) — zero call sites in app/ or src/ outside core. food-search.tsx:690-702 is a ListFooterComponent EmptyState with title/message and no `action` prop, whose message is advice only ('Try a shorter word, a brand name, or the plain ingredient'), and the USDA-empty row at 515-521 is likewise inert. The contrast is in the sibling picker: lift-picker.tsx:495-502 gives the empty state a 'Create custom lift' Button and 506-518 repeats it in the footer, backed by startCreating/handleCreate at 193-212. profile.tsx:645-677 renders a 'Custom foods' Section whose subtitle is `${customFoods.length} saved` and whose empty copy at 651-653 promises 'Foods you create while logging show up here' — a list that can never be non-empty. chat.tsx:214-242 does build an isCustom food but passes it only into addFoodEntry; it never touches customFoods.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Android hardware back on the onboarding flow exits the app and discards every answer</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:297</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** Onboarding is entered with `router.replace('/onboarding')` (`app/_layout.tsx:54`) and `router.replace` again from `app/index.tsx:28`, so it is the only entry in the stack. `app/_layout.tsx:72` sets `gestureEnabled: false` for it, but nothing registers a `BackHandler`. All 15 answers live in local `useState` (`app/onboarding.tsx:181-193`), and the in-flow `back()` (line 297) is wired only to the on-screen 'Back' Button (line 692).

**User impact.** On step 4 of 5, having typed name, age, height, weight, activity and goal, an instinctive hardware-back press exits the app. Re-opening routes back to onboarding at step 0 with every field blank. The user has to redo the entire setup.

**Fix.** Add a `BackHandler.addEventListener('hardwareBackPress', …)` in `app/onboarding.tsx` that calls `back()` and returns `true` while `step > 0`, returning `false` only at step 0. Same treatment belongs on `app/food-search.tsx` (where hardware back at the serving step should call `setSelected(null)` rather than dismissing the modal, mirroring the on-screen ArrowLeft at line 446) and on `app/lift-picker.tsx` (where it should call `setCreating(false)` while the custom-lift form is open, mirroring the 'Back' Button at line 448).

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Grep for 'BackHandler' and 'hardwareBackPress' across the entire repo returns no matches. app/_layout.tsx:54 enters onboarding via router.replace and app/index.tsx:28 via <Redirect href="/onboarding" /> (which also replaces), so onboarding is the only entry in the root Stack; _layout.tsx:72 sets gestureEnabled:false, which stops the iOS swipe but has no bearing on the Android hardware button. All fifteen answers are local useState at onboarding.tsx:178-193, and back() (297-300) is wired only to the on-screen Button at 690-698. Hardware back therefore pops the last stack entry and exits the app, and re-launching remounts the screen at step 0 with every field blank. The extension to food-search (444-454, where back should call setSelected(null)) and lift-picker (446-452, where it should call setCreating(false)) is also correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Dashboard's 'Kcal burned' tile reads from a field the workout logger never writes</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:607</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<GlanceTile … value={formatNumber(caloriesBurned)} label="Kcal burned" />` at `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:607-612`, fed by `nutrition.caloriesBurned` (line 178). `getDayNutrition` derives that solely from `day.exercises`: `for (const ex of day.exercises) base.caloriesBurned += ex.caloriesBurned` (`src/core/utils/calculations.ts:94-95`). `day.exercises` is only ever written by `addExerciseEntry`, which a repo-wide grep shows is never called outside `src/core`. The workout logger writes to `workoutLog` instead (`app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1116`, `appState.ts:478-535`).

**User impact.** A user finishes a 90-minute session, opens the home screen to check the day, and the training tile reads 0. The one place the two halves of the product should meet reports that no training happened.

**Fix.** Either compute the tile from `workoutLog` filtered to today in `app/(tabs)/index.tsx` — session volume or set count are honest, derivable numbers — or drop the tile entirely rather than render a permanent zero (the design rules forbid a fabricated placeholder number). Wiring `workoutLog` sessions into `diary[date].exercises` would be an upstream web-repo change to `src/core/store/appState.ts`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:607-612 renders GlanceTile value={formatNumber(caloriesBurned)} label="Kcal burned", fed from nutrition.caloriesBurned at 178. getDayNutrition derives that field solely from day.exercises (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:94-96, `for (const ex of day.exercises) base.caloriesBurned += ex.caloriesBurned`). Repo-wide grep: addExerciseEntry — the only writer of diary[date].exercises (appState.ts:284-297) — has no call site outside src/core; the same is true of removeExerciseEntry. The workout logger writes to workoutLog instead (appState.ts:478-556). So the tile is a hardcoded 0 for every user forever, which is precisely the fabricated placeholder number the design rules forbid, sitting next to a real streak figure that lends it credibility.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Blurring the Name, Age or Height field on Profile silently overwrites every calorie and macro target</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:249</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** commitBasics (profile.tsx:238-250) ends with `recalculateGoals()`, and it is wired to onBlur on the Name Field (442), the Age Field (450) and the Height Field (459). recalculateGoals (src/core/store/appState.ts:226-239) does `Object.assign(state.goals, calculateMacroGoals(...))`, and calculateMacroGoals (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:36-45) returns calories, protein, carbs, fat, fiber, sugar, sodium and water. So merely tapping into the Name field and tapping out replaces the plan the user accepted from the coach at goals.tsx:597-604 — including the sodium and water goals that acceptRecommendation (appState.ts:451-470) deliberately leaves alone.

**User impact.** A user opens Profile to fix the spelling of their name, taps away, and their accepted coach plan is silently replaced by the generic BMR/TDEE formula output. Nothing on Profile or Goals says a word, and there is no undo — the previous targets are gone. The auditor's targets-split finding named only the Goal and Activity-level Choice controls and missed the three text fields, which are the ones a user is most likely to touch casually.

**Fix.** Remove `recalculateGoals()` from commitBasics in app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:249 — name, age and height edits should update the profile only. Where recalculation genuinely is warranted (goal or activity change, 477/490), gate it: compare state.goals against the current formula output first and, if the user has diverged from it, ask before overwriting.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P2 (15)

<details><summary><b>The assistant has five entry points on three screens; food search has one</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:134</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Entry points to `/chat`: the global FAB (`app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:151`), the Dashboard header IconButton (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:126`), a full-width Dashboard Surface card titled 'AI Nutrition Assistant' with an 'Ask AI' Button (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:134-151`), the Diary header IconButton (`app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:690`), the Workout header IconButton (`app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1145`). Three of those are simultaneously visible on the Dashboard. Progress and Profile have no header entry, so the pattern is not even consistent.

**User impact.** On the home screen the user sees three ways to talk to the AI and zero ways to log a food. The 'AI Nutrition Assistant' card is rendered second, immediately after `HeroCard` and before `MacroCard` (line 153), so an ad for an occasional feature outranks the macro breakdown the screen exists to show.

**Fix.** Delete the 'AI Nutrition Assistant' Surface card at app/(tabs)/index.tsx:134-151 — it is a duplicate of the header IconButton two lines above it. Keep the global FAB as the single always-available assistant entry and drop the per-screen header IconButtons at index.tsx:123-130, diary.tsx:690-692 and workout.tsx:1145-1147, so the freed header `right` slot can carry a screen-specific action instead.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Every cited entry point is real and I confirmed all five: FAB (_layout.tsx:151), Dashboard header IconButton (index.tsx:123-130), Dashboard 'AI Nutrition Assistant' Surface card with 'Ask AI' Button (index.tsx:134-151), Diary header (diary.tsx:690-692), Workout header (workout.tsx:1145-1147). Progress (progress.tsx:1240) and Profile (profile.tsx:307) pass no `right` prop, so the pattern really is inconsistent, and index.tsx:132/134/153 confirms the AI card renders between HeroCard and MacroCard. But severity is inflated: the hero card that answers 'am I on track' is still first and every core task is completable. This is duplication and misprioritised real estate, i.e. noticeable friction, not a materially broken task. Also, the fix's cross-reference to a `Screen`-rendered persistent header slot conflicts with keeping the FAB; pick one owner explicitly.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The weigh-in form is duplicated on Dashboard and Profile, and Progress points at the slower of the two</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:204</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `WeightTargetCard` renders a "Today's weight" `Field` + 'Log' Button at `src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:204-227`. It is mounted twice: `<WeightTargetCard compact />` on the Dashboard (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:173`) and `<WeightTargetCard />` on Profile (`app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:358`). Profile then has a separate 'Weight log' Section (lines 535-567) that can only *delete* entries and tells the user "Add one from the card above". Progress's weight tab charts the same data and its empty state says "Log your weight from the Profile tab" (`app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:987`) — ignoring the Dashboard card entirely. Progress *also* writes weight entries via its Google Fit importer (line 676).

**User impact.** Four screens touch one number. A user told by the Progress chart to 'log your weight from the Profile tab' scrolls a nine-section accordion looking for a weigh-in field that was on the home screen the whole time. Nobody can form a mental model of where their weight lives.

**Fix.** Keep <WeightTargetCard compact /> on the Dashboard (index.tsx:173) as the daily capture point, drop the duplicate <WeightTargetCard /> at profile.tsx:358 so Profile keeps only the 'Weight log' history Section, and update progress.tsx:987 to point at the Dashboard. Note that dropping the Profile card also requires rewording profile.tsx:542 ('Add one from the card above'), which would otherwise reference a card that no longer exists.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The duplication is real: src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:203-228 renders the "Today's weight" Field + 'Log' Button, and the component is mounted twice — <WeightTargetCard compact /> at index.tsx:173 and <WeightTargetCard /> at profile.tsx:358. profile.tsx:535-567 is a delete-only history list, and progress.tsx:676-680 writes weight entries from the importer. But the headline claim is wrong: profile.tsx:542's copy 'Add one from the card above' is literally correct — the card above it on Profile IS the WeightTargetCard at 358. And progress.tsx:987's 'Log your weight from the Profile tab' is also a valid instruction, since Profile does have the form; it is merely not the nearest one. So there is no 'wrong instruction', only redundancy and a suboptimal pointer. Nothing here breaks or misplaces a core task — the user can log a weigh-in from the very first screen they see.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Personal records and the full session archive keep rendering below the live workout instead of being hidden during a session</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1291</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1150` renders `{activeSession && <ActiveWorkout …/>}`, then lines 1227-1289 render 'Personal records' and lines 1291-1298 render `history.map(session => <HistoryCard …/>)` for every past session. Neither block is gated on `activeSession === null` (unlike the 'Ready to train?' card at 1173 and the templates rail at 1190, which are). Each `HistoryCard` is expandable and carries 'Save as template' and 'Delete' actions (lines 1082-1100).

**User impact.** Mid-workout, scrolling down past your exercise cards to reach 'Add exercise' and 'Cancel workout' dumps you into an all-time records table and every workout you have ever done, complete with destructive Delete buttons — while your hands are chalky and you are between sets.

**Fix.** Wrap the PR block (workout.tsx:1227) and the History block (1291) in `activeSession === null && …`, matching the existing pattern at 1173/1190, so the tab is a live logger during a session. Separately, cap or virtualise the history list — history.map at 1294 renders every session ever inside a plain ScrollView.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code claim is right: workout.tsx:1227 gates the Personal-records block only on records.length > 0 and 1291 gates History only on history.length > 0, neither on activeSession === null — unlike 1173 and 1190 which do gate. history (1127-1130) is every session except the active one, unbounded, and each HistoryCard exposes 'Save as template' and 'Delete' (1082-1100). But the stated impact is backwards: inside ActiveWorkout, 'Add exercise' (819-827) and 'Cancel workout' (859-866) render BEFORE the PR and history blocks, so reaching them does not require scrolling through the archive — the archive sits below them. And Delete is double-gated (expand the card at 913-918, then setConfirmDelete at 1097, then 'Delete for good' at 1072-1078). What remains is an unbounded archive rendered under a live session, which is layout clutter and a rendering cost, not a broken task.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Personal records are rendered by both the Workout tab and the Progress tab with different windows and different formatting</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1184</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1131` computes `getPersonalRecords(workoutLog)` and shows the top 5 with best-set weight, e1RM and achieved date, expandable to all (`Show all ${records.length} records`, line 1281). `app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:770-776` computes the same `getPersonalRecords(workoutLog)`, re-sorts by `achievedOn` and slices to 4, showing only e1RM and a date. Same source, two truncations, two orderings, two layouts.

**User impact.** A user checking 'what is my bench PR' sees different lists depending on which tab they open, and neither says it is a partial view of the other. Two screens claim the same job.

**Fix.** Keep the full records table on Progress > Training (it is the review surface) and reduce the Workout tab to nothing, per the `workout-tab-mixes-live-and-archive` fix. If a records glance is wanted while training, show only records set *in this session* — `prSetIds` at `workout.tsx:684-700` already computes exactly that.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** workout.tsx:1131-1132 computes getPersonalRecords(workoutLog) in source order, slices to PR_PREVIEW_COUNT=5 (1112), renders liftName + best-set weight + achieved date + est. 1RM (1246-1274), and offers 'Show all {n} records' (1278-1287). progress.tsx:770-776 computes the same getPersonalRecords(workoutLog), re-sorts by achievedOn descending and slices to 4, rendering only liftName + date + e1RM (1194-1221). Same source, two truncations, two orderings, two layouts, and neither says it is a partial view. P2 is right — the numbers are not wrong, the ownership is.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Diary owns its own date in local state, so arriving from the Dashboard can land on a day the user browsed to last week</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:671</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:670-671` holds `const today = getTodayString()` and `const [date, setDate] = useState(today)`. Tab screens stay mounted, so `date` survives tab switches indefinitely. The Dashboard is hard-wired to today (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:91`) and its meal rows link to `/diary` with no `date` param (line 429). `MealCard` then passes that stale `date` straight into food-search: `params: { meal, date }` (diary.tsx:563), and `food-search.tsx:247` trusts it (`const date = isISODate(params.date) ? params.date : getTodayString()`).

**User impact.** User browses back to last Tuesday in the Diary, goes to the Dashboard, sees 'Breakfast — nothing logged', taps it, adds oatmeal — and it is written to last Tuesday. The Dashboard still says nothing is logged for breakfast today, and the user has no idea why.

**Fix.** In app/(tabs)/diary.tsx, re-sync `date` to `today` when `today` changes (an effect on `today`, so an app left open past midnight does not keep pointing at yesterday), and seed it from useLocalSearchParams so app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429 can pass `{ pathname: '/diary', params: { date: today } }` when it hands off from the Dashboard.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The mechanism is exactly as described: diary.tsx:670-671 holds `const today = getTodayString()` recomputed each render but `const [date, setDate] = useState(today)` seeded once; expo-router Tabs keeps screens mounted after first visit, so `date` survives tab switches; index.tsx:429 passes no date; MealCard forwards that `date` into food-search (563) and food-search.tsx:247 trusts it. So a stale date really can be carried into a log. But the impact is overstated on two counts I verified: the Diary shows the selected date prominently and offers a 'Jump to today' Button whenever date !== today (diary.tsx:145-162), and the food-search header subtitle prints `${meal} · ${formatDate(date)}` (food-search.tsx:422) before the user commits. Two visible date indicators sit between the stale state and the write, so this is friction and a mild trap, not a silent misfile.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Dashboard has no training presence — no live-session card, no start action, no link to Workout</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:119</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `DashboardScreen` (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:119-181`) renders, in order: HeroCard (calories), the AI assistant card, MacroCard, CoachCard, MealsCard, WaterCard, StepsCard, WeightTargetCard, GlanceRow. `activeWorkoutId` is never read on this screen. A workout left open keeps ticking — `ElapsedTimer` counts from `session.startedAt` (`workout.tsx:621-630`) and `startWorkout` silently closes a stale session only when the *next* one starts (`appState.ts:488-489`).

**User impact.** Half the product is invisible from the screen the user opens first. Worse, a session started and forgotten produces a nine-hour 'duration' in the history with nothing anywhere warning that a session is live — the Workout tab icon is unchanged and the Dashboard says nothing.

**Fix.** Add a conditional card to `app/(tabs)/index.tsx` between the hero and the macro ring: when `activeWorkoutId !== null`, show a 'Session in progress' row (elapsed + set count) linking to `/workout`; otherwise show today's completed session summary, or a 'Start workout' row when nothing is logged. Also add a badge to the Workout tab in `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` when `activeWorkoutId` is set.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** DashboardScreen (index.tsx:119-181) renders exactly the listed sequence and never selects activeWorkoutId — I checked every useStore call on the screen (92-95): diary[today], goals, streak, recommendation, and that is all. Nothing links to /workout either. The stale-session half is also right: ElapsedTimer (workout.tsx:621-630) counts from session.startedAt with no upper bound, and startWorkout only closes a forgotten session lazily, when the next one begins (appState.ts:486-494, `if (previous && previous.endedAt === undefined) previous.endedAt = Date.now()`), so a session left open overnight is recorded with a nine-hour duration and nothing anywhere flags that it is live. P2 is fair — the tab bar still reaches Workout in one tap.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard and Diary both claim 'today's nutrition', with roughly 70% overlapping content</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:132</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** Dashboard renders HeroCard (eaten vs goal calories, line 132), MacroCard (protein/carbs/fat vs goal rings, 153) and MealsCard (per-meal kcal + item count, 163). Diary renders DayTotals with the identical calories-vs-goal bar and remaining-kcal sentence (`app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:199-224`), the identical macro progress rows (227-251) and six per-meal cards with the same per-meal kcal totals (698-700). The only Dashboard content not on Diary is water, steps, weight and streak.

**User impact.** Two of five tab slots show the same day's food from two angles, while training gets one slot that mixes live logging with an archive, and the coach gets no slot at all. The user's complaint that 'features are not in the right places' is largely this: the tab bar's frequency ranking does not match the app's.

**Fix.** Keep the Dashboard as the cross-domain 'am I on track today' surface and let the Diary own per-meal detail. Keep HeroCard and MacroCard (they are the only rings in the app), and change MealsCard rows from `<Link href="/diary">` (index.tsx:429) into direct `/food-search` pushes with { meal, date: today }, so the card becomes an action list rather than a second read-only copy of the Diary. Add the missing training presence (live-session row / start action) in the space that frees up.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The overlap is real and I verified each pair: index.tsx:132 HeroCard (eaten vs goal + remaining sentence) against diary.tsx:199-224 DayTotals (identical calories-vs-goal bar and 'X kcal left today'); index.tsx:153 MacroCard rings against diary.tsx:227-251 MacroStat rows; index.tsx:163 MealsCard per-meal kcal against the six MealCard headers at diary.tsx:459-474. Two of five tab slots do answer the same question. But the recommended fix is wrong: deleting MacroCard (153-159) would leave the Dashboard with no macro breakdown at all, since HeroCard is calories-only, and it would remove the app's only MacroRing — the design doc treats the ring as a first-class component and the Diary's flat MacroStat rows are a different, weaker read. The right differentiation is at the meal-row level, not the macro level.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Saved meals are managed in two places with two different capability sets</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:679</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:571-662` renders 'Saved meals' — a horizontal rail where each template can be applied to the current day (`applyMealTemplate`, line 624) or deleted (line 651). `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:679-712` renders a 'Meal templates' Section listing the same `mealTemplates` with delete only, and copy pointing back to the Diary. Templates are *created* from a bookmark IconButton on each Diary meal card (`diary.tsx:477-490`).

**User impact.** Three surfaces for one small feature, none of which is complete: you create in one place, apply in a second, and can delete in either. A user who deletes from Profile has no idea it is the same list as the Diary rail.

**Fix.** Remove the 'Meal templates' Section from `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:679-712` (and 'Custom foods' at 645-677 once creation exists) and keep management where it is used — the Diary rail. Profile should hold identity, units, integrations and account, not content libraries.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:571-662 SavedMeals is a horizontal rail: each template is applied via applyMealTemplate (624) and deleted via deleteMealTemplate (651). profile.tsx:679-712 lists the same mealTemplates array with a delete IconButton only (703-708) and copy at 685-688 pointing back at the Diary. Creation is a third place — the BookmarkPlus IconButton on each Diary meal card (diary.tsx:476-490) calling saveMealTemplate (451). Three surfaces, none complete, and deleting from Profile silently removes the item from the Diary rail with nothing saying they are the same list. P2 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Screen shell has no leading header slot, so every pushed screen puts its back control on the right</b> — <code>src/components/Layout.tsx:22</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `ScreenProps` (`src/components/Layout.tsx:22-30`) exposes `title`, `subtitle`, `right`, `children`, `scroll`, `contentStyle` — there is no `left` or `onBack`. Consequently `app/goals.tsx:428-432` renders a left-pointing `ArrowLeft` inside `right={<IconButton accessibilityLabel="Go back" …>}` at the far right of the header, and `app/chat.tsx:316` puts its close `X` on the right. The two screens that do *not* use `Screen` — `app/food-search.tsx:444` and `app/lift-picker.tsx:237` — both correctly place their dismiss control on the left.

**User impact.** A back arrow on the right edge is where users expect a confirm or an overflow menu, and it is on the wrong side of the screen for a right-handed thumb reaching to go back. Within one app, dismissal lives on the left in two screens and on the right in two others.

**Fix.** Add an optional `left?: React.ReactNode` (or `onBack?: () => void` that renders a standard ChevronLeft) to `ScreenProps` in `src/components/Layout.tsx` and render it before the title block at line 86. Move `app/goals.tsx`'s back button into it; keep `chat.tsx`'s `X` on the right since it is a modal close, which is a different affordance.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** src/components/Layout.tsx:22-30 ScreenProps declares title, subtitle, right, children, scroll, contentStyle — there is no left/onBack, and the header row at 76-95 renders {title block}{right} with nothing before the title. The consequences are as cited: goals.tsx:428-432 puts a left-pointing ArrowLeft inside `right=`, and chat.tsx:315-319 puts its X there. The screens that bypass Screen — food-search.tsx:443-454 and lift-picker.tsx:236-254 — both place dismissal on the left. The sharpest part is one the finding understates: on the tab screens that same right slot is the 'Open the nutrition assistant' Sparkles button (index.tsx:123-130, diary.tsx:690, workout.tsx:1145), so the identical position means 'open chat' on three screens and 'go back' on Goals. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The floating assistant button covers the bottom-right of the last content row on every scrollable screen</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:157</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The FAB occupies from `Math.max(insets.bottom, 8) + 74` to `+130` above the screen bottom (`app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:157-161`, `width/height: 56`). `Screen`'s scroll content stops at `TAB_BAR_SPACE + insets.bottom + spacing.xl` = `76 + insets.bottom + 32` = 108 + inset (`src/components/Layout.tsx:19, 103`). The final ~22pt of the last element therefore sits under the FAB — half the height of a 44pt control.

**User impact.** On the Workout tab the last element is the 'Cancel workout' Button (`workout.tsx:860-865`); on Profile it is the 'Sign out' Button (`profile.tsx:756-761`). Their right edge is under an opaque jade circle, so a tap there opens the chat instead.

**Fix.** Raise `Screen`'s bottom padding in `src/components/Layout.tsx:103` to clear the FAB (`TAB_BAR_SPACE + 62 + insets.bottom + spacing.xl`), or export a `FAB_SPACE` token alongside `TAB_BAR_SPACE` so the two stay in sync.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** The conclusion holds; the arithmetic is slightly off in the safe direction. With spacing.xl = 24 and TAB_BAR_SPACE = 76 (tokens.ts:113, Layout.tsx:20), Screen's contentContainer paddingBottom (Layout.tsx:103) is 100 + insets.bottom, not 108. The FAB spans Math.max(insets.bottom,8)+74 to +130 (_layout.tsx:157-160), so the last 30pt of trailing content sits under it, not 22pt. Horizontally the FAB covers x=18..74 from the right edge while Screen content runs to x=16, so a full-width control's right end is underneath. Both examples survive: workout.tsx:859-866 'Cancel workout' is a bare child of the content stack, so ~30 of its 44pt are covered; profile.tsx:756-761 'Sign out' sits inside a Surface with padding spacing.lg=16, so ~14pt of it are covered. A tap in that corner hits the FAB and opens chat instead. P2 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Onboarding's closing screen tells the user every number is editable under Profile, which is false</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:670</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The final step renders the reassurance `{ Icon: Flame, text: 'Nothing is locked in — every number is editable under Profile.' }` at onboarding.tsx:670, immediately under the calorie and macro figures it just computed (finish() writes them via updateGoals at 281). But Profile's 'Targets' Section (app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:411-435) contains only a goal-weight Field; calories, protein, carbs, fat and fiber are editable solely on app/goals.tsx:708-847, and a repo-wide grep shows Profile contains no link to /goals — the only entry is app/(tabs)/index.tsx:350.

**User impact.** The very last thing the app tells a new user before handing them the tabs is a wrong instruction. Someone who later wants to change the calorie target from onboarding goes to Profile, expands 'Targets', finds one goal-weight box, and concludes the number is not editable. It is the first-run flow actively teaching the wrong mental model.

**Fix.** Either add a navigation row to /goals in profile.tsx's 'Targets' Section and keep the copy, or change onboarding.tsx:670 to name the real location ('editable any time from the coach card on your dashboard'). The first is better — the copy is a symptom of the missing entry point.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Dashboard Meals card hides Pre-Workout and Post-Workout, so its rows do not add up to the hero figure</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:55</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** index.tsx:55 declares `const MEALS: readonly MealType[] = ['Breakfast','Lunch','Dinner','Snacks']` and MealsCard maps only that list (420). But mealTotals (108-117) is built from every entry in day.entries regardless of mealType, and HeroCard (132) and MacroCard (153) both read nutrition from getDayNutrition(day), which sums all entries. app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:33-40 and app/food-search.tsx:28-35 both offer six meal types, and food-search.tsx:614-623 lets the user pick Pre-Workout or Post-Workout at the moment of logging.

**User impact.** Log a pre-workout shake, open the Dashboard, and the hero says 300 kcal eaten while the four meal rows all read 'Nothing logged'. The numbers visibly disagree with each other and there is no row to tap to find or fix the entry — the user has to guess that the Diary holds two meals the home screen does not admit exist.

**Fix.** In app/(tabs)/index.tsx, render a row for any meal type present in mealTotals: iterate the six MealType values and include the two workout meals conditionally on `mealTotals[meal] !== undefined`, keeping the fixed four always visible. That keeps the card short on a normal day and honest on a training day.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>'Copy yesterday' exists in the store but has no UI anywhere, so repeating a day means re-logging every item</b> — <code>src/core/store/appState.ts:270</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** copyDayEntries (appState.ts:270-282) and copyMealEntries (406-416) are fully implemented and exposed on the store interface (lines 53 and 74). A repo-wide grep outside src/core returns zero call sites for either. The only re-logging affordance shipped is the meal-template rail (diary.tsx:571-662), which requires the user to have thought ahead and bookmarked the meal (diary.tsx:476-490) before it can help them.

**User impact.** People who eat the same breakfast every day — the majority of the target user base — get no shortcut unless they discovered the bookmark icon on a previous day. The one-tap repeat is written, tested by the web app, and sitting unreachable in the shared store, while the user retypes the same four foods through search every morning.

**Fix.** Add a 'Copy yesterday' Button to app/(tabs)/diary.tsx — either beside 'Jump to today' in DateNavigator (161) calling copyDayEntries(shiftISODate(date,-1), date), or per-card next to 'Add food' (559) calling copyMealEntries. Both actions already exist; this is wiring only.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>The tab bar and the FAB fire haptics on plain navigation, which the design rules forbid</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:88</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** GlassTabBar's onPress calls `void Haptics.selectionAsync()` before navigation.navigate(route.name) at _layout.tsx:87-90, so every tab switch buzzes. AssistantButton does the same at 149-152: `void Haptics.impactAsync(Haptics.ImpactFeedbackStyle.Light); router.push('/chat')`. Neither is a commit — nothing is written to the store in either handler. Contrast the places that follow the rule: workout.tsx:234 (set completed), diary.tsx:452 (meal template saved), food-search.tsx:414 (food logged).

**User impact.** Haptics stop carrying meaning. When moving between tabs feels the same as completing a set, the buzz that is supposed to confirm 'that set is logged' becomes background noise, and a user who finds the constant feedback annoying can only fix it by disabling system haptics entirely, which also kills the confirmations that matter.

**Fix.** Delete the Haptics.selectionAsync() call at app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:88 and the Haptics.impactAsync() call at :150. Opening chat and switching tabs are navigations, not commits.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>The daily step goal can only be set if Health Connect is installed AND permission has been granted</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:388</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The 'Daily step goal' Field (profile.tsx:388-397, committing profile.stepGoal via commitStepGoal at 264-272) is nested inside the `health.granted ? (…)` branch at 367, which itself is inside `health.availability === 'available' && (…)` at 360. It is the only writer of profile.stepGoal in the app. src/components/StepsCard.tsx:30 falls back to DEFAULT_STEP_GOAL = 8000 when it is unset, and StepsCard returns null entirely when availability !== 'available' (line 28).

**User impact.** A numeric target lives inside a device-integration section instead of the 'Targets' section three cards above it. On iOS, or on any Android device where the user declines the permission, the step goal simply cannot be set — and the user has no way to know the field exists, because the section that contains it never renders. Anyone who connects, sets 12000, then revokes permission loses access to their own setting.

**Fix.** Move the 'Daily step goal' Field out of the Health Connect Section and into the 'Targets' Section (app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:411-435) alongside goal weight, so every numeric target the user owns lives in one place regardless of platform.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (4)

<details><summary><b>A newly added exercise arrives with no set row, costing one extra tap before the first number can be typed</b> — <code>app/lift-picker.tsx:185</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `handleSelect` (`app/lift-picker.tsx:185-191`) calls `addExerciseToWorkout(sessionId, lift)` then `router.back()`. That action pushes `{ id, liftId, lift, sets: [] }` (`src/core/store/appState.ts:521`). `ExerciseCard` therefore renders with an empty `sets` array, no column header (gated on `sets.length > 0`, `workout.tsx:559`) and only an 'Add set' Button (601-607). The progressive-overload placeholder computed at `workout.tsx:468-476` has nowhere to render until a set exists.

**User impact.** Every exercise costs an extra tap before the first number can be entered, and the 'Target from last time' hint the screen just calculated is invisible until you create the row. Across a five-exercise session that is five wasted taps mid-workout.

**Fix.** Optional polish: in app/lift-picker.tsx:186, follow addExerciseToWorkout with addSet(sessionId, exerciseId, { weightKg: 0, reps: 0, isWarmup: false, completed: false }). Safe because the completion checkbox is disabled until reps > 0 (workout.tsx:246, 330), so a seeded row cannot be logged as work that did not happen.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Half of this is wrong. The code claim holds — lift-picker.tsx:185-191 calls addExerciseToWorkout then router.back(), and appState.ts:518-522 pushes `{ id, liftId, lift, sets: [] }`, so the card renders with no rows, no column header (gated at workout.tsx:559) and only 'Add set' (601-607). But the stated impact is false: the 'Target from last time' hint is rendered at workout.tsx:539-557 unconditionally inside ExerciseCard, NOT gated on sets.length, so it is fully visible the moment the exercise is added. What is deferred is only the per-row placeholder text from placeholderFor (468-476), which duplicates that same suggestion. So the real cost is one extra tap per exercise, with no lost information — a nit, and arguably a deliberate choice, since an auto-created 0/0 row would render a set that was never performed.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Pre-Workout and Post-Workout render full empty cards at the bottom of every Diary day</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:698</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `MEAL_TYPES` has six entries including 'Pre-Workout' and 'Post-Workout' (`app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:33-40`) and line 698 maps all six unconditionally. Every empty card still renders a `SectionTitle`, the line "Nothing logged for pre-workout yet." (line 494) and a full-width 'Add food' Button (559-564), below a `DateNavigator` (695) and a tall `DayTotals` card (696).

**User impact.** To log dinner the user scrolls past the date navigator, the totals card, and the Breakfast, Lunch and (usually empty) Pre-Workout cards. Two meal types most users never use occupy the same vertical space as the ones they use daily.

**Fix.** In app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:698, render Pre-Workout and Post-Workout only when byMeal.get(meal) is non-empty, with a single 'More meals' disclosure to reveal them otherwise. The separate suggestion to move each card's 'Add food' Button into the header row next to the kcal total (459-491) is a worthwhile independent change for meals with many entries.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code is as described — diary.tsx:33-40 defines six MEAL_TYPES and 698-700 maps all six unconditionally, with each empty card still rendering a SectionTitle (461), 'Nothing logged for X yet.' (494-496) and a full-width 'Add food' Button (559-564). But the impact statement is factually wrong about the layout: the order is Breakfast, Lunch, Dinner, Snacks, Pre-Workout, Post-Workout, so Dinner is the THIRD card and the two rarely-used meals already sit last, below everything a typical user needs. Nothing about reaching dinner requires scrolling past Pre-Workout. What is left is two empty cards padding the bottom of the scroll before SavedMeals (702) — real, minor, and partly by design.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Progress tab's metric and range selection resets to Calories / 7d every time the tab is left</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:621</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const [tab, setTab] = useState<TabKey>('calories')` and `const [range, setRange] = useState<RangeKey>('7d')` at `app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:621-622`, driving the `Segmented` controls at lines 1242 and 1256. Neither is persisted or reflected in the route.

**User impact.** A user who mainly tracks weight or training volume must re-select their sub-tab on every visit, and can never link or return directly to it — the training breakdown is permanently two taps deep inside a tab.

**Fix.** Persist `tab` and `range` to the store (or to route params via `useLocalSearchParams`) in `app/(tabs)/progress.tsx` so the Progress tab reopens where the user left it, and so the Workout tab can deep-link to `/progress?tab=training`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:621-622 are plain useState hooks (`tab` defaulting to 'calories', `range` to '7d') driving the Segmented controls at 1242 and 1256. Neither is written to the store nor read from useLocalSearchParams — the screen imports no routing hooks at all. Tab screens unmount on app restart, so the selection resets, and there is no way to deep-link /progress at the training or weight view. P3 is the right severity.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Progress photos and fasting sessions are synced from the account but have no mobile screen at all</b> — <code>src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:43</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** 'fastingSession' and 'progressPhotos' are both in SYNC_FIELDS (AuthProvider.tsx:43) and in the matching syncFields list inside hydrateStore (src/core/store/appState.ts:609), so they are downloaded and re-uploaded on every save. A repo-wide grep outside src/core finds no other reference to either key, and no call site for addProgressPhoto/removeProgressPhoto (appState.ts:425-431) or startFasting/stopFasting (433-436).

**User impact.** A user who took progress photos or ran a fast on the web opens the phone app and that data is invisible — not empty-stated, not mentioned, just absent — while the app keeps round-tripping it to the server on every edit. On a shared-account product this reads as 'my photos are gone'.

**Fix.** Either surface them (a read-only 'Progress photos' Section in app/(tabs)/profile.tsx next to 'Body measurements' at 569-643 would cover the common case) or state plainly in the Profile sync row (739-762) that photos and fasting are web-only for now. Silently syncing data the app never shows is the worst of the three options.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

### Dashboard

#### P1 (7)

<details><summary><b>Weight log persists in the display unit, so switching lbs/kg silently reinterprets every past weigh-in</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:83</code> · data-correctness · effort L</summary>

**Code.** submitWeighIn calls `addWeightEntry({ date: today, weight: Math.round(value * 10) / 10 })` with the comment "Stored in the user's display unit, exactly like the rest of the weight log." Downstream, src/core/utils/weightTarget.ts:60 does `toKg = entry.weight / LBS_PER_KG` using the CURRENT `profile.weightUnit` for every historical entry, and app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:518 lets the user flip that unit with `updateProfile({ weightUnit: v })` and no migration. The binding rule is "Weights persist in kg always; convert only at input/display boundary."

**User impact.** A user who logged 180 lbs for three months and then switches the profile to kg opens the dashboard and the Weight goal card reads 180 kg with a start weight of 182 kg; the % complete, the kg/week trend, the ETA countdown and the status sentence ("On track" / "Going the wrong way") all become nonsense until they switch back. currentWeightKg also stays on the old value until the next weigh-in, so TDEE and the coach's calorie target are computed from a body that does not exist.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change (src/core is generated): add `weightKg` to WeightEntry and have appState.addWeightEntry convert at write time, with a migration that reads the unit the entry was recorded under. In the meantime, WeightTarget.tsx should submit kg (`unit === 'lbs' ? value / LBS_PER_KG : value`) and profile.tsx must convert the stored log when the unit toggle changes.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** WeightTarget.tsx:79-85 submits `addWeightEntry({ date: today, weight: Math.round(value*10)/10 })` with the raw field value and the comment "Stored in the user's display unit". appState.ts:319-330 stores it verbatim and only converts for `currentWeightKg` using the CURRENT `state.profile.weightUnit`. Every reader re-interprets history against the current unit: weightTarget.ts:60-61 `toKg = profile.weightUnit === 'lbs' ? entry.weight / LBS_PER_KG : entry.weight`, tdee.ts:52 does the same, coachAlerts.ts:103 the same, and profile.tsx:552 renders `${entry.weight} ${unit}`. profile.tsx:508-519 flips the unit with `updateProfile({ weightUnit: v })` and no migration — the inline comment there ("Only the display unit changes; stored values stay metric") is factually wrong about the weight log. docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md §6 states "Weights persist in kg always". healthConnect.ts:182-188 even converts kg→display unit on import to match this broken convention. Impact as described: trend, fraction, ETA and status all reinterpret, and currentWeightKg (feeding BMR/TDEE at calculations.ts:3-10) stays on the old value until the next weigh-in.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"Kcal burned" tile on the dashboard is permanently 0 — nothing in the app ever writes an exercise entry</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:609</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** GlanceRow renders `value={formatNumber(caloriesBurned)}` from `nutrition.caloriesBurned`, which getDayNutrition (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:94) sums from `day.exercises`. `addExerciseEntry` is defined in src/core/store/appState.ts:284 and is called from NO screen or component in the repo (grep over app/ and src/ returns only the definition). Strength sessions go to `workoutLog`, never to `diary[date].exercises`.

**User impact.** A user finishes a full workout in the Workout tab, returns to Home to see the payoff, and the dashboard states they burned 0 kcal today. It reads as the app losing the session, and it is the only training signal on the home screen.

**Fix.** Either derive the tile from the workout log (session volume/duration via src/core/utils/workoutMath.ts) and rename it accordingly, or delete the tile until an exercise-logging path exists. Do not render a number whose source is never written.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:607-612 renders the tile from `nutrition.caloriesBurned`; calculations.ts:94-96 sums it from `day.exercises` only. A repo-wide grep for `addExerciseEntry` outside src/core returns zero call sites — it exists only as the definition at appState.ts:284-297 and the interface line 55. Workout data goes to `workoutLog` (appState.ts:478-501), never into `diary[date].exercises`. So the tile is a permanent 0 for any mobile-only user, next to a Dumbbell icon, directly violating "Never render a fabricated number as a placeholder" (docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md §6). Only caveat: a diary blob synced from the web app via hydrateStore could carry exercises, so it is not literally unwritable — but nothing in this app writes it.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Hero flags hitting your calorie goal as critical, and shows a green check for a bulking user who has barely eaten</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:204</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `const tone: Tone = progress >= 1 ? 'critical' : progress >= 0.9 ? 'warning' : 'good'` and line 207 `const StatusIcon = tone === 'good' ? Check : AlertTriangle`. `profile.goal` ('lose' | 'maintain' | 'gain') and `recommendation.phase` (cut / lean_bulk / maintain / recomp) are both in the store and neither is consulted. At exactly goal, `over` is false so the sentence reads "0 kcal left today" while the ring track and icon go red/critical.

**User impact.** Someone who lands exactly on 2,150 of 2,150 kcal — the best possible day — gets a red AlertTriangle. Someone at 1,950 of 2,150 gets an amber warning triangle at dinner time for being on plan. A lean-bulk user at 400 of 3,200 kcal gets a reassuring green check for the failure case, and the red triangle only when they finally hit target. The one status the screen exists to give is inverted for half the user base.

**Fix.** Make tone goal-aware in HeroCard: for lose/cut, good below ~0.95, warning 0.95–1.05, critical only meaningfully over; for gain/lean_bulk, invert (under-target is the warning). Treat progress === 1 as 'good' in every phase, and keep the icon+words pairing.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:204 `const tone: Tone = progress >= 1 ? 'critical' : progress >= 0.9 ? 'warning' : 'good'` and 207 `const StatusIcon = tone === 'good' ? Check : AlertTriangle`.
