At exactly goal, line 201-202 give `remaining = 0`, `over = false`, so the sentence is "0 kcal left today" while the icon is a red AlertTriangle and the bar takes theme.status.critical. HeroCard's props (index.tsx:193-197) are only theme/nutrition/goalCalories — `profile.goal` and `recommendation.phase` are in the store (appState.ts:141, 102) and never consulted, so a gain/lean_bulk user gets a green Check for being 2,800 kcal under. Confirmed at P1: this is the one status the screen exists to give and it is inverted at the boundary and for gaining users.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Today's date is captured at render with no app-resume listener, so water logged after midnight lands on yesterday</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:91</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const today = getTodayString()` at line 91 and `WEEKDAYS[new Date().getDay()]` at line 122 are plain render-time reads. WaterCard receives `date={today}` (line 165) and its quick-add calls `addWater(date, amount)` (line 566). Nothing in the tree subscribes to RN AppState 'active' to force a re-render — src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:147 registers an AppState listener but only calls flushSave, and its children element identity never changes, so DashboardScreen does not re-render on resume.

**User impact.** The phone sits in a pocket overnight with the app alive. At 07:30 the user opens Home, sees yesterday's header date and yesterday's totals presented as today, and taps +250 ml — which writes into yesterday's diary day. Their real day starts at zero water and yesterday's record is wrong.

**Fix.** Add an AppState 'change' listener in DashboardScreen (or a shared useToday() hook) that re-renders when the app becomes active and when the local date string changes; store the date in state rather than deriving it inline.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:91 `const today = getTodayString()` and 122 `WEEKDAYS[new Date().getDay()]` are render-time reads; `today` is passed to WaterCard at line 165 and captured in the quick-add closure at line 562 `onPress={() => addWater(date, amount)}`. The only AppState listener in the tree is AuthProvider.tsx:149-154, which calls `flushSave` on background/inactive and nothing on 'active', and it renders `{children}` with stable identity so no descendant re-renders on resume. Nothing else forces a re-render while Home stays focused. Verified the diary/food-search screens compute their own date on mount (food-search.tsx:247), so the exposure is Home specifically: yesterday's totals and header date presented as today, and the first water tap writes into yesterday's DiaryDay.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Tapping a dashboard meal row can log food into a past day, because the Diary tab keeps its own date across tab switches</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** index.tsx:429 `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` passes no date. diary.tsx:670-671 holds the viewed day in component state — `const today = getTodayString(); const [date, setDate] = useState(today)` — initialised once on mount, driven thereafter only by the DateNavigator at :695. Tab screens stay mounted under expo-router/React Navigation, so the value survives switching tabs, and the meal card's own CTA at diary.tsx:563 pushes `{ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date } }` with that stale state. food-search.tsx:247 then trusts the param (`isISODate(params.date) ? params.date : getTodayString()`) and logFood writes to it at :407. Same state is never re-synced at midnight either.

**User impact.** A user checks Saturday's diary on the Diary tab, goes back to Home on Sunday morning, taps Breakfast and logs eggs — they land silently on Saturday and the food is written to Saturday's day. Home still shows 0 kcal eaten, so it looks like the log failed; the user logs it again and now Saturday is double-counted and the coach's 14-day intake average (useCoach.ts:60-99) is wrong in both directions.

**Fix.** Give the Link an explicit day — `href={{ pathname: '/diary', params: { date: today } }}` — and have diary.tsx seed and re-sync its `date` state from a `date` param (plus reset to today when the local date string changes), the same way food-search.tsx:247 already does.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>updateStreak compares a local 'today' against a UTC 'yesterday', so evening loggers west of UTC have their streak reset to 1 every single day</b> — <code>src/core/store/appState.ts:389</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** appState.ts:384 `const today = getTodayString()` is local (calculations.ts:143-146), but :387-389 builds the comparison day as `const yesterday = new Date(); yesterday.setDate(yesterday.getDate() - 1); const yesterdayStr = yesterday.toISOString().split('T')[0]` — setDate mutates local components, toISOString then re-reads them in UTC. In UTC-5 at 20:00 on Aug 10 the result is '2025-08-10', not the local yesterday '2025-08-09', so the `state.streak.lastLoggedDate === yesterdayStr` test at :391 fails and `newCurrent` falls to 1. In UTC+5:30 after midnight the same expression skips a day. The dashboard renders this value directly at index.tsx:177 `streakDays={streak.current}`.

**User impact.** A user in the Americas who logs dinner rather than breakfast watches the Day streak tile on Home reset to 1 every evening no matter how consistently they log. The single accountability number on the dashboard is destroyed daily and there is no way for the user to repair it.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change (src/core is generated): in appState.updateStreak derive yesterday with the existing local helper — `getDateString(new Date(Date.now() - 86_400_000))` or a noon-pinned date — instead of `toISOString()`. Same class of bug as onboarding.tsx:276.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Editing your name in Profile rewrites the dashboard's calorie goal with the flat ±500 formula, discarding the accepted coach plan</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:249</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** profile.tsx:238-250 commitBasics() writes name/age/height and then unconditionally calls `recalculateGoals()` (also at :477, :490, :504). appState.ts:226-239 recalculateGoals recomputes calories via calculateCalorieGoal (calculations.ts:23-27: `goal === 'lose' ? tdee - 500 : goal === 'gain' ? tdee + 300 : tdee`) and Object.assigns the whole macro set over `state.goals`. Onboarding explicitly refuses to do this for exactly this reason — onboarding.tsx:279-281: 'Not recalculateGoals(): that applies the shared flat +/-500 and would overwrite the pace-derived target the last step just showed'. The dashboard hero reads the result at index.tsx:132 `goalCalories={goals.calories}`, and useCoach.ts:195-200 derives `accepted` by comparing goals to the recommendation, so the plan also silently flips back to un-accepted.

**User impact.** A user accepts a 1,720 kcal cut plan, later fixes a typo in their name on Profile, and returns to Home to find the hero now says 'of 2,050 kcal' with different macro goals. Nothing announced the change, the coach card now disagrees with the hero again, and the day they thought they were tracking against no longer exists.

**Fix.** Only call recalculateGoals() when an input the formula actually consumes changes (age, height, gender, activity), never from a name commit; and gate it behind a confirmation when `useCoachData().accepted` is true, since it discards the plan the user accepted.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P2 (16)

<details><summary><b>Coach card shows an unaccepted plan's calorie target with nothing marking it as not yet applied</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:161</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** HeroCard is fed `goalCalories={goals.calories}` (line 132) while CoachCard renders `{formatNumber(recommendation.calories)} kcal/day` (line 394) straight from the stored recommendation. useCoachData already computes `accepted` (src/hooks/useCoach.ts:195: goals.calories === recommendation.calories && ...), and the dashboard imports neither useCoach nor useCoachData — it reads `recommendation` raw from the store at line 95.

**User impact.** A user who received a plan but never opened /goals to accept it sees "of 2,000 kcal" in the hero and "Cut · 1,720 kcal/day" 300px below. Both look authoritative. They cannot tell which number the "kcal left today" figure is counting against, so the screen fails its one job of answering "am I on track".

**Fix.** Read `accepted` from useCoachData() in DashboardScreen; when false, label the card "Proposed — not applied" and add an Apply action; when true, drop the duplicated kcal figure and show only the phase.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The mechanism is real: index.tsx:132 passes `goalCalories={goals.calories}` to the hero, index.tsx:394 renders `formatNumber(recommendation.calories)` from the raw store value read at line 95, and the import list (lines 1-32) contains no useCoach/useCoachData, so `accepted` (useCoach.ts:195-200) is never consulted. But the impact is overstated: the hero self-labels its number ("of 2,000 kcal" at lines 229-235, right beside the eaten figure), and the coach card is explicitly framed as a plan with a chevron into /goals and the a11y label "Coach plan: Cut, 1720 kilocalories a day. Open goals." (line 344-346). A user is not left unable to tell what "kcal left" counts against; they are left with an unaccepted plan that has no visible accept path from Home. That is noticeable confusion, not a materially broken task.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard meal rows route to the whole diary instead of the meal-scoped food search that already exists</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** MealsCard renders `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` for every row, including the empty-state row whose call to action literally says "Add food" (line 478). app/food-search.tsx:245 already accepts `useLocalSearchParams<{ meal?: string; date?: string }>` and app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:563 uses it: `router.push({ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date } })`.

**User impact.** Logging breakfast from the home screen is: tap Breakfast → land at the top of a full-day diary → scroll to the Breakfast card → tap Add food → search. Four taps plus a scroll for the app's single most frequent action, when a one-tap deep link into the pre-filtered search screen is already implemented.

**Fix.** For rows with nothing logged, use `href={{ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date: today } }}`; keep a separate chevron/route into /diary for rows that already have entries.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The routing claim is accurate: index.tsx:429 `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` wraps every row including the empty one whose CTA reads "Add food" (line 477-479), while food-search.tsx:245-254 already accepts and applies `meal`/`date` params and diary.tsx:563 already deep-links that way. The tap count is wrong though — from Home it is tap meal row → tap "Add food" on that meal's card = 2 taps plus a scroll, not four. Severity is friction on the most frequent action, not a broken task, so P2. (The genuinely severe consequence of this Link — landing on whatever day the Diary tab was left on — I report separately under missed.)

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard shows no workout state at all — an in-progress session is invisible from the screen the app opens on</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:132</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The card sequence at lines 132-179 is Hero, AI promo, Macros, Coach, Meals, Water, Steps, Weight, streak/burned. `activeWorkoutId` (src/core/store/appState.ts:112) is read only by app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1117 and app/lift-picker.tsx:127; DashboardScreen never selects it, never renders a start-workout action, and never shows today's session.

**User impact.** In an app sold as nutrition + strength training, a user who started a workout, backgrounded the app to answer a message and reopened it lands on Home with no sign a session is open and no way back into it except finding the Workout tab. There is also no way to start a workout from the screen the app opens on.

**Fix.** Add a compact card between Hero and Macros: when `activeWorkoutId` is set show the session name, elapsed time and Resume; otherwise show the last session date and a 'Start workout' button into /(tabs)/workout.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified: index.tsx:132-179 renders Hero, AI promo, Macros, Coach, Meals, Water, Steps, Weight, Glance — no workout content, and `activeWorkoutId` (appState.ts:112) is selected only at workout.tsx:1117 and lift-picker.tsx:127. But the stated impact ('no way back into it except finding the Workout tab') overstates it: _layout.tsx:192-198 mounts a permanently visible tab bar with a labelled Dumbbell "Workout" tab, so resuming a session is always one tap. This is a missing-affordance/IA gap on the landing screen, not a materially broken task.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Steps tile renders a confident 0 while the first Health Connect read is pending or after it fails</b> — <code>src/components/StepsCard.tsx:53</code> · empty-error-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const steps = todaySteps ?? 0`, then line 77 `<StatValue size={36}>{steps.toLocaleString()}</StatValue>` and line 83 `<ProgressTrack progress={goal > 0 ? steps / goal : 0} ...>`. useHealthSync initialises `todaySteps` to null (src/hooks/useHealthSync.ts:41) and readTodaySteps returns null both while nothing has been read and when the query fails — src/lib/healthConnect.ts:129 swallows every error with `catch { return [] }`. The file's own header comment says "an empty '0 steps' tile ... would be a lie, not an empty state."

**User impact.** Every cold start, a user with Health Connect connected sees "0 / 8,000" with an empty bar for the first few hundred ms, then it jumps to 6,431. If the read fails permanently (provider update required, revoked permission after grant) the tile shows 0 steps forever and the user believes their phone stopped counting.

**Fix.** Branch on `todaySteps === null`: show 'Reading from Health Connect…' for the in-flight case and 'Could not read steps — tap Refresh' for failure (which requires readSteps to distinguish empty from thrown), and only draw the figure and bar once a number arrives.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Code is exactly as described: StepsCard.tsx:53 `const steps = todaySteps ?? 0`, :77 renders it at 36pt, :83 feeds `steps / goal` to the bar. useHealthSync.ts:41 initialises `todaySteps` to null and refreshSteps (54-59) is fired from the mount probe (line 71), so the first paint after `granted` is genuinely 0. healthConnect.ts:129-131 swallows every read error into `[]`, and :135-140 turns that into null, so a permanent failure is indistinguishable from zero steps. It does violate "Never render a fabricated number as a placeholder". Demoting to P2: this is a secondary tile that renders only on Android with Health Connect granted, the wrong value is transient in the common case, and no core task is blocked.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Three simultaneous entry points to the same chat screen, one of them occupying the second-highest slot on the dashboard</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:134</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The header IconButton at line 124 (`accessibilityLabel="Open the nutrition assistant"` → router.push('/chat')), the full-width Surface card at lines 134-151 (Ask AI → router.push('/chat')), and the global AssistantButton FAB in app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:140-186 (same label, same destination) are all on screen at once. The card sits above MacroCard, CoachCard and MealsCard.

**User impact.** The second thing a user sees after their calorie status is a promo for a feature they can already reach from a permanent FAB and the header icon. Macros are pushed down and Meals — the daily job — sits fifth. The Sparkles icon also means two different destinations on one screen (header/card → /chat, CoachCard line 382 → /goals).

**Fix.** Delete the promo Surface and the header IconButton; the FAB from the tabs layout already covers the assistant from every tab. Reorder to Hero → Meals → Macros → Coach → Water → Steps → Weight. Give CoachCard a non-Sparkles icon (e.g. Target) so one glyph means one thing.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** All three entry points verified on one screen: index.tsx:124-129 header IconButton (accessibilityLabel 'Open the nutrition assistant' → router.push('/chat')), index.tsx:134-151 the full-width Surface promo occupying slot two, and _layout.tsx:140-186 AssistantButton FAB with the identical label and destination, mounted for every tab at line 199. The glyph-collision claim also holds: Sparkles at index.tsx:128 and :137/:149 goes to /chat while Sparkles at index.tsx:382 (inside the CoachCard Link at :350) goes to /goals. P2 is right — pure IA noise pushing Meals to fifth.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard tells the user they can log by speaking; the assistant is text-only</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:141</code> · copy · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Body size={12} tone="muted">Log meals, water or weight by speaking</Body>`. app/chat.tsx imports no audio/speech module (no expo-av, expo-speech, mic icon or recording state anywhere in the file — its icon set is AlertTriangle, Bot, Check, Droplets, Lightbulb, Scale, Send, User, Utensils, X) and its input is `<Field>` plus a Send button; the welcome copy is "Tell me what you ate...".

**User impact.** A user taps Ask AI expecting to talk while cooking with dirty hands, gets a keyboard, and has to type the meal anyway. The screen made a promise the product does not keep.

**Fix.** Change the copy to 'Describe a meal in plain English and it gets logged' (or ship dictation). Copy on the home screen must match what the destination does.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:141 renders `<Body size={12} tone="muted">Log meals, water or weight by speaking</Body>`. Grepping app/chat.tsx case-insensitively for expo-av|expo-speech|Mic|voice|speech|Audio|recording returns no matches; its imports (chat.tsx:1-36) are RN, expo-router, expo-blur, safe-area, uuid, lucide, core types and the app's own components, and the input is a `<Field>` with a Send Button (chat.tsx:548-564). The welcome text at chat.tsx:51-52 says "Tell me what you ate" and suggests typed phrases. The home screen promises a capability the destination does not have.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Going over a macro is invisible on the ring — the arc clamps and nothing changes colour, icon or wording</b> — <code>src/components/MacroRing.tsx:47</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `const clamped = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, s.progress))` — 100% and 240% of a macro draw an identical full arc. The caller (app/(tabs)/index.tsx:278-282) passes only `progress` and the macro hue; the legend figure at line 305 is rendered `color={item.color}` (the data-series colour) whether the user is at 40 g or 160 g of a 60 g fat goal, and there is no status icon or sentence anywhere in MacroCard. The hero has an `over` path (ProgressTrack `over` prop) but MacroCard never uses it.

**User impact.** A user checking whether they have room for a fatty dinner sees three full rings and has to read and mentally compare six small numbers to discover they blew past fat two meals ago. Per the design rules, status must ship with an icon AND words; here an overshoot ships with neither.

**Fix.** Pass an `over` flag per series and render the overshoot segment distinctly (e.g. a second dashed arc or a tick at the goal), and add a status line under the legend using theme.status + icon + words, e.g. 'Fat 28 g over'.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** MacroRing.tsx:47 `const clamped = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, s.progress))` and :65 uses it for strokeDasharray, so 100% and 240% draw identical full arcs. The caller (index.tsx:275-286) passes only progress/color/label and never the `over` affordance that ProgressTrack exposes (MacroRing.tsx:82-84, used by the hero at index.tsx:238). MacroCard has no status icon or sentence anywhere (lines 270-330) — the only overshoot signal is comparing '160' at line 312 against 'of 60 g' at line 319-324. The design rule requires status to ship with icon AND words. P2 stands: the signature element is silent on the one thing a user checks it for.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Every progress bar on the dashboard is an unnamed progressbar to a screen reader</b> — <code>src/components/MacroRing.tsx:96</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** ProgressTrack sets `accessibilityRole="progressbar"` and `accessibilityValue={{ min: 0, max: 100, now: ... }}` but never accepts or sets an accessibilityLabel. It is used four times on the dashboard: hero calories (index.tsx:238), water (index.tsx:536), steps (StepsCard.tsx:83) and weight goal (WeightTarget.tsx:107).

**User impact.** A VoiceOver/TalkBack user swiping the dashboard hears "progress bar, 46 percent" four times with no indication of what is 46 percent. The rule that every touchable needs a label has the same intent here: a role with no name is noise.

**Fix.** Add a required `label` prop to ProgressTrack, set it on all four call sites ('Calories against goal', 'Water against goal', 'Steps against goal', 'Weight goal progress'), and apply it as accessibilityLabel.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** ProgressTrack (MacroRing.tsx:86-114) sets `accessibilityRole="progressbar"` and `accessibilityValue={{min:0,max:100,now}}` at lines 96-97 and accepts no label prop (interface at 78-84). Its four dashboard instances are index.tsx:238 (calories), index.tsx:536 (water), StepsCard.tsx:83 and WeightTarget.tsx:107. The View has no text children, so TalkBack/VoiceOver announces a bare percentage four times. Real a11y defect, P2 correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The dashboard weigh-in field is the 8th card in a plain ScrollView with no keyboard avoidance</b> — <code>src/components/Layout.tsx:100</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Screen renders a bare `<ScrollView contentContainerStyle={{...}} showsVerticalScrollIndicator={false} keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled">` — no KeyboardAvoidingView, no `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets`. WeightTargetCard (rendered at index.tsx:173) contains a `<Field numeric keyboardType="decimal-pad">` plus its Log Button (WeightTarget.tsx:208-225) and sits near the bottom of a nine-card scroll.

**User impact.** Tapping the weight field on the dashboard raises the number pad over the field and the Log button on iOS; the user types blind or has to dismiss the keyboard, scroll and retry. Daily weigh-in is what makes the whole trend/ETA card work, so this friction degrades the screen's core promise.

**Fix.** Wrap the Screen body in KeyboardAvoidingView (behavior 'padding' on iOS) or set `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets` on the ScrollView; both fix every form on every screen using this shell.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Layout.tsx:99-109 renders a bare ScrollView with only contentContainerStyle, showsVerticalScrollIndicator and keyboardShouldPersistTaps — no KeyboardAvoidingView, no automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets, and no KeyboardAvoidingView anywhere in the Screen shell (lines 36-115). WeightTargetCard is the 8th of nine children (index.tsx:173) and contains a decimal-pad Field plus its Log Button at WeightTarget.tsx:206-226. app.json sets no android.softwareKeyboardLayoutMode, so Android's default resize mostly saves it; iOS overlays the keyboard as described. P2 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Day streak keeps showing a broken streak's old value until the user logs again</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:177</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** GlanceRow is fed `streakDays={streak.current}` and nothing recomputes it at render. `updateStreak` (src/core/store/appState.ts:383) is only called after a successful log — app/food-search.tsx:413 and app/chat.tsx:287 — and it only ever increments or resets to 1; there is no path that decays `current` when a day is missed. `streak.lastLoggedDate` is stored but never read by the dashboard.

**User impact.** A user logs for 12 days, stops for a week, opens Home and is congratulated with a 12-day streak. The one number on the dashboard meant to create accountability actively misreports the lapse, and silently drops to 1 the next time they log with no explanation.

**Fix.** Derive the displayed value at render from `streak.lastLoggedDate`: show `streak.current` only when lastLoggedDate is today or yesterday, otherwise 0 with copy like 'Log today to restart'. The store-side decay itself is an upstream web repo change to appState.updateStreak.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:175-179 feeds `streakDays={streak.current}` straight to GlanceRow with no derivation. appState.ts:383-400 updateStreak returns early when lastLoggedDate is today and otherwise sets current to previous+1 or 1 — there is no decay branch, and it runs only from food-search.tsx:413 and chat.tsx:287, both after a successful log. `streak.lastLoggedDate` is stored but never read by the dashboard. A lapsed user is congratulated with a stale streak until they log again. P2 correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"7-day average" is the mean of however many days had records, and includes today's partial count</b> — <code>src/components/StepsCard.tsx:56</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `weekAverage = weekSteps.length > 0 ? Math.round(weekSteps.reduce((sum, d) => sum + d.steps, 0) / weekSteps.length) : null`, rendered as `7-day average ${weekAverage} steps` at line 87. `weekSteps` comes from readSteps(7) (src/lib/healthConnect.ts:117-128), which builds buckets only from days that actually returned records, and its range `startOfLocalDay(end - 6 days)` → `now` includes today's incomplete day.

**User impact.** A user who wore their phone on 3 of the last 7 days sees the mean of those 3 days labelled as a 7-day average — inflated. Everyone sees today's partial count dragging the figure down in the morning. It is the only comparative number on the tile, so a user judging 'am I moving more this week' is judging a number that does not mean what it says.

**Fix.** Divide by 7 (missing days count as zero) or relabel to 'Average over N recorded days', and exclude today from the window since it is incomplete.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** StepsCard.tsx:54-57 divides the sum by `weekSteps.length`, and healthConnect.ts:117-128 builds the array from a Map keyed only by days that actually returned records, so `length` is the number of recorded days, not 7. The window at healthConnect.ts:106-107 runs from startOfLocalDay(now - 6 days) to `new Date()`, i.e. it includes today's partial count. Line 87 nonetheless labels the result '7-day average'. It is the only comparative figure on the tile, so the label misinforms in both directions.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Steps Refresh button gives no feedback at all — no spinner, no result, no failure message</b> — <code>src/components/StepsCard.tsx:69</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Button label="Refresh" variant="ghost" onPress={() => void refreshSteps()} ... />` with no `loading` prop. In src/hooks/useHealthSync.ts, `busy` is set only by connect (line 76) and importWeightHistory (line 88); `refreshSteps` (lines 54-59) sets no loading flag, and readSteps/readTodaySteps swallow every error (healthConnect.ts:129, :192), so a failed read resolves to null and the tile falls back to `todaySteps ?? 0`.

**User impact.** The user taps Refresh because the count looks stale. Nothing moves for a second or two, then either the number changes or — if the read failed — it silently stays put or shows 0. There is no way to tell 'still reading', 'unchanged', or 'that failed'.

**Fix.** Add a `refreshing` flag to useHealthSync set around refreshSteps, pass it to the Button's `loading` prop, and have readSteps distinguish empty from failed so the card can show 'Could not read from Health Connect'.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** StepsCard.tsx:68-73 passes no `loading` prop, and Button only shows an ActivityIndicator when `loading` is set (Button.tsx:93-94). useHealthSync.ts:54-59 refreshSteps sets no flag — `busy` is toggled only inside connect (76/83) and importWeightHistory (88/98) — and healthConnect.ts:129-131 / :192-194 swallow every error, so a failed read resolves to `[]`/null and the tile silently falls back to 0. The user gets no in-flight, no-change or failure signal. P2 correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>StepsCard renders data numbers in body font and via Intl, breaking both the typographic rule and the screen's own formatter</b> — <code>src/components/StepsCard.tsx:79</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Line 79 renders the goal inside a Body: ``<Body tone="muted">{`/ ${goal.toLocaleString()}`}</Body>`` and line 87 does the same for the average: ``<Body size={12} tone="muted">{`7-day average ${weekAverage.toLocaleString()} steps`}</Body>``. Both are Figtree, not Fraunces/tabular, against the rule "EVERY data number uses Fraunces via <StatValue>". Line 77 also uses `steps.toLocaleString()` while app/(tabs)/index.tsx:41 deliberately hand-rolls `formatNumber` with the comment that locale data resolving differently "would silently change the separator and the column width with it".

**User impact.** On the same scroll the user sees "6,431" (Fraunces, app formatter) next to "/ 8 000" or "/ 8,000" (Figtree, device locale). The steps tile visibly does not belong to the same product as the cards above it, and on a locale using thin-space grouping the two disagree outright.

**Fix.** Wrap both figures in <StatValue> and use the shared formatter — export `formatNumber` from a util (e.g. src/theme or src/utils/format.ts) and use it in StepsCard, index.tsx and WeightTarget.tsx.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** StepsCard.tsx:78-80 renders the goal inside `<Body tone="muted">{`/ ${goal.toLocaleString()}`}</Body>` and :86-88 the average inside `<Body size={12} tone="muted">`. Body applies fonts.body/Figtree (Text.tsx:79-84) with no tabular figures, while StatValue applies fonts.display/Fraunces + fontVariant tabular-nums (Text.tsx:47-51). docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md §3: 'Every data number wears Fraunces… Use <StatValue>'. Line 77 also uses toLocaleString where index.tsx:41-42 deliberately hand-rolls formatNumber for exactly the separator-stability reason quoted in its comment. The mismatch sits in the same row of the same card.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Target weight, percent complete and the weeks figure in the Weight goal card are body text, not StatValue</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:101</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Line 101 ``<Body tone="muted">{`${unit} → ${toDisplay(progress.targetKg)} ${unit}`}</Body>``, line 109 ``<Body size={12} tone="muted">{`${Math.round(progress.fraction * 100)}% of the way from your starting weight`}</Body>``, line 133 ``<Body size={13} tone="secondary">{`days left (${progress.etaWeeks} week...)`}</Body>``. All three are data numbers in Figtree without tabular figures, while the adjacent current weight (line 99) and the day countdown (line 131) correctly use StatValue.

**User impact.** Inside a single card the goal weight is set in one typeface and the current weight in another, so the pair a user is meant to compare (82.4 → 76.0) does not read as a pair, and the percentage jitters as it ticks.

**Fix.** Wrap the numeric spans in <StatValue size=... tone=...> and leave only the words in Body, matching how the hero card composes 'of 2,000 kcal' at index.tsx:229-235.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** WeightTarget.tsx:100-102 puts the target weight in `<Body tone="muted">`, :108-110 the percentage in `<Body size={12}>`, :132-134 the weeks figure in `<Body size={13}>`, while the adjacent current weight (:99) and day countdown (:131) correctly use StatValue. Same §3 violation, and it splits the one pair the card exists to compare (current → target) across two typefaces.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Onboarding files the first weigh-in under the UTC date, so the dashboard immediately asks for it again</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:276</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `addWeightEntry({ date: new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10), weight: ... })` uses the UTC calendar day, while everything that reads it uses the local day: WeightTarget.tsx:45-46 `const today = getTodayString(); const loggedToday = weightLog.some(e => e.date === today)`, and getTodayString (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:143) builds the string from local getFullYear/getMonth/getDate.

**User impact.** A user in UTC+5:30 who finishes setup at 01:00 has their starting weight filed under yesterday. The dashboard's Weight goal card immediately shows an empty 'Today's weight' field asking them to weigh in again, and the trend baseline is a day off from every later entry.

**Fix.** Use `getTodayString()` from @core/utils/calculations in onboarding's finish(), matching addWeightEntry callers elsewhere.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** onboarding.tsx:275-278 files the first weigh-in under `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` (UTC) while every reader uses the local day: WeightTarget.tsx:45-46 compares against getTodayString(), which builds from local getFullYear/getMonth/getDate (calculations.ts:143-146). Worth noting the failure is symmetric and worse westward: at 20:00 in UTC-5 the ISO slice yields TOMORROW's date, so the entry is future-dated, weightTrendKgPerWeek's `p.day <= 0` filter (weightTarget.ts:87) drops it while startKg (weightTarget.ts:167) still uses it, and loggedToday stays false. P2 stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard meal rows silently omit Pre-Workout and Post-Workout food, so the rows never reconcile with the hero total</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:55</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** index.tsx:55 `const MEALS: readonly MealType[] = ['Breakfast', 'Lunch', 'Dinner', 'Snacks']` with the comment 'pre/post-workout stay in the diary itself', and MealsCard maps only over that list (:420). But core types define six meal types (src/core/types/index.ts:44 includes 'Pre-Workout' | 'Post-Workout'), and both logging surfaces offer them: diary.tsx:38-39 and food-search.tsx:33-34. The hero and macro figures come from getDayNutrition (calculations.ts:76-92), which sums every entry regardless of mealType, and mealTotals (index.tsx:108-117) buckets them too — they are computed and then never rendered.

**User impact.** A user who logs a pre-workout banana sees 2,140 kcal in the hero while the four meal rows add up to 2,040, with no row accounting for the difference and no way to see or correct that entry from Home. In an app that pairs nutrition with training, the two meal slots built for training are the ones the dashboard hides.

**Fix.** Render any mealType present in `mealTotals` rather than a fixed four — keep the canonical four always visible and append Pre-Workout/Post-Workout rows when they have entries — so the rows always sum to the hero figure.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (6)

<details><summary><b>Hero leads with calories eaten rather than calories remaining</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:226</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Line 223 `<Label>Eaten today</Label>`, line 226 `<StatValue size={56}>{formatNumber(eaten)}</StatValue>`, and the remaining figure appears only inside the status sentence at line 246 as `<Body size={14} weight="semibold">{status}</Body>` where status is "1,140 kcal left today". `nutrition.netCalories` (calories minus caloriesBurned) is computed in core and never used on this screen.

**User impact.** Standing in a kitchen deciding what to cook, the question is "how much have I got left". That answer is the smallest text in the card and is rendered in Figtree, while the largest, most scannable element is a number the user cannot act on. It takes a read-and-subtract instead of a glance.

**Fix.** Optional: swap the 56pt figure to remaining ('Left today' / 'Over today') and demote 'eaten of goal' to the secondary line. Not a correctness issue.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Lines are right (223 Label 'Eaten today', 226 StatValue size 56, 246 the status sentence in Body size 14 semibold), but the supporting claims are not: the remaining figure is NOT the smallest text in the card — Label is 11px (Text.tsx:102) and the 'of 2,000 kcal' run is also 14px — and it is not unmarked, it carries a colour, an icon and words (lines 245-248) per the status rule. Leading a calorie card with consumed-of-goal is a defensible product choice, not a defect; the remaining value is already stated in plain language one line below. This is a hierarchy preference with weak user consequence.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Haptics fire on navigation (Ask AI button, assistant FAB, tab presses) but not on the water commits</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:147</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Button label="Ask AI" variant="primary" haptic onPress={() => router.push('/chat')} ... />` — the `haptic` prop triggers `Haptics.impactAsync(ImpactFeedbackStyle.Light)` (src/components/Button.tsx:69). The binding rule is "Haptics ONLY on meaningful commits (set completed, plan accepted, food logged). Never on scroll or plain navigation." Meanwhile the water quick-add buttons (index.tsx:558-577), which are real commits, fire none.

**User impact.** Taps that only open a screen buzz, and taps that actually write data do not, so the haptic stops meaning "that landed" — the exact signal it exists to carry.

**Fix.** Drop `haptic` from index.tsx:147 and the impact at _layout.tsx:150; reconsider the tab-press selectionAsync at _layout.tsx:88; add a light impact to the water quick-add Pressables at index.tsx:557-578, which are real commits.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Accurate: index.tsx:144-150 passes `haptic` to a Button whose onPress only calls router.push('/chat'), and Button.tsx:68-70 fires `Haptics.impactAsync(Light)`; the water quick-add Pressables at index.tsx:557-578 commit data via addWater and fire nothing. The rule at docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md §4 is explicit. Two corrections: this is not the only navigation haptic — _layout.tsx:150 fires the same impact for the FAB and _layout.tsx:88 fires selectionAsync on every tab press — and the user consequence is a weakened signal, not confusion or lost work, so P3.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Button press scale ignores useReducedMotion() and runs at 120ms against the 150ms spec</b> — <code>src/components/Button.tsx:63</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `onPressIn={() => { scale.value = withTiming(0.97, { duration: 120 }) }}` with `useAnimatedStyle(() => ({ transform: [{ scale: scale.value }] }))` at line 46. There is no `useReducedMotion()` check anywhere in the file (or in any component on this screen). The binding rule: motion "MUST honour useReducedMotion() (skip transforms, keep opacity)". The duration is also 120ms against the 150ms press spec.

**User impact.** A user with Reduce Motion enabled — often because scaling/zooming UI triggers nausea or vestibular symptoms — still gets a scaling transform on every button on the dashboard (Ask AI, Connect Health Connect, Refresh, Log weight).

**Fix.** Read `useReducedMotion()` from react-native-reanimated in Button; when true drive opacity instead of scale, and set both timings to 150ms.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified: Button.tsx:45-46 drives `transform: [{ scale }]`, :62-67 animates to 0.97 over 120ms, and a repo-wide grep for `useReducedMotion` returns only docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:85 — no component reads it. So the binding rule is violated and the 120ms also misses the 150ms press spec. But the motion in question is a 3% scale on press with no translation, parallax or looping — the vestibular impact is minimal, and nothing is blocked or misread. This is a compliance nit rather than P2 friction.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Water goal is never derived at onboarding and has no editor — it only changes as a hidden side effect of editing an unrelated profile field</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:165</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** WaterCard is fed `goalMl={goals.water}`. `goals.water` is only ever the DEFAULT_GOALS value 2500 (src/core/store/appState.ts:152): onboarding writes only calories and the three macros (app/onboarding.tsx:281 `updateGoals({ calories, protein, carbs, fat })`), `recalculateGoals` (which would set water = weightKg * 35) is deliberately not called, and grep finds no water control in app/goals.tsx or app/(tabs)/profile.tsx.

**User impact.** A 55 kg and a 110 kg user are both told their daily target is 2,500 ml, presented in the same authoritative style as their personalised calorie target, and neither can edit it. The 'Goal met' check at index.tsx:544 therefore congratulates or withholds on a number that means nothing.

**Fix.** Set water in onboarding's finish() from calculateMacroGoals (weightKg * 35) and add a water row to the goals editor so the number the dashboard presents is one the user chose.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Half the evidence is wrong. It is true that onboarding.tsx:281 writes only `{calories, protein, carbs, fat}` and deliberately skips recalculateGoals (comment at 279-280), and that grepping goals.tsx and profile.tsx for 'water' returns nothing — there is no direct editor. But `goals.water` is NOT 'only ever 2500': profile.tsx calls recalculateGoals() at lines 249, 477, 490 and 504, and appState.ts:226-239 does `Object.assign(state.goals, macros)` with calculateMacroGoals, which returns `water: Math.round(weightKg * 35)` (calculations.ts:44). So editing your name, age, height, activity level or gender silently repersonalises the water goal. The real defect is the opposite of 'immutable': it is unset at onboarding and then changes as an invisible side effect. A 2,500 ml default is also a defensible number, so the user impact is small.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The macro ring's centre repeats the calorie figure already shown at 56px in the hero</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:284</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<StatValue size={30}>{formatNumber(nutrition.calories)}</StatValue>` with `<Label>kcal today</Label>` at line 285 — the same `nutrition.calories` rendered by HeroCard at line 226 one card above.

**User impact.** The most valuable pixels inside the signature ring restate a number the user read two seconds ago, instead of answering the question the ring raises: how much protein is left.

**Fix.** Put the scarcest remaining macro in the ring centre (e.g. '48 g protein left') or the remaining calories, so the two cards answer different questions.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** index.tsx:284 `<StatValue size={30}>{formatNumber(nutrition.calories)}</StatValue>` with the Label 'kcal today' at :285 — the identical `nutrition.calories` value rendered at 56pt by HeroCard at :226-228, one card above in the same scroll. Genuine redundancy in the most valuable pixels of the signature component, but no correctness or task impact. P3 correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Backdrop and Surface hardcode colour literals that duplicate theme token values</b> — <code>src/components/Backdrop.tsx:31</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Line 31 `colors={[dark ? 'rgba(18,161,117,0.22)' : 'rgba(56,188,141,0.30)', 'transparent']}` — those are jade[500] #12A175 and jade[400] #38BC8D written out by hand; line 25 `'rgba(120,113,108,0.16)' / 'rgba(214,211,209,0.55)'` are stone[500]/stone[300]; line 61 is jade[600]/jade[300]. src/components/Glass.tsx:99 does the same with `shadowColor: '#1C1917'` (stone[900]). The binding rule: "Colors read through useTheme() only. NEVER a hardcoded hex in a component."

**User impact.** No immediate user-visible break, but the ambient wash behind every glass surface on the dashboard silently stops matching the brand if a jade or stone step is ever retuned — the exact drift the token rule exists to prevent.

**Fix.** Derive these from the imported `jade`/`stone` tokens with an alpha helper (e.g. `withAlpha(jade[400], 0.30)`) or add the wash colours to the Theme object and read them via useTheme().

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Checked every literal against tokens.ts: Backdrop.tsx:31 rgba(18,161,117) = jade[500] #12A175 and rgba(56,188,141) = jade[400] #38BC8D; :25 rgba(120,113,108) = stone[500] #78716C and rgba(214,211,209) = stone[300] #D6D3D1; :61 rgba(12,130,97) = jade[600] #0C8261 and rgba(113,213,175) = jade[300] #71D5AF; Glass.tsx:99 '#1C1917' = stone[900], and _layout.tsx:169 repeats that same literal plus '#FFFFFF' at :184. Backdrop.tsx:5 even imports `{ jade }` and never uses it. §2 says never hardcode a hex in a component. No user-visible break today, so P3 is right.

</details>

### Diary & food logging

#### P1 (1)

<details><summary><b>No way to create a custom food — the store action and the UI that lists them both exist, the creation screen does not</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:343</code> · broken-flow · effort L</summary>

**Code.** `addCustomFood` is defined at `src/core/store/appState.ts:336` and is called from nowhere in `app/` or `src/` (grep over the repo excluding src/core returns only `removeCustomFood` at `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:206,670`). `app/food-search.tsx:343` renders `addSection('custom', 'Your foods', customMatches)` and line 302 reads `customFoods`, but the screen has no "create a food" affordance anywhere — the only two states are the results list and the serving step. `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:652` literally promises "Foods you create while logging show up here."

**User impact.** The user eats a home-cooked curry, or a local brand of yoghurt. It is not among the 60 rows in `FOOD_DATABASE` and USDA either doesn't have it or is unreachable. There is no path forward at all — the user must either log a wrong food or skip the meal. Profile shows a "Custom foods — 0 saved" section with a delete button for a list that can never be filled.

**Fix.** Add a "Can't find it? Create a food" row to `app/food-search.tsx` (as the last item of the FlatList and inside the `EmptyState` action slot) that opens a form for name, brand, serving size, unit, calories and the macros, calls `addCustomFood`, then drops straight into the existing serving step with the new food selected.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified. `addCustomFood` exists at src/core/store/appState.ts:336 and a repo-wide grep excluding src/core returns zero call sites (only removeCustomFood at app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:206 and :670). app/food-search.tsx:343 renders `addSection('custom', 'Your foods', customMatches)` for a list nothing can populate, and app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:652 promises "Foods you create while logging show up here." The only two screen states in food-search.tsx are the results FlatList (line 678) and the serving step (line 524) — no create affordance in either, and EmptyState (line 692) is rendered with no `action` even though Layout.tsx:189 supports one. FOOD_DATABASE is 86 foods (the finding says 60), which does not change the conclusion.

</details>

#### P2 (20)

<details><summary><b>A logged entry's meal can only be fixed by deleting and re-logging it</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:376</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The expanded `EntryRow` editor (`diary.tsx:376-419`) offers exactly two operations: `applyServings` → `updateFoodEntry(date, entry.id, safe)` and a `Remove` button → `removeFoodEntry`. The store's signature is `updateFoodEntry: (date: string, entryId: string, servings: number)` (`src/core/store/appState.ts:263`) — servings is the only mutable field. There is no action to change `mealType` or move an entry to another date. Meanwhile `app/food-search.tsx:254` defaults to `'Snacks'` whenever `params.meal` isn't a valid MealType.

**User impact.** A user who logs their eggs while the meal chip still says Snacks (or arrives without a meal param) has to notice it in the diary, expand the row, tap Remove, tap Add food again, re-search the food, re-enter the amount, and re-log. A one-tap correction becomes a six-tap re-entry, and until they do it the per-meal totals are wrong.

**Fix.** Add a meal ChoiceChip row (reuse food-search.tsx:614-623) to the expanded EntryRow at diary.tsx:376. Upstream web repo change: add `moveFoodEntry(date, entryId, mealType)` or widen updateFoodEntry to a patch, since appState.ts:263 only writes `entry.servings`.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The core claim holds: diary.tsx:376-419 offers only applyServings → updateFoodEntry and Remove → removeFoodEntry, and appState.ts:263 takes `servings` as the only mutable field. But two supporting claims are wrong. (1) "arrives without a meal param" is unreachable — app/_layout.tsx:74 registers food-search and the ONLY navigation into it is diary.tsx:563, which always passes a valid `meal`; the 'Snacks' fallback at food-search.tsx:254 never fires in-app. (2) The meal IS changeable before committing — food-search.tsx:612-625 renders a full ChoiceChip row for all six meal types. So the gap is post-hoc correction only, and the recovery path (expand row, Remove, Add food, re-search) is available. That is friction, not a materially broken core task.

</details>

<details><summary><b>On iOS the decimal keypad covers "Add to <meal>" and the only way out is tapping blank space</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:535</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The serving step is a bare `ScrollView` (`food-search.tsx:535`) with `contentContainerStyle` paddingBottom of only `insets.bottom + spacing.xxl` (line 540). There is no `KeyboardAvoidingView` in `app/food-search.tsx` (grep: the only files using it are chat.tsx, login.tsx, onboarding.tsx) and no `keyboardDismissMode` on this ScrollView — `keyboardDismissMode="on-drag"` is set only on the results FlatList at line 683. The amount `Field` at line 575 uses `keyboardType="decimal-pad"`, and iOS's decimal keypad has no return key, so the `returnKeyType="done"` on line 580 renders nothing. The commit button `Add to ${meal}` is the last child of the scroll content at line 663.

**User impact.** User taps the amount field to type 1.5 servings. The keypad slides up over the bottom ~40% of the screen. The confirm button is now behind it and, because the ScrollView frame never shrinks, scrolling to the very bottom still leaves the button under the keypad. There is no Done key to dismiss with. The user has to guess that tapping a blank part of the card dismisses the keyboard before the button becomes reachable.

**Fix.** Wrap the serving-step ScrollView (food-search.tsx:535) in KeyboardAvoidingView with behavior='padding' on iOS, add keyboardDismissMode='interactive', and pin the commit button in a footer above the keyboard. No Android change needed — adjustResize already handles it.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Structurally accurate — food-search.tsx:535 is a bare ScrollView, no KeyboardAvoidingView in this file (grep confirms only chat.tsx:323, login.tsx:125, onboarding.tsx:318 use it), the commit button is the last scroll child at line 663, and iOS's decimal-pad has no return key so `returnKeyType="done"` at line 580 is inert. But the severity is inflated on two counts. (1) Android is fine: android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:21 has `android:windowSoftInputMode="adjustResize"` (and app.json sets no override), so the ScrollView frame does shrink and the button is scrollable into view — this is an iOS-only defect. (2) `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"` at line 536 means a tap on empty card space still dismisses the keyboard, so an escape exists; it is undiscoverable, not absent. Worth noting the screen is `presentation: 'modal'` (app/_layout.tsx:75), which makes the iOS coverage worse, not better.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"Recent" silently drops every USDA food, so the shelf is empty for the foods people log most</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:321</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `recentMatches` resolves ids with `const food = customFoods.find(item => item.id === id) ?? getFoodById(id)` (`food-search.tsx:321`). `getFoodById` (`src/core/data/foodDatabase.ts:151`) is `FOOD_DATABASE.find(f => f.id === id)` — the 60-item preset array only. But `addFoodEntry` pushes `entryData.food.id` into `recentFoodIds` (`appState.ts:253`), and a USDA food's id is `` `usda-${f.fdcId}` `` (`usdaApi.ts:67`). Those ids resolve to `undefined` forever and line 322's `if (food) found.push(food)` skips them.

**User impact.** A daily user logs their actual foods — a branded protein bar, a supermarket yoghurt — almost all of which come from USDA. Every one of them occupies a slot in the 20-id recent list but is invisible in the Recent shelf. Tomorrow morning the shelf is empty or shows two apples, and the user re-searches and re-enters the same food from scratch. The single biggest speed feature of a food tracker is dead for exactly the foods people log most.

**Fix.** Build recents from the diary rather than an id lookup — every FoodEntry carries a denormalized `food` snapshot (appState.ts:245-250), so walk `diary` newest-first. Upstream web repo change: store a denormalized `recentFoods: Food[]` alongside `recentFoodIds` in appState.ts:253.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The mechanism is exactly right. food-search.tsx:321 resolves ids via `customFoods.find(...) ?? getFoodById(id)`; getFoodById (src/core/data/foodDatabase.ts:151-153) searches only FOOD_DATABASE; appState.ts:253-254 pushes `entryData.food.id` into recentFoodIds; usdaApi.ts:67 mints `usda-${f.fdcId}`. Those ids resolve to undefined forever and line 322 skips them. The code comment at 319-320 shows this was a known trade-off, not an oversight. Two corrections: the category is not data-correctness — no logged number is wrong and no broken row renders (addSection at line 334 bails on an empty array, so the Recent header simply never appears); and the user can still search normally, so this is degraded speed rather than a broken task.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Dashboard meal rows say "Add food" but drop the meal and land at the top of the diary</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `MealsCard` renders one row per meal, each wrapped in `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429`). The row already has the meal in scope and its own a11y label says "Open diary to add food" (line 426). It navigates to the diary root, not to `/food-search` with `{ meal, date }`. Grep confirms `router.push({ pathname: '/food-search', ... })` appears exactly once in the whole app, at `app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:563`.

**User impact.** From the app's landing tab, logging breakfast is: tap the Breakfast row (1) → land at the top of the diary, scrolled to the date navigator and the big totals card, not to Breakfast → scroll down → tap "Add food" (2) → tap the search field (3) → type → tap the food (4) → tap "Add to Breakfast" (5). Five taps plus a scroll plus typing, when the very first tap already declared the intent.

**Fix.** Change the `MealsCard` rows in `app/(tabs)/index.tsx` to `<Link href={{ pathname: '/food-search', params: { meal, date: getTodayString() } }}>`. That collapses the journey to three taps: meal row → food → Add.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified. app/(tabs)/index.tsx:429 is `<Link key={meal} href="/diary" asChild>` with the meal already in scope; the row even renders the words "Add food" in brand text at line 477-479 plus a ChevronRight at 482, then lands the user at the top of the diary. Grep confirms router.push to /food-search appears once in the whole app, at diary.tsx:563. So the false affordance and the wasted intent are real. But the user does reach the diary and can complete the log — this is extra taps and a mismatched label, not a materially broken task, so P1 overstates it.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Recent disappears the moment the user types a single character</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:317</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `recentMatches` opens with `if (debounced.length > 0) return []` (`food-search.tsx:317`), so the Recent section is emitted only for an empty query. `customMatches` (line 302) correctly filters by the needle instead of bailing out.

**User impact.** A user who eats the same chicken breast every day types "chi" expecting their usual entry at the top. Instead Recent vanishes and they get the raw preset list plus a USDA spinner, and have to pick the right row out of a dozen near-identical descriptions.

**Fix.** Filter recents by the query the same way `customMatches` does (name/brand `includes(needle)`) instead of returning `[]`, so the user's own history stays first while they narrow.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** food-search.tsx:316 is literally `if (debounced.length > 0) return []` inside the recentMatches useMemo, while customMatches at 301-311 filters by the needle instead. So the Recent section (added at line 342) is emitted only for an empty query. The contrast with customMatches makes the inconsistency plain.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Switching between servings and grams silently throws away the amount already entered</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:399</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `switchUnit` (`food-search.tsx:399-403`) does `setQuantity(next === 'serving' ? '1' : formatAmount(selected.servingSize))` — it resets to one serving's worth regardless of what the user had typed. It never converts the current value.

**User impact.** User types 2 servings of Apple (medium), then taps the "g" chip to sanity-check what that is in grams. The field jumps to 182 — i.e. one apple. Their entered amount has been halved with no warning. Going the other way, 250 g becomes "1". If they don't notice, they log half the food they ate.

**Fix.** Convert rather than reset in `switchUnit`: `next === 'serving' ? formatAmount(parsedQuantity / selected.servingSize) : formatAmount(parsedQuantity * selected.servingSize)` when `quantityValid`, keeping the current reset only as the invalid-input fallback.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** food-search.tsx:399-403: `switchUnit` sets `setQuantity(next === 'serving' ? '1' : formatAmount(selected.servingSize))` with no reference to the current `quantity`. Typed 2 servings of Apple (servingSize 182, foodDatabase.ts:5) then tapping the 'g' chip yields 182 — one apple, not two. The preview at 629-660 does update consistently, so the number on screen is never a lie, but the user's entered amount is destroyed with no signal and the chips sit directly above the field where switching after typing is natural. P2 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>USDA search is permanently pinned to the shared DEMO_KEY — the configured key can never be read on Hermes</b> — <code>src/lib/env.ts:107</code> · empty-error-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `src/lib/env.ts:107` exports `USDA_API_KEY` from `EXPO_PUBLIC_USDA_API_KEY` (documented in README.md:40, .env.example:41, and injected by .github/workflows/android-release.yml:55). Grepping `USDA_API_KEY` across app/ and src/ returns only that definition and `src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:5` — the export has zero importers. Core reads Vite's `VITE_USDA_API_KEY` and falls back to `'DEMO_KEY'`, which its own comment on line 4 documents as "30 req/min, 1000 req/day per IP". `usdaApi.ts:57` throws on any non-ok status, and `food-search.tsx:492-511` renders every rejection as "Could not reach USDA FoodData Central" / "Check your connection."

**User impact.** Once the crash above is fixed, USDA search still runs on a globally shared demo key. Behind carrier NAT the per-IP quota is exhausted by strangers, so the user gets a 429 and is told their connection is broken — it isn't. "Try again" (line 506) retries straight into the same limit, so the advice is unactionable and the user concludes the app is offline.

**Fix.** Add src/lib/usda.ts owning the fetch and reading USDA_API_KEY from src/lib/env.ts:107, and import searchUSDA from there in app/food-search.tsx:25. Branch the error card at food-search.tsx:492 on status (429/403 → rate-limit copy, network failure → connection copy). Upstream web repo change: make src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts accept the key as an argument instead of reading import.meta.env, which is unreadable outside Vite.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The substance is confirmed and is in fact the real defect behind finding #1. Repo-wide grep for USDA_API_KEY returns exactly: .github/workflows/android-release.yml:55, .env.example:41, README.md:40, src/lib/env.ts:107 (the definition, zero importers), and src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:5. usdaApi.ts:57 throws on any non-ok status and food-search.tsx:492-511 renders every rejection as "Could not reach USDA FoodData Central / Check your connection." One correction to the mechanism: it is not merely that the export has no importers — even the core path can never pick up a key, because on Hermes `import.meta` compiles to Expo's ImportMetaRegistry, which exposes only a `url` getter (expo/src/winter/ImportMetaRegistry.ts:11) and no `env`. So the key is hardcoded to DEMO_KEY on device no matter what anyone configures, and the CI secret injected at android-release.yml:55 is dead weight.

</details>

<details><summary><b>USDA failure shows the error card and a contradicting "No matches" empty state together</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:691</code> · empty-error-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `hasResults = rows.some(row => row.kind === 'food')` (`food-search.tsx:366`). When the query has no local match and USDA errors, `rows` is `[{kind:'section'}, {kind:'usda-error'}]`, so `hasResults` is false and `usda.status` is `'error'` not `'loading'` — the `ListFooterComponent` condition on line 691 therefore also renders the `EmptyState` titled "No matches" with "Try a shorter word, a brand name, or the plain ingredient."

**User impact.** The user searches "kombucha" on a bad connection and reads two mutually exclusive explanations stacked on top of each other: "Could not reach USDA FoodData Central — check your connection" and then "No matches — try a shorter word". The second one sends them off rewording a query that was never the problem.

**Fix.** Suppress the footer empty state when the USDA request is not in a settled successful state — change line 691 to `hasResults || usda.status === 'loading' || usda.status === 'error' ? null : (...)`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Traced it. `hasResults = rows.some(row => row.kind === 'food')` at food-search.tsx:366; on a USDA error with no local match, rows is [section-usda, usda-error] (lines 347, 351), so hasResults is false and status is 'error', and the ListFooterComponent guard at 691 (`hasResults || usda.status === 'loading'`) does not suppress the EmptyState. The user reads "Could not reach USDA FoodData Central" and then "No matches — try a shorter word". Worth adding: the same double-message fires in the ready-but-empty case, where the usda-empty row at 515-521 says "No USDA matches. Try a shorter or more general word." immediately above the near-identical footer copy at 698.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Deleting a logged entry or a saved meal is instant, unconfirmed and unrecoverable</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:651</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `SavedMeals` puts an `IconButton` calling `deleteMealTemplate(template.id)` directly inside each template card (`diary.tsx:649-654`), immediately adjacent to the `Pressable` that applies the meal — both inside a horizontal `ScrollView`. No confirmation, no undo. Likewise `EntryRow`'s Remove button calls `removeFoodEntry(date, entry.id)` with no confirmation (`diary.tsx:415`). Nothing in the store keeps a tombstone or supports restore.

**User impact.** A saved meal represents real setup work (log every item, name it, save it). It sits one mis-swipe away from the trash icon in a horizontally scrolling strip — the exact gesture context where a stray tap during a fling is most likely. One accidental tap and the template is gone permanently with no dialog and no way back.

**Fix.** Gate `deleteMealTemplate` behind a confirm (Alert.alert with Cancel/Delete) and move it off the primary tap surface — a long-press or an edit mode rather than a trash icon inside the scroll strip. For `removeFoodEntry`, show a 5-second "Removed <food> · Undo" snackbar that re-adds the entry rather than deleting outright.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:649-654 puts an IconButton calling `deleteMealTemplate(template.id)` directly inside each template card, sharing a row with the apply Pressable (617-647), all inside a horizontal ScrollView (589-593). diary.tsx:415 calls `removeFoodEntry(date, entry.id)` with no confirmation. appState.ts:361-363 and 257-261 both filter the item out with no tombstone. The template case is the sharper one — a template is real setup work and the trash icon is a tap target inside a fling surface. The entry Remove is behind an expand tap, so it is less exposed, but neither is recoverable. P2 stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"kcal left today" is shown when the user is looking at yesterday or a future day</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:223</code> · copy · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `DayTotals` receives only `day` and renders `<Body size={12} tone="secondary">{`${remaining} kcal left today`}</Body>` (`diary.tsx:223`) with no reference to whether `day.date` is the current date. The over-goal branch at line 219 is date-neutral ("Over goal by N kcal"), but the under-goal branch is not. `DateNavigator` (line 118) lets the user move to any past or future date.

**User impact.** The user taps back to yesterday to check whether they hit their protein, and the card tells them "430 kcal left today" for a day that ended twelve hours ago. On a future date it invites them to "use up" a budget that hasn't started. Both read as the app having lost track of which day it is showing.

**Fix.** Pass `isToday` (already computed in `DateNavigator`, `diary.tsx:124`) down to `DayTotals` and switch the copy: "N kcal left today" only when the date is today, otherwise "N kcal under goal".

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:223 renders `{`${remaining} kcal left today`}` inside DayTotals, whose props are `{ day: DiaryDay }` only (line 191) — it has no idea what date is on screen. DateNavigator at 118-165 lets the user page to any past or future date via shiftISODate, and it already computes `isToday` at line 124. The over-goal branch at 215-221 is correctly date-neutral, which makes the inconsistency clearly unintentional.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Four mostly-empty meal cards sit between the user and the meals they actually log</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:698</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `DiaryScreen` renders `MEAL_TYPES.map(...)` over all six meal types unconditionally (`diary.tsx:698-700`), including 'Pre-Workout' and 'Post-Workout'. Each empty card is a full `Surface` with padding `spacing.lg`, a `SectionTitle`, the line "Nothing logged for pre-workout yet." and a full-height "Add food" button (lines 459-565). The "Add food" button is the LAST child of each card, so it sits below every entry already logged.

**User impact.** Someone who never logs pre/post-workout meals scrolls past two identical dead cards every single day, plus Snacks if unused. Combined with the date navigator and the tall totals card above, the Breakfast "Add food" button is already below the fold on first open, and on a day with five breakfast items the button is pushed further down still.

**Fix.** Render meal cards only for meals that have entries, plus the three canonical ones (Breakfast/Lunch/Dinner/Snacks), and collapse the rest behind a single "Add to another meal" row. Move each card's "Add food" to a compact icon button in the card header next to the kcal total so it stays reachable regardless of entry count.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:698-700 maps unconditionally over MEAL_TYPES (33-40), all six including Pre-Workout and Post-Workout. Each empty card is a full Surface with SectionTitle, the "Nothing logged for pre-workout yet." line (494-496) and a full-width "Add food" Button (559-564) that is the last child, i.e. below every logged entry. Above them sit DateNavigator and the tall DayTotals card (200-293, five stat blocks plus the sugar/sodium row). The exact fold position is device-dependent so I would not state it as fact, but the structural waste and the buried Add button are both real.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Editing an entry to below a quarter serving silently rewrites the number to 0.25</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:317</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `applyServings` in `EntryRow` is `const safe = round2(Math.max(0.25, next)); updateFoodEntry(date, entry.id, safe); setDraft(formatAmount(safe))` (`diary.tsx:317-321`). `commitDraft` (line 323) only rejects values that are non-finite or `<= 0`, so a valid entry like 0.1 passes straight into the clamp. Nothing tells the user their value was changed.

**User impact.** User had a tenth of a bagel, types 0.1, blurs, and the field snaps to 0.25 — two and a half times what they ate — recorded as fact in the day's calories. The only signal is that the number they typed is no longer the number on screen.

**Fix.** Either accept small positive fractions (the serving step in `food-search.tsx` already allows any positive value) or, if a floor is genuinely required, surface it: keep the typed value and show "Minimum 0.25 servings" in the same inline-error style used at `food-search.tsx:602`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:317-321 is `const safe = round2(Math.max(0.25, next)); updateFoodEntry(date, entry.id, safe); setDraft(formatAmount(safe))`, and commitDraft at 323-330 only rejects non-finite or <= 0, so 0.1 passes into the clamp. Nothing surfaces the change. This is not a contrived case: for the many 100 g foods in FOOD_DATABASE, 0.1 servings is 10 g of oil or nut butter — a real portion. The serving step in food-search accepts any positive value (quantityValid at 381), so the two screens disagree about the same quantity.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"Copy yesterday's meal" is implemented in the store and reachable from nowhere</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:698</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `copyDayEntries(fromDate, toDate)` (`src/core/store/appState.ts:270`) and `copyMealEntries(fromDate, toDate, mealType)` (`appState.ts:406`) are both fully implemented. Grepping `copyMealEntries|copyDayEntries` across app/ and src/ (excluding src/core) returns zero call sites. `diary.tsx` wires up `saveMealTemplate`, `applyMealTemplate`, `deleteMealTemplate`, `updateFoodEntry` and `removeFoodEntry` but never these two.

**User impact.** The single fastest path for a habitual eater — "same breakfast as yesterday" — exists in the codebase and the user can never invoke it. The nearest substitute is the saved-meal flow, which demands the user first log the meal, then find the bookmark icon in the card header, then name it.

**Fix.** Add a "Same as yesterday" secondary button to each empty `MealCard` in `diary.tsx` (shown only when `diary[shiftISODate(date,-1)]` has entries for that meal) wired to `copyMealEntries`, with the same success haptic used by `applyMealTemplate` at line 625.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified by grep across the repo excluding src/core: `copyDayEntries` (appState.ts:270) and `copyMealEntries` (appState.ts:406) have zero call sites — the only store hits outside core are removeCustomFood in profile.tsx. diary.tsx wires saveMealTemplate (433), applyMealTemplate (574), deleteMealTemplate (575), updateFoodEntry (302) and removeFoodEntry (301) but neither copy action. Both are fully implemented, including day creation and uuid re-keying.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Nothing indicates a logged entry is tappable to edit</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:340</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `EntryRow`'s `Pressable` (`diary.tsx:340-372`) toggles `editing` and renders name, meta and kcal — no chevron, no caret, no disclosure indicator, no border treatment. The only hint is in `accessibilityLabel` ("Show serving controls", line 343-345), which sighted users never receive. Every other tappable list surface in the app does signal itself: the dashboard meal rows carry a `ChevronRight` (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx:406`).

**User impact.** A user who logged 2 servings instead of 1 stares at the row looking for an edit button, finds none, and either leaves the wrong number or gives up and re-logs. The stepper, the numeric field and the Remove button — the entire edit and delete story — are behind an invisible tap.

**Fix.** Add a `ChevronDown`/`ChevronUp` (rotating with `editing`) at the trailing edge of the `EntryRow` pressable, matching the disclosure pattern already used elsewhere.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:340-372: the Pressable toggles `editing` and renders only name, meta and kcal — the only feedback is `opacity: pressed ? 0.6 : 1` (line 355) and the accessibilityLabel at 343-345, which sighted users never see. No chevron, no caret, no border change. By contrast every other tappable list surface signals itself: the dashboard meal rows carry ChevronRight at app/(tabs)/index.tsx:482. The stepper, the numeric field and the entire delete path (378-418) are behind that invisible tap.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Every USDA food defaults to a 100 g serving even when it is a single packaged item</b> — <code>src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:75</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The USDA mapper hardcodes `servingSize: 100, servingUnit: 'g'` for every result (`usdaApi.ts:75-76`), including the `Branded` data type requested on line 53, whose records carry real `servingSize`/`servingSizeUnit`/`householdServingFullText` fields. `openServingStep` then sets `setQuantity('1')` (`food-search.tsx:373`), so the amount step opens at 1 × 100 g.

**User impact.** The user picks a branded protein bar from USDA. The app proposes 100 g — a bar is typically 60-68 g — and the "Adds to your day" card confidently shows the calories for 1.5 bars. Nothing on screen hints that this is a generic per-100 g figure rather than the packet the user is holding, so the over-count is invisible.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change: map USDA's `servingSize`/`servingSizeUnit` when present (falling back to 100 g only when absent), and carry `householdServingFullText` onto the Food so the serving step can label it "1 bar (68 g)" instead of "1 × 100 g".

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:75-76 hardcodes `servingSize: 100, servingUnit: 'g'` for every result including the Branded dataType requested at line 53, and food-search.tsx:373 opens the step at `setQuantity('1')`. One nuance in the app's favour: the nutrients come from foodNutrients, which for Branded records are also per 100 g, so the figures are internally consistent and the user can switch to the g chip (servingUnit 'g' is in MEASURED_UNIT_STEP at line 50, so `measurable` is true at 378). The defect is the default and the missing household-serving label, not corrupt data — which is what the finding says. Correctly filed as an upstream change.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Logging food updates the streak against a UTC "yesterday", breaking the streak daily for western timezones</b> — <code>src/core/store/appState.ts:389</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `logFood` calls `updateStreak()` (`app/food-search.tsx:413`). Inside, `const today = getTodayString()` is local (`calculations.ts:143-146` builds from `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate`), but yesterday is computed as `yesterday.toISOString().split('T')[0]` (`appState.ts:389`) — UTC. In UTC-5, at 20:00 local on 2026-07-29, the yesterday Date is 2026-07-28 20:00 local = 2026-07-29T01:00Z, so `yesterdayStr` is `'2026-07-29'`, which equals `today`. `state.streak.lastLoggedDate === yesterdayStr` is then false for a genuinely consecutive day and `newCurrent` falls to 1. Separately, `updateStreak` always uses today regardless of which `date` the entry was written to.

**User impact.** A user in the Americas who logs dinner in the evening sees their streak reset to 1 every single day no matter how consistently they log — the one number the app uses to reward the habit. And back-filling yesterday's breakfast marks *today* as logged, so the streak counts a day the user never ate on record.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change: derive yesterday with the same local helpers — `getDateString(new Date(Date.now() - 86400000))` or a local-safe shift — instead of `toISOString()`, and take the logged date as a parameter (`updateStreak(date)`) so back-filling does not credit today.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified the mixed clocks. appState.ts:384 `const today = getTodayString()` is local (calculations.ts:143-146 builds from getFullYear/getMonth/getDate), while appState.ts:387-389 builds yesterday and calls `yesterday.toISOString().split('T')[0]` — UTC. Worked the example: UTC-5, 20:00 local on 2026-07-29 → the yesterday Date is 2026-07-29T01:00Z → yesterdayStr '2026-07-29' === today, so a lastLoggedDate of '2026-07-28' fails the comparison at 391 and newCurrent falls to 1. Any evening log west of Greenwich resets the streak. The second half is also right: updateStreak takes no date argument (interface line 72), so back-filling a past day from food-search.tsx:413 stamps today as logged. Correctly filed as upstream.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Logging a whole meal from a saved template never advances the streak</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:623</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** diary.tsx:623-626 is the entire commit path for a saved meal: `onPress={() => { applyMealTemplate(template.id, date); void Haptics.notificationAsync(...) }}` — no updateStreak call. A grep for `updateStreak` outside src/core returns exactly two call sites: app/food-search.tsx:413 and app/chat.tsx:287. So the store action at appState.ts:383 that owns `streak.current` only ever fires from the search flow and the AI coach, never from the template flow that diary.tsx:583 advertises as the one-tap way to log a meal.

**User impact.** A habitual user who logs breakfast by tapping their saved "Standard breakfast" card does everything the app asked and still watches the streak stay at zero. Since applyMealTemplate is pitched as the fastest path, the users who adopt it are exactly the ones the streak is meant to reward.

**Fix.** Call `updateStreak()` alongside `applyMealTemplate(template.id, date)` at diary.tsx:624. Better upstream web repo change: move the streak bump inside `addFoodEntry`/`applyMealTemplate`/`copyMealEntries` in appState.ts so no future caller can forget it, and take the logged date as a parameter (see the streak-utc finding).

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Applying a saved meal changes nothing on screen and silently duplicates on a second tap</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:702</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `<SavedMeals date={date} />` is rendered at diary.tsx:702, AFTER all six MealCards (698-700), so the strip lives at the very bottom of the scroll.
