Tapping a card calls `applyMealTemplate(template.id, date)` (line 624), which in appState.ts:365-377 pushes the entries into the meal cards far above the viewport. The only local feedback is `Haptics.notificationAsync` at 625 and `opacity: pressed ? 0.6 : 1` at 633 — no toast, no scroll-to, no count badge. appState.ts:371-376 has no dedupe, so every tap appends a fresh copy with new uuids.

**User impact.** The user taps their saved lunch and the screen does not visibly change, because the Lunch card is several screens up. The natural response is to tap again — which logs the whole meal a second time, doubling the day's calories, with no undo and no way to remove it except expanding and deleting each entry individually.

**Fix.** After applyMealTemplate, show a confirmation with the item count and an Undo that removes the just-inserted entry ids, and either move SavedMeals above the meal cards or scroll to the affected MealCard. Upstream web repo change: have applyMealTemplate in appState.ts:365 return the inserted entry ids so an undo can target them.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Hardware back / swipe-down from the amount step throws away the whole search instead of going back one step</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:446</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The two-step flow is component state, not routes: `selected` gates the amount screen at food-search.tsx:524, and only the in-app IconButton at 444-454 does `setSelected(null)`. app/_layout.tsx:73-76 registers the screen as `presentation: 'modal'` with `animation: 'slide_from_bottom'` and no `gestureEnabled: false` (contrast onboarding at _layout.tsx:72, which does disable it). A repo-wide grep for `BackHandler` returns zero hits, and there is no `usePreventRemove`/`beforeRemove` listener anywhere in app/. So Android's hardware/predictive back and iOS's modal swipe-down both pop the entire route from the amount step.

**User impact.** The user searches "greek yoghurt", waits out the 350 ms debounce plus the USDA round trip, picks a food, then swipes down or hits back intending to try a different result — and lands back in the diary with the query, the results and the selection all gone. They have to retype and re-wait from scratch.

**Fix.** Intercept back while `selected !== null`: add a `BackHandler.addEventListener('hardwareBackPress', ...)` (or navigation `beforeRemove`) in food-search.tsx that calls `setSelected(null)` and returns true, and set `gestureEnabled: false` on the food-search Stack.Screen at app/_layout.tsx:75 while the amount step is open.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Opening food search with no query shows 10 fruits, 11 vegetables and 4 grains — no eggs, chicken or bread</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:313</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** food-search.tsx:313 calls `searchFoods(debounced, 25)`, and with an empty query foodDatabase.ts:141 returns `FOOD_DATABASE.slice(0, limit)` — i.e. the literal first 25 array entries, which I enumerated from src/core/data/foodDatabase.ts: f001-f010 are the FRUITS block (Apple … Pineapple), the next 11 are VEGETABLES (Broccoli … Asparagus), then 4 GRAINS. The DAIRY, MEAT & POULTRY, FISH, LEGUMES, NUTS, BEVERAGES, SNACKS and FAST FOOD blocks (rows 29, 38, 48, 53, 59, 64, 69, 76) are all past the cut. food-search.tsx:344 labels this list "Food database", and there is no category filter anywhere on the screen.

**User impact.** A first-time user opens Add food to log breakfast and is shown watermelon, kale and asparagus. Nothing about the list suggests the database also contains eggs, chicken breast or bread, so the screen reads as a thin produce list rather than a food database, and there is no browse path — only a blind search.

**Fix.** When the query is empty, either show nothing but Recent/Your foods plus a prompt, or surface category chips backed by `FoodCategory` and browse within a category. At minimum stop using array order: seed the default list with one representative item per category rather than `FOOD_DATABASE.slice(0, 25)`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (8)

<details><summary><b>USDA fetch has no timeout or abort — a hung request spins forever with no escape</b> — <code>src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:56</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `searchUSDA` uses a bare `const res = await fetch(...)` (`usdaApi.ts:56`) with no `AbortController`, no `signal` parameter in its signature, and no timeout. The caller's guard at `food-search.tsx:284-298` only sets a `cancelled` flag to suppress the state update — the socket keeps hanging, and `usda` stays `{ status: 'loading' }` because neither `.then` nor `.catch` ever fires.

**User impact.** On a captive-portal wifi or a dead-zone cell connection, the row reads "Searching the USDA database…" indefinitely. The error card and its "Try again" button never appear, and because `hasResults || usda.status === 'loading'` suppresses the empty state (line 691), the user gets a permanent spinner with no way to tell that anything is wrong.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change: give searchUSDA an optional `signal?: AbortSignal` (or wrap in AbortSignal.timeout(~8000)) at usdaApi.ts:45-56. App side, create a controller in the effect at food-search.tsx:275 and abort it in the cleanup at 296-298 so the loading row resolves into the existing error card.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code facts hold: usdaApi.ts:56 is a bare `await fetch(...)` with no AbortController, no signal in the signature, and no timeout; food-search.tsx:284-298 only flips a `cancelled` flag. But the impact is overstated. The local sections still render above the spinner (rows are assembled at 328-364 with recent/custom/preset added before the USDA block at 346), so the user is not stuck — they can still pick a preset food, retype, or back out of the modal. And a genuinely hung socket is uncommon compared with the fast-failure case, which already lands in the error card. This is a missing-guardrail polish item.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The search field does not focus on open, costing a tap and a keyboard animation on every log</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:463</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `{selected === null && <SearchBar value={query} onChange={setQuery} />}` (`food-search.tsx:463`); the `TextInput` inside `SearchBar` (lines 169-185) sets no `autoFocus`. By contrast `diary.tsx:529` does use `autoFocus` — on the far less urgent "name this saved meal" field.

**User impact.** Every single food log costs one extra tap and one extra keyboard animation before the user can type a character. For an interaction repeated four-plus times a day, that is the difference between the search screen feeling instant and feeling like a form.

**Fix.** Add `autoFocus` to the `TextInput` in `SearchBar`, and pair it with the Recent fix above so the history shelf is still worth showing above the keyboard.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Factually correct — food-search.tsx:463 renders `<SearchBar value={query} onChange={setQuery} />` and the TextInput at 169-185 sets no autoFocus, while diary.tsx:529 does use autoFocus on the save-meal name field. But the cost is exactly one tap, and there is a defensible product reason not to autofocus: the screen deliberately opens onto browsable content (Recent at 342, Your foods at 343, Food database at 344), which a keyboard would immediately cover. That makes this a preference with a one-tap consequence, not P2 friction.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The target date disappears from the header once a food is selected</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:422</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `headerSubtitle` is `selected === null ? `${meal} · ${formatDate(date)}` : selected.brand ?? 'Choose an amount'` (`food-search.tsx:421-422`). Once a food is picked, the date is nowhere on the amount screen; the commit button says only `Add to ${meal}` (line 664).

**User impact.** A user back-filling yesterday's lunch navigates to yesterday in the diary, taps Add food, searches, picks the food — and from that point on every piece of on-screen context says "Lunch" with no date. They cannot confirm they are still writing to yesterday before they commit.

**Fix.** Keep the date in the subtitle on the amount step (`${formatDate(date)}${selected.brand ? ` · ${selected.brand}` : ''}`), or append it to the commit button label when `date !== getTodayString()`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** food-search.tsx:421-422: `selected === null ? `${meal} · ${formatDate(date)}` : selected.brand ?? 'Choose an amount'`. Once a food is picked the date is gone from the header, the commit button reads only `Add to ${meal}` (line 664), and nothing else on the amount screen mentions the date. Given the screen is a modal opened from a diary that can be on any date (diary.tsx:563 passes it), a back-filling user has no confirmation before committing. P3 is right — the date is still correct, only unshown.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The in-memory 60-item search waits 350 ms alongside the network search</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:313</code> · performance · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const presetMatches = useMemo(() => searchFoods(debounced, 25), [debounced])` (`food-search.tsx:313`) and `customMatches` (line 302) both key off `debounced`, which is only set 350 ms after the last keystroke (`DEBOUNCE_MS`, line 43; effect at 266-269). `searchFoods` is a synchronous `Array.filter` over the 115-row `FOOD_DATABASE` (`foodDatabase.ts:140-149`) — no reason to defer it.

**User impact.** Typing "egg" feels laggy: the list sits frozen for a third of a second after the last keystroke before local results move, even though the answer was available on the first keypress. It reads as the app being slow rather than as a network wait.

**Fix.** Run local search off the raw `query` and keep `debounced` for the USDA effect only — the local list then tracks keystrokes instantly while the network request stays throttled.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** food-search.tsx:313 `useMemo(() => searchFoods(debounced, 25), [debounced])` and customMatches at 301-311 both key off `debounced`, set 350ms after the last keystroke (DEBOUNCE_MS line 43, effect 266-269). searchFoods (foodDatabase.ts:140-149) is a synchronous Array.filter. One factual correction: FOOD_DATABASE holds 86 foods, not 115 — which only strengthens the point that deferring it is pointless.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Clearing the amount field flashes a red critical error while the user is still typing</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:602</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `{!quantityValid && (...)}` (`food-search.tsx:602-609`) renders a `TriangleAlert` in `theme.status.critical` with "Enter an amount greater than zero." on every render where `Number.parseFloat` fails — including the transient empty string between backspacing and typing the replacement. The "Adds to your day" card simultaneously swaps to its placeholder text (line 657) and the commit button disables (line 666).

**User impact.** User backspaces "1" to type "0.5". For the keystroke in between, a red error and a disabled button appear and the nutrition preview blanks — the screen looks like it rejected something the user hadn't finished saying yet.

**Fix.** Only show the error when the field is non-empty and unparseable, or after blur; treat the empty string as "still editing" and keep the previous preview dimmed instead of blanking it.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** food-search.tsx:602-609 renders the TriangleAlert in theme.status.critical whenever `!quantityValid`, and quantityValid (line 381) is false for the empty string because Number.parseFloat('') is NaN. Simultaneously the preview swaps to the placeholder at 656-660 and the button disables at 666. So backspacing '1' to type '0.5' does flash a critical error plus a blanked preview for the intermediate keystroke. P3 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Diary entries and saved meals render via .map inside ScrollViews</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:498</code> · performance · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `MealCard` renders `entries.map(...)` (`diary.tsx:498-509`) inside the `Screen` component's `ScrollView` (`src/components/Layout.tsx:100`), across all six meal cards. `SavedMeals` renders `templates.map(...)` inside a horizontal `ScrollView` (`diary.tsx:589-658`) with no cap on template count. Each `EntryRow` mounts a Pressable, three `Pill`s, a `StatValue`, and — once expanded — two `IconButton`s and a `TextInput`.

**User impact.** A heavy logging day (25-30 entries across six meals) plus a dozen saved meals mounts every row up front, so opening the Diary tab and scrolling it gets visibly heavier the more diligently the user logs — the opposite of the incentive the app wants.

**Fix.** Convert `DiaryScreen` to a `FlatList`/`SectionList` with `Screen scroll={false}` (the prop already exists at `Layout.tsx:27`) using meals as sections and entries as items, and give `SavedMeals` a horizontal `FlatList`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** diary.tsx:498-509 maps entries inside MealCard, all six of which sit inside the Screen ScrollView (src/components/Layout.tsx:100-109), and SavedMeals maps templates into a horizontal ScrollView at 589-658 with no cap. Each EntryRow mounts a Pressable, three Pills via MacroChips (95-114) and a StatValue, plus two IconButtons and a TextInput once expanded. `Screen` already accepts `scroll={false}` (Layout.tsx:27-28, 99-112), so the SectionList refactor is available. P3 is right — this only bites the heaviest logging days.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The dashboard Meals card silently drops Pre-Workout and Post-Workout, so its rows never sum to the day total</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:55</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** app/(tabs)/index.tsx:55 is `const MEALS: readonly MealType[] = ['Breakfast', 'Lunch', 'Dinner', 'Snacks']` and MealsCard maps over it at line 420. But `mealTotals` (108-117) buckets EVERY entry by `entry.mealType`, and the diary offers six meals (app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:33-40, food-search.tsx:28-35 both include 'Pre-Workout' and 'Post-Workout'). Those two buckets are computed and then never rendered, while their calories still land in `nutrition` via getDayNutrition (index.tsx:106, calculations.ts:76-92) which feeds the hero card.

**User impact.** A user who logs a pre-workout shake sees it counted in the day's calorie ring but absent from every row of the Meals card, so the four meal rows visibly fail to add up to the headline number and the shake looks like it was lost.

**Fix.** Either render any mealType present in `mealTotals` that is not in MEALS as an extra row, or add an "Other" row aggregating the leftovers. Keep MEALS as the canonical order and append the rest.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>A long USDA food name expands the food-search header to several lines</b> — <code>app/food-search.tsx:456</code> · layout · effort S</summary>

**Code.** food-search.tsx:420 sets `headerTitle = selected === null ? 'Add food' : selected.name` and line 456 renders `<SectionTitle>{headerTitle}</SectionTitle>`. SectionTitle (src/components/Text.tsx:115-125) is a plain Text with fontSize 20 and no `numberOfLines` prop at all — unlike the subtitle directly beneath it at line 457, which does set `numberOfLines={1}`. USDA descriptions are long by construction (usdaApi.ts:70-72 just sentence-cases `f.description`, e.g. "Chicken, broilers or fryers, breast, meat only, cooked, roasted"), and FoodRow guards against this with `numberOfLines={2}` at line 216 while the header does not.

**User impact.** Picking a typical USDA result grows the glass header to three or four lines, shoving the Amount card and the +/- controls down the screen — worst on the amount step, which is precisely where the keyboard is already competing for vertical space.

**Fix.** Add a `numberOfLines` prop to SectionTitle in src/components/Text.tsx:115 and pass `numberOfLines={2}` at food-search.tsx:456.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

### Workout

#### P1 (7)

<details><summary><b>Tapping a weight or reps cell never scrolls it above the keypad — the field is typed into blind until the user drags the list by hand</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1141</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `WorkoutScreen` renders everything inside `<Screen>`, whose ScrollView (src/components/Layout.tsx:100-107) sets only `contentContainerStyle`, `showsVerticalScrollIndicator` and `keyboardShouldPersistTaps`. There is no `KeyboardAvoidingView`, no `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets`, and no `keyboardDismissMode`. `grep -rn "KeyboardAvoidingView" app src` returns hits only in app/chat.tsx, app/login.tsx and app/onboarding.tsx — never workout.tsx or lift-picker.tsx. Bottom padding is `TAB_BAR_SPACE + insets.bottom + spacing.xl` = 76+34+24 ≈ 134pt, against an iOS numeric keypad of ~216pt plus the home indicator.

**User impact.** Mid-set the user taps the reps field of the exercise they are actually doing (always the last card, since exercises are appended as you go), the keypad slides up over it, and nothing scrolls. They type the reps blind and cannot see or reach the green Done checkbox or the 'Add set' button beneath it without manually dragging the list with one sweaty thumb while the keyboard is up. On a first session with no history below the cards, the last ~80pt of content cannot be scrolled clear of the keyboard at all.

**Fix.** Add `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets` (iOS) plus `keyboardDismissMode="interactive"` to the ScrollView in src/components/Layout.tsx:100, exposed behind a `keyboardAvoiding` prop that workout.tsx opts into, so the focused row scrolls into view. An InputAccessoryView 'Done' bar is optional — keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled" already lets a tap outside dismiss the keypad.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified. src/components/Layout.tsx:100-107 — the Screen ScrollView sets only contentContainerStyle, showsVerticalScrollIndicator and keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"; no KeyboardAvoidingView, no automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets, no keyboardDismissMode. Grep across app/ and src/ confirms KeyboardAvoidingView appears only in app/login.tsx:125, app/onboarding.tsx:318 and app/chat.tsx:323 — never workout.tsx. RN ScrollView does not auto-scroll a focused TextInput into view, so tapping a reps cell on the 3rd or 4th exercise card leaves it behind the keypad. TWO corrections. (a) The claim 'nothing scrolls' / 'the last ~80pt cannot be scrolled clear of the keyboard at all' is wrong: below the last set row sit the card's 'Add set' button (44), 'Add exercise' (44), 'Cancel workout' (44), three spacing.lg gaps and 134pt of bottom padding — well over 300pt, so the user CAN drag the content clear, it just never happens automatically. (b) keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled" (Layout.tsx:106) means the green Done checkbox and 'Add set' are tappable in ONE tap while the keyboard is up, so the 'cannot reach Done' part is overstated. The real defect is the missing auto-scroll-to-focused-input, which stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>A set can be ticked complete with a blank weight, silently logging 0 kg while the placeholder shows a real number</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:246</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const canComplete = set.reps > 0` (line 246) — weight is not checked. `handleWeight` writes `weightKg: value === null ? 0 : toKg(...)` (line 216), so an untouched weight cell stays 0 while the placeholder renders the suggestion, e.g. `String(fromKg(suggestion.weightKg, unit))` = "225" (lines 291-295). Downstream, `setVolume` returns 0 for `weightKg <= 0` (src/core/utils/workoutMath.ts:72), the PR loop skips it (`set.weightKg <= 0 || set.reps <= 0`, line 691), and `groupSets` renders it as `${group.count} × ${group.reps}` with no load (line 1016).

**User impact.** The user sees "225" already sitting in the weight cell, types 8 in reps, taps Done, and the row turns green — it looks logged. In fact 0 kg was recorded: that set adds nothing to session volume, cannot trigger the PR badge, and reads back in history as "1 × 8" with the weight gone forever. The number they lifted is unrecoverable.

**Fix.** Either commit the placeholder value to state when the user completes a set with a blank weight (write `suggestion.weightKg` into `weightKg` in `handleComplete`), or disable Done until weight is entered for a non-bodyweight lift and say why in the existing `accessibilityHint`. Bodyweight lifts (`suggestion.weightKg === 0`) should keep the current behaviour and show the word 'Bodyweight' in the cell rather than a blank.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:246 is `const canComplete = set.reps > 0` — weight is not part of the gate, and the Done Pressable's disabled prop (line 330) uses only that. handleWeight (216) writes `weightKg: value === null ? 0 : toKg(...)`, so an untouched cell stays 0 while lines 291-295 render the suggestion as visible grey text (e.g. '225'). Confirmed downstream: src/core/utils/workoutMath.ts:70-74 setVolume returns 0 for weightKg <= 0; the PR loop skips it at workout.tsx:691 (`set.weightKg <= 0 || set.reps <= 0`); groupSets renders it as `${group.count} × ${group.reps}` with no load at line 1016. The row still turns green (settled styling, lines 254-255) and still counts in sessionSetCount (isWorkingSet at workoutMath.ts:34-35 requires only completed + not warmup + reps > 0), so the hero reports the set but not its volume. The number the user lifted is unrecoverable. Severity P1 is right — silent data corruption, not a crash.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The delete-set bin sits 4pt from the Done checkbox and deletes an unticked set on the first tap</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:240</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `handleRemove` is `if (!set.completed || confirmRemove) onRemove(); else setConfirmRemove(true)` (lines 240-243) — the two-tap guard applies ONLY to already-completed sets. The Done Pressable (lines 319-345, `width: COL_ACTION` = 44) and the Trash2 Pressable (lines 347-367, also 44) are laid out in the same row with `gap: ROW_GAP` = 4 (lines 149, 260).

**User impact.** The mis-tap the guard should protect against is exactly the one it allows: the user has just typed 102.5 × 8, reaches for the green tick, lands 4pt right on the bin, and the entire row — weight and reps included — vanishes with no confirmation and no undo. The confirmation only kicks in after the set is safely ticked off, which is when the user is least likely to hit the bin by accident.

**Fix.** Invert the guard: require the two-tap confirm whenever the row holds real work (`set.reps > 0 || set.weightKg > 0 || set.completed`), free-delete only a genuinely empty row. Additionally separate the two controls — move the bin behind a swipe-to-delete gesture or push it to the far right with `spacing.md` (12) of clearance instead of 4.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:240-243 is literally `if (!set.completed || confirmRemove) onRemove(); else setConfirmRemove(true)` — the two-tap guard fires only for sets that are ALREADY completed. The comment directly above it (238-239) states the intent as 'a set that already holds real work asks twice. An untouched set goes on the first tap', so the implementation contradicts its own stated intent: a row holding a typed-but-unticked 102.5 × 8 counts as 'untouched'. Geometry confirmed: Done Pressable width COL_ACTION = 44 (319-345), Trash2 Pressable width 44 (347-367), laid out in the same flex row with `gap: ROW_GAP` where ROW_GAP = 4 (149, 260). Irreversible loss of just-entered data with no confirm and no undo, adjacent to the control the user is aiming for. P1 stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The X on an exercise card deletes the lift and every set logged against it with no confirm and no undo</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:522</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Pressable accessibilityLabel={`Remove ${exercise.lift.name} from this workout`} onPress={onRemoveExercise}>` (lines 522-536) calls `removeExerciseFromWorkout(session.id, exercise.id)` (line 814) directly, which does `session.exercises = session.exercises.filter(e => e.id !== exerciseId)` (src/core/store/appState.ts:524-528). No `confirm` state, no `AlertTriangle` branch — unlike removing a single set (line 240), cancelling a workout (lines 830-858) and deleting a session (lines 1057-1080), which all ask twice.

**User impact.** Five completed sets of Back Squat are destroyed by one 44pt tap in the top-right corner of the card — the exact zone a thumb brushes when scrolling the list one-handed. The app asks twice before deleting one set but zero times before deleting fifteen.

**Fix.** Give the exercise card the same two-tap confirm pattern already used by `SetRow.handleRemove` and `HistoryCard.confirmDelete`, wording it with the stake ('Remove Back Squat and its 5 logged sets?'). Skip the confirm only when `exercise.sets` is empty.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:522-536 — the X Pressable's onPress is `onRemoveExercise`, wired at line 814 straight to `removeExerciseFromWorkout(session.id, exercise.id)`, which is `session.exercises = session.exercises.filter(e => e.id !== exerciseId)` at src/core/store/appState.ts:524-528. No confirm state, no AlertTriangle branch, no undo. The inconsistency is real and verified: SetRow.handleRemove confirms (240), Cancel workout confirms (830-858), HistoryCard delete confirms (1057-1080), deleting a whole exercise plus every set logged against it does not. P1 is correct — mis-tap data loss, not a guaranteed one, so not P0.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The rest timer is an inline block near the top of the scroll — it is gone by the time you are logging the next set</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:770</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `<RestTimer triggerKey={restKey} onDismiss={() => setRestKey(0)} />` (line 770) is rendered as an ordinary child between the `GlassSurface` hero and the exercise cards, inside `Screen`'s ScrollView (src/components/Layout.tsx:100). It is not positioned, not sticky, and not a `stickyHeaderIndices` target. RestTimer itself is a plain `<View>` with `radius.control` (src/components/RestTimer.tsx:77-85).

**User impact.** The user ticks off set 2 of exercise 4, scrolls down to look at the next row, and the countdown is now two screens above them. To check 'how long left?' — the single question rest is for — they scroll up, losing their place in the set grid, then scroll back down. The one number they need between sets is the one number they cannot see.

**Fix.** Promote the rest timer to a pinned bar: absolutely position it above the tab bar (`bottom: TAB_BAR_SPACE + insets.bottom`) as a GlassSurface pill — sheets/floating chrome is exactly where glass is permitted per docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md — so it stays visible over the scrolling set grid, and add its height to the ScrollView's bottom padding while active.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:770 renders `<RestTimer triggerKey={restKey} onDismiss={...} />` as an ordinary child between the GlassSurface hero (719-767) and the exercise list (789-816), inside Screen's plain ScrollView (src/components/Layout.tsx:100-109) — no position, no stickyHeaderIndices, and RestTimer itself is a plain View (RestTimer.tsx:77-85). With 4+ exercise cards open, the countdown is one to two screens above the row the user is logging into. The rubric explicitly lists 'misplaced' under P1, and this is the definition of it: the one number rest exists to show is the one the user must scroll away from their work to see. Severity and fix (pin it as a floating GlassSurface pill above the tab bar — permitted glass per docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md) both stand.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Every character typed into a weight field serialises the entire store — including up to 20 base64 JPEGs — to AsyncStorage</b> — <code>src/store/useStore.ts:25</code> · performance · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `persist(immer(createAppState), { name: 'macrofit-storage', storage: createJSONStorage(() => AsyncStorage) })` (lines 24-32) declares no `partialize`, so the whole `AppState` is stringified on every `set()`. `handleWeight`/`handleReps` call `onChange` on every `onChangeText` (app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:213-223) → `updateSet` (line 797) → a store write per character. The persisted blob includes `progressPhotos`, typed `dataUrl: string // base64 compressed JPEG` (src/core/types/index.ts:186-192), capped at 20 entries (src/core/store/appState.ts:426), plus the full `diary` map and `workoutLog`. `renameWorkout` (workout.tsx:658-665) is worse still: it rebuilds every session object in the log on each keystroke.

**User impact.** Typing "102.5" into a weight cell fires five full JSON serialisations of several megabytes of base64 image data on the JS thread. On a mid-range Android device the keypad visibly lags and characters drop — while the user is trying to log a set with one hand between working sets.

**Fix.** SetRow already holds `weightText`/`repsText` locally, so commit to the store on `onEndEditing`/`onBlur` and on `handleComplete` instead of on every `onChangeText`. Independently, add a `partialize` (or debounced storage wrapper) in src/store/useStore.ts, and move progress-photo bytes to `expo-file-system` with only the URI persisted — an upstream change to the web repo's `ProgressPhoto` shape.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified end to end. src/store/useStore.ts:24-32 — `persist(immer(createAppState), { name, storage: createJSONStorage(() => AsyncStorage), onRehydrateStorage })` with no partialize, no debounce; createJSONStorage runs JSON.stringify of the entire AppState synchronously on the JS thread on every set(). The write path per character is confirmed: handleWeight/handleReps (workout.tsx:213-223) call onChange on every onChangeText → updateSet at 797 → appState.ts:537-544 mutates through immer → persist write. The blob really does include base64 image bytes: ProgressPhoto.dataUrl is typed `string // base64 compressed JPEG` (src/core/types/index.ts:186-192) and appState.ts:425-428 keeps up to 20 of them, plus the whole `diary` map and `workoutLog`. renameWorkout (658-665) is worse as claimed — `useStore.setState({ workoutLog: ...map(...) })` allocates a new object for every session in the log per keystroke and triggers its own persist write. P1 is right for the one screen where input latency is the whole experience.

</details>

<details><summary><b>A mistyped weight can never be corrected — a finished session is read-only and poisons every future target for that lift</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:972</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** HistoryCard's expanded body (lines 972-1101) renders sets as static text only — `<StatValue>{group.weightKg > 0 ? `${group.count} × ${group.reps} @ ${fromKg(group.weightKg, unit)} ${unitLabel}` : ...}` at 1013-1017 — with no TextInput, no per-set controls, and exactly two actions: 'Save as template' and 'Delete' (1082-1100). The AppState interface (src/core/store/appState.ts:114-125) exposes updateSet/removeSet but they need an active session, and nothing can reopen one: endWorkout (498-502) sets endedAt and nulls activeWorkoutId, and there is no resume action. So the only route to a typo is deleteWorkout(session.id) (553-556), which destroys the whole session.

**User impact.** The user fat-fingers 1025 instead of 102.5 and taps Done. That set is now permanent. The Personal records table (workout.tsx:1131, 1227-1289) reports an est. 1RM of 1025 kg forever, and worse, suggestNextSet picks the heaviest completed set as the working weight (src/core/utils/workoutMath.ts:202-226), so the target on the Back Squat card reads '1027.5 kg × 5' from then on — the progression engine is broken for that lift. The only fix on offer deletes every other set of that session too.

**Fix.** Make HistoryCard's expanded sets editable for a finished session: render SetRow (already built, workout.tsx:171) against updateSet/removeSet, which take a sessionId and work regardless of activeWorkoutId (appState.ts:537-551) — no upstream change needed for the edit path. If reopening is preferred instead, add resumeWorkout(sessionId) upstream in the web repo's src/core/store/appState.ts.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P2 (16)

<details><summary><b>New sets arrive empty — the previous set's weight and reps are a grey placeholder, never a prefilled value</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:806</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `onAddSet` always calls `addSet(session.id, exercise.id, { weightKg: 0, reps: 0, isWarmup: false, completed: false })` (lines 806-813). `placeholderFor(position)` (lines 468-476) walks back to the previous set but the result is passed as `suggestion` and rendered only as `placeholder=` on the TextInputs (lines 291-295, 313) — the comment at line 162-165 states it is 'PLACEHOLDER text only'. Completion is gated by `const canComplete = set.reps > 0` (line 246), so an untouched set can never be ticked. There are no +/- steppers on weight or reps anywhere in the file.

**User impact.** A straight 5×5 costs the user: 5 × (tap Add set → tap weight → type 3 digits → tap reps → type 1 digit → tap Done) = 25 taps plus 20 keypad digits, for a workout where sets 2-5 are identical to set 1. Standing at the rack with chalked hands, the single most common action in strength training — 'same again' — has no one-tap path.

**Fix.** In `onAddSet`, seed the new set from the last set of that exercise (`weightKg`/`reps` copied, `completed:false`) so the row arrives filled and one tap on Done logs it. Keep the progressive-overload suggestion as the placeholder only for the first set. Add 44pt −/+ steppers flanking the weight cell (plate increments: 2.5/1.25 kg or 5/2.5 lb) so the keyboard is optional.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Code verified exactly as described: app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:806-813 always seeds `{ weightKg: 0, reps: 0, isWarmup: false, completed: false }`; placeholderFor (468-476) walks back to the previous set but its result reaches SetRow only as `placeholder=` (291-295, 313), and the comment at 162-165 says so explicitly; canComplete = set.reps > 0 (246); there are no +/- steppers anywhere in the file. Tap arithmetic checks out. Severity is the only thing wrong: the set is logged correctly and the task completes — nothing is broken or mis-recorded, it is repetitive typing. That is the P2 definition ('friction a real user would notice'), not P1. The genuinely P1 consequence of this design (a grey placeholder that looks like a committed value) is already carried by complete-set-with-zero-weight.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Finish workout closes the session on one unguarded tap and the session can never be reopened; the only summary is a collapsed history row further down the page</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:766</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `<Button label="Finish workout" full haptic onPress={endWorkout} />` (line 766) sits inside the glass hero at the very top of the screen and calls the store action unguarded. `endWorkout` sets `session.endedAt = Date.now(); state.activeWorkoutId = null` (src/core/store/appState.ts:498-502). `activeWorkoutId` is only ever set by `startWorkout` and `applyWorkoutTemplate` — `grep -rn "resumeWorkout|reopenWorkout"` returns nothing. After the tap the screen falls through to the 'Ready to train?' card (lines 1173-1188) and the session drops into the collapsed History list.

**User impact.** Two failures at the moment the workout ends. (1) One mis-tap on a full-width primary button at the top of the screen irreversibly closes the session — the user has to start a brand-new workout and re-log, because nothing can reopen a session with `endedAt` set. (2) Even when deliberate, the payoff is nothing: the PR badges earned during the session, the volume total and the elapsed time disappear from view instantly. The user gets a haptic buzz and an empty start screen where a summary belongs.

**Fix.** Put a confirm in front of Finish that names what is being closed (sets, volume, duration, PRs hit) with 'Save workout' / 'Keep training', and auto-expand the resulting HistoryCard so the summary that already exists at lines 972-1024 is what the user lands on. A `resumeWorkout(sessionId)` action (clear endedAt, restore activeWorkoutId) is an upstream change to the web repo's src/core/store/appState.ts.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Half right. Verified: line 766 `<Button label="Finish workout" full haptic onPress={endWorkout} />` is unguarded; appState.ts:498-502 sets endedAt and nulls activeWorkoutId; the AppState interface (appState.ts:114-125) exposes startWorkout / endWorkout / cancelWorkout / applyWorkoutTemplate and no resume, so a finished session genuinely cannot be reopened. But the impact is overstated on both counts. (1) Nothing is lost — the session and all its sets stay in workoutLog and render immediately; the user can start a fresh session and keep lifting, they just end up with two sessions for the day. That is annoyance, not irreversibility. (2) 'The payoff is nothing' is false: with activeWorkoutId null, `history` (1128) includes the session just finished and it renders first in the History list at 1291-1298, showing name, date, set count, duration and total volume (927-962) — and expanding it shows every exercise grouped as '3 × 8 @ 100 kg' (986-1024). What is genuinely missing is a confirm, a resume path, and any surfacing of PRs hit this session. That is P2 friction.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Rest timer only alerts while the app is foregrounded — backgrounded, the buzz arrives late, when you reopen it</b> — <code>src/components/RestTimer.tsx:57</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The only completion signal is `if (left <= 0 && !firedRef.current) { firedRef.current = true; void Haptics.notificationAsync(...) }` inside a `setInterval` running at 250ms (lines 52-63). That interval is suspended when the app is backgrounded or the device locks, and `expo-haptics` produces nothing when the app is not foregrounded. `package.json` lists no `expo-notifications` and no audio package, and there is no `AppState` listener in this file.

**User impact.** The intended use is: tick the set, put the phone down or switch to a podcast, come back when it buzzes. It never buzzes. The user either stares at the screen for 120 seconds or loses track of rest entirely, then returns to a timer that has silently read 0:00 for three minutes. The countdown value itself survives (it is deadline-based, line 46), but the alert does not.

**Fix.** Add `expo-notifications` and schedule a local notification (with sound) for the rest deadline when the timer starts, cancelling it on dismiss/adjust/next set. Keep the haptic for the foreground case. Optionally fire the haptic on `AppState` returning to 'active' if the deadline passed while backgrounded.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Facts verified: src/components/RestTimer.tsx:52-63 — the only completion signal is `Haptics.notificationAsync(Success)` inside a 250ms setInterval; there is no AppState listener in the file; package.json (lines 5-40) has no expo-notifications and no audio package, and app.json's plugins array lists only expo-build-properties, expo-router, expo-font, expo-image-picker, expo-splash-screen and react-native-health-connect. So a backgrounded/locked device gets no alert. One correction to the impact: because the countdown is deadline-based (line 46) and firedRef is still false, the success haptic DOES fire on the next interval tick once the app returns to the foreground — the user gets a late buzz, not silence forever. Severity: this is a missing capability on an assistive control; the workout is still completable and the elapsed number is still correct. P2, not P1.

</details>

<details><summary><b>expo-keep-awake is absent — the screen locks during a set and during rest</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:632</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `package.json` dependencies (lines 5-40) contain no `expo-keep-awake`, and `grep -rn "KeepAwake|keep-awake" src app` returns zero hits. `ActiveWorkout` (line 632) mounts for the whole session with no wake lock. The default iOS auto-lock is 30s-2min and the rest timer's default is `DEFAULT_REST_SECONDS = 120` (src/components/RestTimer.tsx:12).

**User impact.** The phone is on the floor between sets. Rest is 120 seconds; auto-lock is 60. The user picks the phone up mid-rest to a black screen, unlocks with sweaty fingers or Face ID at a bad angle, and lands back on the workout tab having lost sight of the timer. Same failure between the weight input and the Done tap if they are slow.

**Fix.** Add `expo-keep-awake` and call `useKeepAwake()` inside `ActiveWorkout` (so the lock is held only while a session is live, not on the history view). Gate it behind nothing — it is scoped by the component's mount lifetime.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified: package.json dependencies (5-40) contain no expo-keep-awake, app.json's plugin list has no keep-awake entry, and no useKeepAwake / KeepAwake reference exists anywhere in app/ or src/. ActiveWorkout (line 632) holds no wake lock for the life of the session, and DEFAULT_REST_SECONDS is 120 (RestTimer.tsx:12), longer than common auto-lock settings. The problem is real and the one-line fix (useKeepAwake inside ActiveWorkout) is correct. Severity is the issue: the screen locking is an OS behaviour the user already lives with everywhere, nothing is lost, and the session and deadline both survive (RestTimer is deadline-based, line 46). Unlocking to check the timer is noticeable friction, not a materially broken task.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Personal records and progression targets are recomputed over the entire workout history on every keystroke</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:675</code> · performance · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `priorRecords` has deps `[workoutLog, session.id]` (lines 675-681) and calls `getPersonalRecords(...)`, which walks every session, exercise and set (src/core/utils/workoutMath.ts:121-181). `suggestions` has deps `[exercises, workoutLog]` (lines 702-710) and calls `suggestNextSet` per exercise, each of which runs `getLiftHistory` → `.filter().slice().sort(compareNewestFirst)` over the whole log (workoutMath.ts:98-105, 192). Immer produces a new `workoutLog` array reference on every `updateSet`, so both memos invalidate on every character typed.

**User impact.** For a user a year into the app (≈150 sessions) with 8 exercises open, one keystroke triggers a full PR scan plus 8 sorts of 150 sessions before React can paint. This compounds with the AsyncStorage write above into visible input lag in exactly the interaction that has to feel instant.

**Fix.** Key both memos off something that does not change while typing — e.g. memoise on `workoutLog.length` plus a `completedSetsSignature` derived from ids of completed sets, or lift `priorRecords`/`suggestions` into a selector computed once when the session opens and refreshed only on `completed` transitions.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** priorRecords deps are `[workoutLog, session.id]` (workout.tsx:675-681) and it calls getPersonalRecords, which walks every session/exercise/set (workoutMath.ts:121-181). suggestions deps are `[exercises, workoutLog]` (702-710) and call suggestNextSet per exercise, each running getLiftHistory → filter + slice + sort over the entire log (workoutMath.ts:98-105, 192). Immer replaces the workoutLog array reference on every updateSet, and `exercises` (667) is re-derived from the new session object, so both memos plus prSetIds (684-700) invalidate on every character. Not mentioned but the same class: `records` at line 1131 runs getPersonalRecords over the full log again for the PR table. P2 is right — it is real work per keystroke that compounds with the persist write, but on a small log it is invisible.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Adding 30 seconds after the rest timer has finished does nothing but re-buzz</b> — <code>src/components/RestTimer.tsx:68</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `adjust` does `deadlineRef.current += delta * 1000` and `firedRef.current = false` (lines 68-74). Once the deadline has passed, e.g. by 90s, `+30` moves it to 60s still in the past, so `setRemaining(Math.max(0, negative))` stays 0 and the display stays '0:00 / Rest complete'. The 250ms interval then immediately sees `left <= 0 && !firedRef.current` and fires `Haptics.notificationAsync(Success)` again (lines 57-60).

**User impact.** The user finishes rest, decides they need another 30 seconds, taps +30 and gets a completion buzz with the clock still frozen at 0:00 and the bar still full. The control reads as broken and there is no way to extend a finished rest short of ticking another set.

**Fix.** In `adjust`, rebase from now when the deadline has already passed: `deadlineRef.current = Math.max(deadlineRef.current, Date.now()) + delta * 1000`, and only clear `firedRef` when the resulting remaining time is > 0.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** src/components/RestTimer.tsx:68-74 — `deadlineRef.current += delta * 1000` is a relative shift, so if the deadline is already 90s in the past, +30 leaves it 60s in the past; line 72's `setRemaining(Math.max(0, ...))` clamps to 0 and the display stays '0:00' / 'Rest complete' (line 90) with the ProgressTrack at `1 - 0/target` = full (109-113). And because line 73 clears firedRef, the interval at 57-60 immediately re-fires `Haptics.notificationAsync(Success)`. So the control does nothing except buzz. The proposed rebase-from-now fix is correct. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Exercises cannot be reordered or swapped mid-workout — only deleted and re-added, losing the sets</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:789</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `exercises.map(exercise => <ExerciseCard ... />)` (lines 789-816) renders in raw insertion order. The only mutations offered are `onAddSet`, `onRemoveSet` and `onRemoveExercise`; there is no drag handle, no move up/down, and no swap. The store exposes no reorder action either — src/core/store/appState.ts:117-122 has `addExerciseToWorkout` / `removeExerciseFromWorkout` and nothing else.

**User impact.** The squat rack is taken so the user does rows first — but rows are now permanently card #4 and they scroll past three untouched cards for the whole session. Wanting dumbbell press instead of barbell press after two sets means deleting the exercise (destroying those sets, with no confirm per remove-exercise-no-confirm) and starting the lift over.

**Fix.** Add up/down 44pt controls to the exercise card header (cheapest, works one-handed) or drag-to-reorder via react-native-gesture-handler, backed by a `reorderExercises(sessionId, from, to)` action — an upstream change to the web repo's src/core/store/appState.ts. A `swapExerciseLift(sessionId, exerciseId, lift)` action would cover the substitution case while keeping logged sets.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** workout.tsx:789-816 maps `exercises` in raw insertion order and passes only onChangeSet, onRemoveSet, onAddSet and onRemoveExercise — no drag handle, no move up/down, no swap anywhere in the card header (480-537). The store confirms there is nothing to call: the AppState interface at src/core/store/appState.ts:117-122 lists addExerciseToWorkout / removeExerciseFromWorkout / addSet / updateSet / removeSet / deleteWorkout and no reorder or swap. addExerciseToWorkout pushes to the end (appState.ts:521), so a lift done out of order is stuck at the bottom. Substituting a lift means removing the exercise, which destroys its sets with no confirm (see remove-exercise-no-confirm). P2 is right, and flagging reorderExercises/swapExerciseLift as an upstream web-repo change is the correct framing.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The lift picker closes after every single selection — a 6-exercise session is 6 modal round trips</b> — <code>app/lift-picker.tsx:185</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `handleSelect` is `if (sessionId) { addExerciseToWorkout(sessionId, lift); void Haptics.impactAsync(...) } router.back()` (lines 185-191) — `router.back()` runs unconditionally on every tap, including from the 'Your lifts' header rows (line 480) and from `handleCreate` (line 205). There is no multi-select state and no 'Done' affordance.

**User impact.** Planning a session up front means: tap Add exercise → modal slides up → tap lift → modal slides down → find the Add exercise button again → repeat, six times, with the search query and filter chips reset every trip. Users who know their whole session in advance pay 30+ taps and 12 modal animations to set it up.

**Fix.** Make selection additive: keep the modal open, mark chosen lifts with a checkmark and a running count, and add a primary 'Add 3 exercises' button in the header that calls `router.back()` once. Retain the current tap-to-add-and-close behaviour only when the picker was opened from the empty-state 'Add exercise' button, or offer both via a long-press.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/lift-picker.tsx:185-191 — handleSelect adds the lift then calls `router.back()` unconditionally, on every path: the FlatList rows (465), the 'Your lifts' header rows (476-482), and handleCreate (205). There is no multi-select state, no running count, no Done affordance anywhere in the file. Query, muscle chip and equipment chip are all component-local useState (135-137), so they reset on every remount. Planning a 6-lift session really is 6 push/pop cycles with the filters cleared each time. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Your six most-used lifts are listed twice on the picker's default screen</b> — <code>app/lift-picker.tsx:466</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `ListHeaderComponent` renders `frequentLifts` under a 'Your lifts' label whenever `!filtersActive && frequentLifts.length > 0` (lines 466-488). `data={results}` is `[...custom, ...searchLifts(query, LIFT_DATABASE.length)]` (lines 166-183) — with no query and no filters that is the entire 90-lift catalogue plus all custom lifts, which necessarily contains the same six lifts. They are only distinguished by a `frequent-${lift.id}` key prefix (line 478).

**User impact.** Opening the picker shows Bench Press, Back Squat and Deadlift at the top, then the same three again ~20 rows down under 'All lifts'. The user scrolling for a lift they just saw cannot tell whether they are looking at a shortcut or the real entry, and reads the list as buggy.

**Fix.** Exclude the frequent ids from `results` when the header is shown: `results.filter(l => !frequentIds.has(l.id))` guarded by the same `!filtersActive && frequentLifts.length > 0` condition used at line 467.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified including the part that makes it certain. The header renders frequentLifts whenever `!filtersActive && frequentLifts.length > 0` (lift-picker.tsx:466-488). `results` (166-183) is `[...custom, ...searchLifts(query, LIFT_DATABASE.length)]`, and src/core/data/exerciseDatabase.ts:165 shows searchLifts returns `LIFT_DATABASE.slice(0, limit)` for an empty needle — with limit = LIFT_DATABASE.length that is the entire catalogue. frequentLifts are drawn from workoutLog exercises (149-164), which are either presets (in LIFT_DATABASE) or custom lifts (already prepended to results), so every frequent lift is necessarily listed a second time. Only the `frequent-${lift.id}` key prefix (478) keeps React quiet. The suggested filter on frequentIds is the right fix. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Tapping the set number silently converts a working set to a warmup — nothing says so</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:262</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The set-number cell is a 44pt Pressable whose `onPress` is `() => onChange({ isWarmup: !set.isWarmup })` (lines 262-283); the only visual is the digit becoming 'W' (line 281). The column header reads simply `<Label>Set</Label>` (line 569). The comment at line 261 explains the intent but no on-screen copy does. Warmup sets are excluded from volume, set count and PRs by `isWorkingSet` (src/core/utils/workoutMath.ts:34-35).

**User impact.** Two failures. Nobody discovers warmup marking, so users either log warmups as working sets (inflating volume and polluting the progression target via `suggestNextSet`) or skip logging them. And the reverse: the leftmost 44pt column of every row is a live toggle in the zone a thumb rests while scrolling — one accidental tap turns a real top set into a 'W' that stops counting toward volume and PRs, with no undo prompt and no explanation of what changed.

**Fix.** Label the column 'Set / W' or add a one-line hint under the header ('Tap the number to mark a warmup'), and give the W state a visible chip rather than a single glyph. Consider requiring a long-press to toggle so a stray tap cannot silently remove a set from volume.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** workout.tsx:262-283 — the set-number cell is a 44pt Pressable whose onPress is `() => onChange({ isWarmup: !set.isWarmup })`, and the only visible consequence is the digit rendering as 'W' (281). The column header is a bare `<Label>Set</Label>` (569) and no on-screen copy mentions warmups; only the accessibilityLabel (265-269) explains it, which sighted users never hear. The stake is confirmed: isWorkingSet (workoutMath.ts:34-35) excludes warmups from volume, sessionSetCount and getPersonalRecords, and from suggestNextSet's progression basis. Discoverability failure with a real data consequence. One overstatement worth noting rather than adjusting for: a thumb *resting* during a scroll does not fire onPress (the touch is cancelled by the scroll gesture), so the accidental-toggle-while-scrolling path needs a genuine tap. The primary claim — nobody discovers it, and a stray tap silently removes a set from volume and PRs with no undo — holds. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The full personal-records table and complete workout history render below the live session</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1227</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `{records.length > 0 && ...}` (lines 1227-1289) and `{history.length > 0 && ...}` (lines 1291-1298) are gated only on content, not on `activeSession`. Only the 'Ready to train?' card (line 1173) and the templates row (line 1190) check `activeSession === null`. So during a live session the scroll order is: glass hero → rest timer → exercise cards → Add exercise → Cancel workout → every PR → every past session.

**User impact.** Mid-workout the screen is dominated by a growing tail of content the user has no use for while training, and the destructive 'Cancel workout' button is buried in the middle of it rather than at the end. Every one of those `HistoryCard`s also re-renders on each keystroke in a weight cell (see the memo/persist findings), compounding the input lag.

**Fix.** Gate both sections on `activeSession === null`, the same way the start card and templates already are. If history must stay reachable mid-session, put it behind a collapsed 'Past workouts' disclosure below Cancel rather than rendering it inline.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Gating verified: the PR block at 1227 is `{records.length > 0 && ...}` and the History block at 1291 is `{history.length > 0 && ...}` — neither checks activeSession, while the start card (1173) and Quick start (1190) both do. `history` at 1127-1130 is workoutLog minus the active id, i.e. every past session, so mid-workout the scroll order really is hero → rest timer → exercise cards → Add exercise → Cancel workout → full PR table → every past session. The compounding perf claim also checks out: HistoryCard is not memoized and each one recomputes sessionSetCount (908) and sessionVolume (960) on every parent render, which happens on every keystroke. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>A saved workout template can never be renamed or deleted</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1190</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The Quick start row renders `workoutTemplates.map(...)` with a single `onPress={() => applyWorkoutTemplate(template.id, getTodayString())}` (lines 1198-1222) — no long-press, no edit, no delete. The store defines `saveWorkoutTemplate` and `applyWorkoutTemplate` only (src/core/store/appState.ts:124-125, 564-601); there is a `deleteMealTemplate` (line 68) but no workout equivalent anywhere in the interface.

**User impact.** A template saved with a typo, or one built from a session the user later restructured, sits in the Quick start row permanently. Because tapping it immediately starts a workout (there is no confirm), a stale template is also a live hazard: a mis-tap ends any in-progress session (`applyWorkoutTemplate` closes the previous one at appState.ts:593-594) and starts a workout the user did not want.

**Fix.** Add deleteWorkoutTemplate(id) and renameWorkoutTemplate(id, name) upstream in the web repo's src/core/store/appState.ts, then surface them from a long-press on the chip at workout.tsx:1199 using the two-tap confirm pattern already in the file. Drop the 'mis-tap ends an in-progress session' rationale — the section is hidden during a session.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The gap is real: the Quick start chips (1198-1222) have a single onPress and no long-press, edit or delete, and the AppState interface (src/core/store/appState.ts:124-125) defines saveWorkoutTemplate and applyWorkoutTemplate only — there is a deleteMealTemplate at line 68 but no workout equivalent anywhere, so a template is permanent. But the 'live hazard' half is refuted by the code: the whole Quick start section is wrapped in `{activeSession === null && workoutTemplates.length > 0 && ...}` (line 1190), so the chips do not exist while a session is in progress and cannot end one. The residual mis-tap only starts an unwanted empty session, which Cancel workout deletes outright (appState.ts:504-516, no completed sets → filtered out).

</details>

<details><summary><b>A workout you forget to finish stays 'Live' for days, and everything you log afterwards is filed under the old date</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1126</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const activeSession = workoutLog.find(session => session.id === activeWorkoutId) ?? null` (line 1126) has no staleness check against session.date or session.startedAt. activeWorkoutId is persisted (src/store/useStore.ts:24-32) and is in the cloud sync list (appState.ts:611), and the only things that ever clear it are endWorkout (498-502), cancelWorkout (504-516), startWorkout (486-494) and applyWorkoutTemplate (592-599) — all user-initiated. formatElapsed (workout.tsx:90-98) already has an hours branch (`H:MM:SS`) so it happily renders 26:41:07.

**User impact.** User starts a session Monday evening, gets distracted, never taps Finish. Tuesday the workout tab still shows the glass hero with a green 'Live' pill and an elapsed clock reading 20+ hours. Every set they add on Tuesday is appended to the Monday session, so session.date stays '2026-07-27': the workout shows up on the wrong day in History, and weeklyVolumeByMuscle (workoutMath.ts:242-287, which buckets strictly by session.date) attributes Tuesday's volume to Monday. There is no prompt anywhere offering to close it.

**Fix.** In WorkoutScreen, when activeSession.date !== getTodayString(), render a banner above the hero — 'This session was started on {formatDate(session.date)}. Finish it or start a new one.' — with Finish and Start new actions, using the endWorkout/startWorkout already wired at 1120-1121. Purely local to app/(tabs)/workout.tsx.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Tapping Done before entering reps does nothing at all — no message, no hint, just a dead 44pt target</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:330</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `disabled={!canComplete && !set.completed}` (line 330) with `canComplete = set.reps > 0` (246). A disabled Pressable swallows the touch entirely, so onPress never runs and no state changes. The only cues are `opacity: !canComplete && !settled ? 0.4 : 1` (341) and an accessibilityHint of 'Enter reps first' (329) plus the accessibilityLabel at 322-328 — both invisible to a sighted user. Nothing else on the card says reps are mandatory: the empty-state copy at 777 only says 'Every set is weight × reps, ticked off as you finish it.'

**User impact.** The user types their weight, reaches for the green tick, and the app does nothing — no flash, no toast, no text. There is no way to tell a broken button from a rule they have not been told about. Combined with complete-set-with-zero-weight (where the mirror case — reps but no weight — silently succeeds and logs 0 kg), the gate is both invisible and inconsistent about which field it protects.

**Fix.** Keep the Pressable enabled and, on press with reps === 0, focus the reps input and show the existing hint text inline under the row (the confirmRemove branch at 397-413 is the pattern already in the file). Or gate on `reps > 0 || weightKg > 0` and commit the placeholder for whichever cell is blank.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Every workout ever logged is mounted at once — the tab gets slower with each session and there is no cap</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1291</code> · performance · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `{history.map(session => <HistoryCard key={session.id} session={session} unit={unit} />)}` (1294-1296) inside Screen's plain ScrollView (src/components/Layout.tsx:100-109) — no FlatList, no windowing, no slice. `history` (1127-1130) is the entire workoutLog minus the active session. Contrast the PR table right above it, which caps at PR_PREVIEW_COUNT = 5 (1112, 1132) behind a 'Show all' toggle. Each HistoryCard is unmemoized and calls sessionSetCount(session) (908) and sessionVolume(session) (960) on every render, each of which walks every exercise and set (workoutMath.ts:84-92).

**User impact.** A year in at three sessions a week, opening the Workout tab mounts ~150 cards and walks every set in the entire training history twice before the first frame. Because workoutLog gets a new reference on every updateSet, all 150 re-render and re-walk on every character typed into a weight cell — the same keystroke that is already paying for a full-store AsyncStorage write and a full getPersonalRecords scan. The screen degrades permanently as the user succeeds at using it.

**Fix.** Cap history the way records already are — `history.slice(0, 10)` with a 'Show all N workouts' ghost button mirroring lines 1278-1287 — and wrap HistoryCard in React.memo. A FlatList is the fuller fix but requires Screen to give up its ScrollView.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Workouts can only be logged for today — a session you forgot to record yesterday is unrecordable</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1137</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Both entry points hard-code today: `startWorkout(defaultWorkoutName(), getTodayString())` (line 1137) and `applyWorkoutTemplate(template.id, getTodayString())` (line 1203). session.date is set once in the store (src/core/store/appState.ts:479-485, 579-585) and nothing in workout.tsx or the AppState interface (114-125) can change it afterwards — the only editable field on a live session is the name (658-665). HistoryCard renders `formatDate(session.date)` as static text (934).

**User impact.** The diary tab lets a user pick any date; the workout tab does not. Someone who trains and logs on paper, or who finishes at 11:50pm and logs at 12:10am, has no way to file the session on the right day — it lands on today, which double-counts one day and leaves the real one blank in History and in weeklyVolumeByMuscle (workoutMath.ts:242-287, bucketed strictly on session.date).

**Fix.** Add a date row to the 'Ready to train?' card (workout.tsx:1173-1188) defaulting to getTodayString() and passed into startWorkout/applyWorkoutTemplate — both already take the date as a parameter, so no upstream change is required. Editing the date of a finished session needs the same edit surface as the read-only-history finding above.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (5)

<details><summary><b>The rest timer's first appearance pushes the exercise cards down ~80pt (once per session, and again after each manual dismiss)</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:770</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** RestTimer returns `null` while `triggerKey === 0` (src/components/RestTimer.tsx:65) and renders a full block once a working set completes. It sits between the hero and the exercise list (line 770) inside a `gap: spacing.lg` (16) column (src/components/Layout.tsx:48), so the list below jumps by the timer's height (~66pt) plus the gap. Dismissing it (`onDismiss={() => setRestKey(0)}`) jumps everything back.

**User impact.** Tap Done on set 2, and while the thumb is still travelling toward set 3's weight cell the whole grid slides down ~80pt — the row under the finger is now a different one. This is the classic mis-tap generator on a screen the brief describes as used one-handed and sweaty, and it repeats after literally every set.

**Fix.** Pinning the timer as a floating bar (see rest-timer-scrolls-out-of-view) removes the shift entirely. If it must stay inline, reserve its height with a fixed-height placeholder so the layout never reflows, and animate opacity only (honouring `useReducedMotion()` per docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md).

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The mechanism is verified — RestTimer returns null while triggerKey === 0 (RestTimer.tsx:65), it is rendered inline at workout.tsx:770 inside a `gap: spacing.lg` column (Layout.tsx:48), and its block is roughly 80pt (padding md ×2 + a 44pt IconButton row + gap + 5pt track), so everything below it jumps. But the frequency claim is wrong, and it is the whole argument. restKey only goes 0 → 1 once; every subsequent completed working set increments 1 → 2 → 3 (workout.tsx:801-803) with the timer already mounted, so there is NO layout shift. The jump happens once per session — on the first completed working set — and again only if the user taps the X to dismiss (onDismiss sets restKey back to 0, line 770) and then completes another set. A once-per-session 80pt shift is a nit, not P2 friction repeated after every set.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The custom-lift form does not dismiss the keyboard on drag, unlike the results list it replaces</b> — <code>app/lift-picker.tsx:351</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The `creating` branch is a plain `<ScrollView keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled" contentContainerStyle={listPadding}>` (lines 351-355) with no `KeyboardAvoidingView` and no keyboard inset. Between the name `Field` (line 360) and the 'Create and add' button (line 453) sit 11 muscle chips, 7 equipment chips, a compound checkbox and a help paragraph — the buttons are far below the fold, and `listPadding.paddingBottom` is `insets.bottom + spacing.xxl` = ~66pt against a ~300pt alphabetic keyboard.

**User impact.** The user types the name of their custom lift, the keyboard is up, and the only way to finish is to dismiss the keyboard first — but the number of taps to find a blank spot in a form that is nothing but chips is non-obvious, and dragging does not dismiss (`keyboardDismissMode` is unset on this ScrollView, unlike the results list at line 462).

**Fix.** Add `keyboardDismissMode="on-drag"` to the creating-branch ScrollView at lift-picker.tsx:351 so it matches the FlatList at 462. No KeyboardAvoidingView is needed — the name field is above the keyboard and keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled" already makes the buttons tappable.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Mostly refuted on impact. The structural claim is right — lift-picker.tsx:351-355 is a bare ScrollView with no KeyboardAvoidingView and no keyboardDismissMode, and the buttons at 446-454 sit below 11 muscle chips, 7 equipment chips, a checkbox and a help paragraph. But the stated impact does not follow. (a) The field being typed into is the Name field at the very top of the form (360-369) — the keyboard never covers it. (b) The ScrollView sets `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"` (352), which means 'Create and add' is tappable in ONE tap with the keyboard still up; the assertion that 'the only way to finish is to dismiss the keyboard first' is wrong. (c) The form scrolls normally with the keyboard up, so nothing is unreachable. What is genuinely missing is only `keyboardDismissMode="on-drag"`, which the results list two branches away does have (462) — an inconsistency, not a broken flow.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Adding an exercise gives no visible confirmation — the new card appears below the fold</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:712</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `openPicker` pushes the modal (lines 712-714); the picker writes the exercise into the session and calls `router.back()` (app/lift-picker.tsx:185-191); `addExerciseToWorkout` does `session.exercises.push(...)` (src/core/store/appState.ts:521), appending to the end. `WorkoutScreen` holds no ScrollView ref and never scrolls, so the user returns at their previous scroll offset.

**User impact.** With four exercises already open, adding a fifth returns the user to the same view they left, with the new card off-screen below. Nothing on screen changed, so the natural reaction is to tap 'Add exercise' again and add it twice.

**Fix.** Hold a ref on `Screen`'s ScrollView (expose it via a prop) and `scrollToEnd({ animated: true })` when `exercises.length` grows, or briefly highlight the newly added card. Both should skip the animation under `useReducedMotion()`.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** openPicker (712-714) pushes the modal; lift-picker.tsx:185-191 writes the exercise and calls router.back(); appState.ts:521 pushes it onto the end of session.exercises. WorkoutScreen holds no ScrollView ref and never calls scrollTo — Screen (src/components/Layout.tsx:100) does not expose one — so the user returns at the same offset with the new card below the fold and nothing visibly changed. P3 is the right weight: recoverable by scrolling, and the double-add it invites is itself removable.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The rest length resets to 120s on every app launch and is shared across every lift</b> — <code>src/components/RestTimer.tsx:38</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const [target, setTarget] = useState(DEFAULT_REST_SECONDS)` (line 38, `DEFAULT_REST_SECONDS = 120` at line 12) is component-local React state. `adjust` mutates it (lines 68-74) but nothing writes it to the store, and `RestTimer` is mounted per `ActiveWorkout`, so the value is lost when the session ends or the app is killed. There is no per-lift rest field on `WorkoutExercise` (src/core/types/index.ts:275-281).

**User impact.** A user who rests 3 minutes on squats and 60 seconds on curls has to re-tap the ± buttons on every single set, and re-tap them again after every app restart. The timer never learns anything.

**Fix.** Persist the last-used rest length in the store (a `restSeconds` field on the profile, or `restSeconds?: number` on `WorkoutExercise` for per-lift defaults) — an upstream change to the web repo's src/core/store/appState.ts and types — and seed `target` from it.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** src/components/RestTimer.tsx:38 `const [target, setTarget] = useState(DEFAULT_REST_SECONDS)` with DEFAULT_REST_SECONDS = 120 (line 12); adjust (68-74) is the only writer and writes nowhere but component state. RestTimer is mounted inside ActiveWorkout (workout.tsx:770), so the value does survive across sets within one session (the component returns null at line 65 but stays mounted) — it is lost when the session ends or the app restarts, exactly as claimed. WorkoutExercise carries no rest field (src/core/types/index.ts:275-281: id, liftId, lift, sets, notes). P3, and correctly flagged as an upstream types/store change.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The rest timer's − button cuts 30s off the clock even when it refuses to lower the target, ending rest instantly</b> — <code>src/components/RestTimer.tsx:68</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** adjust() at 68-74: `const next = Math.max(STEP_SECONDS, target + delta); setTarget(next); deadlineRef.current += delta * 1000`. The clamp guards `target` only — the deadline shift is applied unconditionally. With target already at the 30s floor, pressing − leaves target at 30 but moves the deadline 30s earlier, so remaining clamps to 0 (line 72), the label flips to 'Rest complete' (90), the ProgressTrack renders full at `1 - 0/30` (110), and firedRef is cleared at 73 so the interval at 57-60 fires the success haptic.

**User impact.** At the shortest rest setting the − button silently behaves as 'skip rest' and buzzes as if the rest had elapsed, while the target it displays says nothing changed. The displayed target and the actual countdown have diverged and there is no way to get back in sync short of completing another set.

**Fix.** Apply the same clamp to the deadline: compute `const applied = next - target` and use `deadlineRef.current = Math.max(deadlineRef.current, Date.now()) + applied * 1000`, then only clear firedRef when the resulting remaining time is > 0. That single change also fixes the +30-after-completion case.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

### Progress & goals

#### P1 (4)

<details><summary><b>Weight tab claims "No weigh-ins yet" for users with months of weigh-ins</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:983</code> · empty-error-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** weightPoints (736-742) keeps only entries whose date is in the selected window (`indexByDate.has(w.date)` where indexByDate comes from getLast7Days/getLast30Days). Line 983 branches on `weightPoints.length === 0` and renders EmptyState title "No weigh-ins yet" / "Log your weight from the Profile tab." The Summary tiles below (1015-1023) then read "No data yet" for Latest and Entries.

**User impact.** Someone who has weighed in 200 times but last stepped on the scale nine days ago opens Progress → Weight on the default 7d range and is told they have never weighed in. Their real, populated weight history is one tap away on the 30d control but nothing says so, so the screen reads as data loss.

**Fix.** Distinguish "no weigh-ins at all" (weightLog.length === 0) from "none in this window". For the latter, keep the Summary tiles populated from the most recent entry and render a message naming it — "Nothing in the last 7 days. Your last weigh-in was 82.4 kg on 18 Jul." — with a control that switches range to 30d.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:736-742 filters weightLog to `indexByDate.has(w.date)` over getLast7Days/getLast30Days; 983 branches on `weightPoints.length === 0` and 986-987 renders `No weigh-ins yet` / `Log your weight from the Profile tab.` The Summary tiles at 1015-1023 pass null and fall through to StatTile's `fallback = 'No data yet'` (554). Nothing anywhere consults `weightLog.length`, so a 200-entry log with nothing in the last 7 days reads as empty.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Goals has no weight goal, and the pace every ETA is projected from can never be edited after setup</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:425</code> · information-architecture · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `<Screen title="Goals" subtitle="Your coach plan and your own numbers">` contains only calorie/macro fields. `profile.targetWeightKg` and `profile.targetRateKgPerWeek` are set in onboarding (onboarding.ts:268-269) and are afterwards editable only in profile.tsx:411-435 ("Targets" section, collapsed by default once a target exists). weightTarget.ts:160 and 208 read both, and everything the WeightTargetCard renders — remaining, fraction, status, ETA — is derived from them.

**User impact.** A user who decides to aim for 78 kg instead of 80 kg opens the screen literally named "Goals", finds calories and macro grams, and leaves without it. The goal weight is buried inside a collapsed accordion on Profile between "Health Connect" and "About you".

**Fix.** Add a "Weight goal" section to goals.tsx with goal weight (display unit → kg on commit, as profile.tsx:260 does) AND a pace selector writing profile.targetRateKgPerWeek via signedRate (onboarding.ts:94-97) — the latter has no editor anywhere today. Then make profile.tsx:411-435 a link to /goals rather than a second editor.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The core claim holds — goals.tsx contains only calorie/macro/fiber fields, and weightTarget.ts:160/208 derives remaining, fraction, status and ETA from profile.targetWeightKg and profile.targetRateKgPerWeek. But the auditor's account of where they live is wrong. I grepped the whole app: targetRateKgPerWeek is written in exactly one place, onboarding.ts:269 via answersToProfile, and appears in no editor afterwards — profile.tsx:411-435 exposes ONLY goal weight (Field at 421-430, commitTargetWeight at 252-262). So the pace that drives every projected ETA is unreachable for the life of the account, which is worse than the finding says.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Weight tab's Google Fit import overwrites manual weigh-ins and silently discards the body composition it advertises</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:672</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The card header (1074-1077) promises "Sync weight, height & body composition historical records via Health Connect" and the preview lists "Body Fat: X%" (1090). handleConfirmImport (672-689) writes only `weights` and `latestHeightCm`; `healthSyncResult.bodyFatMeasurements`, built at health.sync.ts:46-64, is never written to the store. Separately, appState.ts:321 does `state.weightLog = [entry, ...state.weightLog.filter(w => w.date !== entryData.date)]`, so an imported device reading replaces a manual weigh-in on the same day — the exact opposite of the sibling implementation in useHealthSync.ts:92-95, which comments "Existing days win" and filters them out.

**User impact.** The user is shown "Found 412 historical entries" and "Body Fat: 18.2%", confirms, and gets only weigh-ins — with their own hand-entered scale readings replaced by whatever their phone recorded that day. Running the Profile import and the Progress import produces different results from the same source data.

**Fix.** Make handleConfirmImport filter out dates already in weightLog exactly as useHealthSync.ts:93-94 does, and either import bodyFatMeasurements via addBodyMeasurement or remove the body-composition claims from the header and preview. Better: delete the src/lib/health stack from progress.tsx and reuse `useHealthSync().importWeightHistory()` so one implementation serves both screens.
