**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Both halves check out. handleConfirmImport (progress.tsx:672-689) writes only `weights` and `latestHeightCm`; `bodyFatMeasurements`, built at health.sync.ts:46-64 and typed into HealthRecordNormalized (health.types.ts:9), is never written, while the header (1076) promises "body composition" and the preview (1090) prints "Body Fat: X%". Separately appState.ts:321 `state.weightLog = [entry, ...state.weightLog.filter(w => w.date !== entryData.date)]` replaces a same-day manual entry, and useHealthSync.ts:92-95 explicitly guards against exactly that ("Existing days win") — so the two import paths in one app behave differently on the same data.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Editing any Profile basic silently overwrites the accepted coach plan and every manual target</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:249</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** commitBasics (profile.tsx:238-250) ends in `recalculateGoals()`, and it is wired to the onBlur of the Name, Age and Height fields (442, 450, 458). The same call fires on every Goal, Activity level and Gender change (477, 490, 504). appState.ts:226-239 then recomputes calories from calculateBMR/calculateTDEE/calculateCalorieGoal and does `Object.assign(state.goals, macros)` with the full calculateMacroGoals result — calories, protein, carbs, fat, fiber, sugar, sodium and water (calculations.ts:36-45). Nothing checks whether the current goals came from an accepted coach plan or from the manual form. goals.tsx:701-704 promises the opposite: "your numbers win until you accept a new plan."

**User impact.** A user accepts a coach plan (2,150 kcal / 180 P) or hand-tunes their targets, then goes to Profile, taps the Name field to fix a typo and taps away. Their calorie and macro targets are silently replaced by the flat Mifflin ±500 formula. Nothing on screen says anything happened, and back on Goals the button has quietly flipped from "Plan accepted" to "Accept plan" because useCoach.ts:195-200 no longer sees a match.

**Fix.** Drop the unconditional recalculateGoals() from commitBasics and the three Choice handlers. Either recompute nothing and let /goals own the targets, or surface it as an explicit prompt ("Your body details changed — recalculate targets from the formula?") that names the numbers it would replace.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P2 (20)

<details><summary><b>The Weight tab sends you to another tab to log a weigh-in</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:987</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The empty state message is `Log your weight from the Profile tab. Two entries in the ${rangeWords} draw a trend.` The weigh-in field lives only in WeightTarget.tsx:204-227, which is mounted on the Dashboard (index.tsx:173, `<WeightTargetCard compact />`) and on Profile (profile.tsx:358). progress.tsx never renders WeightTargetCard and has no Field/Button for weight.

**User impact.** A user opens Progress → Weight to check whether they are losing, sees a flat or empty chart, and has to leave the tab, find Profile (or scroll the Dashboard), log, and navigate back. The screen also names the wrong destination first — the Dashboard card is closer and is what most users will have already seen.

**Fix.** Render `<WeightTargetCard compact />` at the top of the weight tab in progress.tsx, or at minimum add an inline weigh-in Field + Log button next to the chart. Then correct the empty-state copy to point at the control on the same screen.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code claim is accurate: progress.tsx renders no WeightTargetCard and no weight Field/Button anywhere (the only weigh-in control is WeightTarget.tsx:204-227, mounted at index.tsx:173 and profile.tsx:358), and the empty-state copy at 987 points at Profile. But the destination it names is real and works, and the Dashboard card is one tab away — this is navigation friction, not a broken or dead-ended flow. Demoting.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The manual card's "TDEE — kcal you burn" cell is an unlabelled second burn figure that disagrees with the coach's</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:344</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** goals.tsx:344-348 computes `formula = { bmr: calculateBMR(profile, currentWeightKg), tdee: calculateTDEE(bmr, activityLevel), suggested: calculateCalorieGoal(...) }` — always Mifflin-St Jeor (calculations.ts:3-10). It is rendered at line 725 ("Your profile puts your burn at 2,450 kcal a day") and line 755-757 (FigureCell "TDEE / kcal you burn"). The card two sections above renders `coach.tdee.predicted` (line 641-645) which comes from tdee.ts:89-94 and uses Katch-McArdle whenever `bodyComp.leanMassKg > 0`, captioned "Katch-McArdle, from your lean mass".

**User impact.** Any user who has entered neck and waist measurements (profile.tsx:589+) sees two different burn figures on one scroll — e.g. "Predicted 2,310 · Katch-McArdle" in the burn card and "TDEE 2,450 · kcal you burn" in the manual card — and the three Cut/Maintain/Bulk presets are anchored to the number the coach itself considers less accurate. There is nothing on screen explaining the gap, so the user cannot tell which one to trust.

**Fix.** Delete the local `formula` memo and drive the preset anchors and the BMR/TDEE/Suggested row from `coach.tdee` (predicted, plus `coach.anchorTdee` for the anchor), so one number governs the whole screen. If a Mifflin figure is genuinely wanted for comparison, label it "Formula only (ignores your measurements)".

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Both numbers are real and can differ: goals.tsx:344-348 always uses calculateBMR (calculations.ts:3-10, Mifflin) and renders at 725 and 755-757, while coach.tdee.predicted (rendered 639-645) comes from tdee.ts:84-99 which switches to Katch-McArdle whenever bodyComp.leanMassKg > 0, captioned via BASIS_LABEL (goals.tsx:53). However the auditor overstates the confusion: the section is explicitly headed "Anchor to your formula burn" (723) and the caption at 725 says "Your profile puts your burn at…", so the manual block is framed as the formula figure. The unqualified FigureCell caption "kcal you burn" (756) is the actual defect. Real, but noticeable-friction grade rather than materially broken.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Accepting a coach plan destroys the user's manual targets with no confirmation and no undo</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:598</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Button label={coach.accepted ? 'Plan accepted' : 'Accept plan'} onPress={coach.accept} haptic .../>` fires appState.ts:451-470 immediately, which overwrites goals.calories/protein/carbs/fat plus proteinPct/carbsPct/fatPct. Nothing captures the previous values. The confirmation row that appears afterwards (583-595) states what the goals now are but offers no reversal. The copy at 701-704 promises "Set anything you like here and press Save — your numbers win until you accept a new plan."

**User impact.** A user who spent time tuning 2,150 kcal / 180 P / 190 C / 62 F taps the prominent primary "Accept plan" button to read the plan more closely and their targets are gone. Re-entering them means remembering four numbers the app no longer holds anywhere.

**Fix.** Snapshot `goals` in useCoach.accept before calling acceptRecommendation and render an "Undo" text button inside the accepted row at goals.tsx:583-595 that writes it back through updateGoals.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Code is as described: goals.tsx:598-604 calls coach.accept with no confirmation; useCoach.ts:326-328 → appState.ts:451-470 overwrites goals.calories/protein/carbs/fat plus the three pct fields with nothing captured; the accepted row (583-595) offers no reversal; goals.tsx:337-342 then re-seeds the manual draft from the new goals, so the old values are gone from the form too. But the control is a primary button labelled "Accept plan" doing exactly what its label says — the destructive act is explicit and intended, not a trap. The gap is the missing undo, which is friction rather than a materially broken flow.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The "Import from Google Fit" card is always rendered, including on iOS</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1073</code> · empty-error-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The Surface at 1073-1137 is rendered unconditionally inside weightTab. The platform check only happens after the user presses the button: handleHealthSync (636-651) awaits `getHealthConnectStatus()` and, on 'not_supported', sets the message "Health Connect is only supported on Android devices." profile.tsx:360 gates the equivalent section with `health.availability === 'available' && (...)`.

**User impact.** Every iPhone user gets a permanent card in Progress → Weight inviting them to sync from Google Fit, and only discovers it cannot work by tapping it and waiting through a spinner.

**Fix.** Probe availability on mount (as useHealthSync.ts:62-73 already does) and render the card only when it is 'available'. Reusing useHealthSync in progress.tsx gets this for free.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** The Surface at progress.tsx:1073-1137 sits unconditionally inside weightTab with no availability check. The platform test is inside the handler: handleHealthSync (636-652) awaits getHealthConnectStatus(), which returns 'not_supported' for `Platform.OS !== 'android'` (health-connect.client.ts:29), and only then sets "Health Connect is only supported on Android devices." profile.tsx:360 gates its equivalent with `health.availability === 'available'`, and useHealthSync.ts:62-73 already probes on mount.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Personal records ignore the time-range control and hide most of the user's lifts</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:770</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `getPersonalRecords(workoutLog).sort((a, b) => b.achievedOn.localeCompare(a.achievedOn)).slice(0, 4)` — the whole log, not `sessionsInRange`. It sits under the range chip that reads `{rangeWords}` = "last 7 days" (1253) and the caption "Best estimated 1RM per lift" (1185). workoutMath.ts:180 already returns records sorted by bestEstimated1RM descending; progress.tsx re-sorts by date and truncates.

**User impact.** With "7d" selected the card shows PRs set eight months ago under a heading that says last 7 days. And because the truncation is by recency, a lifter who has trained twelve lifts sees four — a light accessory PR set yesterday displaces their squat PR — with no "show all" and no indication anything is missing.

**Fix.** Either filter records to the selected window and label the card "PRs set in the last 30 days" (with a real empty state when none), or move the card out from under the range control, keep workoutMath's strength ordering, and add a "Show all N lifts" expander.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:770-776 `getPersonalRecords(workoutLog).sort((a, b) => b.achievedOn.localeCompare(a.achievedOn)).slice(0, 4)` — the whole log, not sessionsInRange (752-755), re-sorted by date over workoutMath.ts:180's `.sort((a, b) => b.bestEstimated1RM - a.bestEstimated1RM)`. The card sits under the range chip rendered at 1253 (`{rangeWords}`), and there is no "show all".

</details>

<details><summary><b>"Days logged" on Progress and "fully logged days" on Goals count different things</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:701</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** dayStats (701-722) includes any day where `entries.length > 0`, and `loggedDays = dayStats.length` is rendered as "Days logged 6/7" (836). tdee.ts:145 requires `calories >= MIN_QUALIFYING_CALORIES` (500) and coachAlerts.ts:236 uses the same 500 kcal floor for its "fully logged" count, which is what goals.tsx:97 and the insufficient_data alert quote back to the user.

**User impact.** A user who logged only a coffee on four of the last seven days reads "Days logged 6/7" on Progress, feels compliant, then opens Goals and is told "you have 2 fully logged days — log everything you eat on 8 more days". The two screens contradict each other about the same week and neither explains the difference.

**Fix.** Apply the same 500 kcal qualification in progress.tsx dayStats (it already computes `nutrition.calories`), and label the tile "Days fully logged". If partial days should still chart, chart them but exclude them from the count and the average.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:701-722 pushes a DayStat for any day where `entries.length > 0` with no calorie floor; `loggedDays = dayStats.length` (724) renders as "Days logged 6/7" (836). tdee.ts:12/145 requires `calories >= MIN_QUALIFYING_CALORIES` (500) before a day counts toward daysOfData, and coachAlerts.ts:30/236 uses the same floor — and goals.tsx:97 quotes `tdee.daysOfData` back as "fully logged days". Two different counts of the same week on two screens.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Averages are taken over logged days only, then stated as a conclusion about the week</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:725</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const average = (pick) => loggedDays > 0 ? dayStats.reduce(...) / loggedDays : null` — the divisor is the number of logged days, not the window. Lines 838-850 then render an unhedged sentence: "You averaged <StatValue>1,100</StatValue> kcal above goal." The same avg feeds the Macros "Average vs goal" ProgressTrack bars (939-942).

**User impact.** One heavy day logged out of seven produces "Avg intake 3,100 kcal" and a flat assertion that the user is 1,100 kcal over goal, next to a "Days logged 1/7" tile that most people will not weigh against it. The macro progress bars are similarly a single day dressed as a weekly average.

**Fix.** Suppress the summary sentence and the average-vs-goal bars below a minimum sample (say 3 days), replacing them with "Only 1 day logged — not enough to average yet." When shown, qualify the sentence: "Across your 4 logged days you averaged …".

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:725-726 `loggedDays > 0 ? dayStats.reduce(...) / loggedDays : null` — divisor is logged days, not `slots`. 843-849 renders the unhedged "You averaged <StatValue>N</StatValue> kcal above goal." and the same averages feed the ProgressTrack bars at 939-942. No minimum-sample guard anywhere.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The weight chart has no goal line and no trend line, and auto-zooms noise into a cliff</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:995</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The weight LineChart (995-1004) passes `fillToZero={false} connectGaps showDots` but neither `goal` nor `goalColor`, although LineChart supports both (props at 191-192, rendering at 280-290) and the calorie chart uses them (813-815). `profile.targetWeightKg` and `getWeightTargetProgress().trendKgPerWeek` are both available. In the non-zero-baseline branch (222-228) the y-range is `[min - spread*0.18, max + spread*0.18]`, so a 0.3 kg spread yields a ~0.4 kg tall canvas.

**User impact.** The single chart that answers "am I getting closer to my goal weight?" does not draw the goal weight. And because the axis auto-zooms to the data, a 0.3 kg overnight water swing is rendered as a full-height plunge — the user reads a dramatic drop where the fitted trend (which the app already computes) is flat.

**Fix.** Pass `goal={profile.targetWeightKg}` converted to the display unit and `goalColor={theme.textMuted}` to the weight LineChart, and add a Legend like the calorie tab has ("Weigh-in" / "Goal" dashed). Overlay the least-squares trend from getWeightTargetProgress as a second dashed path so the noise reads against a line.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:995-1004 passes width/slots/points/color/fillToZero/connectGaps/showDots/format and no `goal` or `goalColor`, although LineChartProps declares them (192-193) and renders a dashed line at 280-290, and the calorie chart uses them at 814-815. The non-zero branch (222-228) pads by `spread * 0.18` of the data range only, so a 0.3 kg spread fills the 152pt canvas.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Health-sync errors are painted in the protein data colour and ship no icon</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1113</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Body size={13} tone="muted" style={{ color: theme.macro.protein }}>{healthSyncMessage}</Body>`. theme.macro.protein is '#168BE1' / '#2F9AF2' (tokens.ts:76, 100), a validated *data* colour. healthSyncMessage carries failures ("Health Connect is not installed…", "Permission to read Google Fit / Health Connect was not granted.", lines 643-656), plain info, and successes ("Successfully imported 12 weigh-in entries!", line 687) — all in the same hue, with no icon.

**User impact.** On the same tab where blue means protein in the legend and the stacked bars, blue prose appears meaning "something failed". A colour-blind or colour-inattentive user gets no signal at all that the sync failed versus succeeded, since the only differentiator is the sentence and there is no icon to scan for.

**Fix.** Route these through the same pattern the app already uses at goals.tsx:850-877: an icon plus words plus a status token — AlertTriangle + theme.status.warning for failures, Check + theme.status.good for the import confirmation, Info + theme.textMuted for neutral instructions. Never theme.macro.* for status.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:1112-1116 `<Body size={13} tone="muted" style={{ color: theme.macro.protein }}>{healthSyncMessage}</Body>`, with no icon. tokens.ts:76 and 100 give macro.protein as '#168BE1' / '#2F9AF2' — a validated data hue also used for the protein legend swatch (878) and the stacked bars (383) on the same screen. healthSyncMessage carries failures (643, 649, 656, 665) and the success confirmation (687) in that one colour.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Data numbers on Goals and the weight card render in the body face instead of Fraunces/StatValue</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:592</code> · design-system · effort M</summary>

**Code.** goals.tsx:592 `<Body>{`Accepted. Your daily goals now match this plan: ${fmtInt(rec.calories)} kcal, ${fmtInt(rec.protein)} g protein, ${fmtInt(rec.carbs)} g carbs, ${fmtInt(rec.fat)} g fat.`}</Body>`; also 506 ("Built around your 2,450 kcal/day … burn"), 725 ("puts your burn at 2,450 kcal a day"), 865 ("macros add up to 2,110 kcal, which is 110 kcal above"), 874, and 689 which renders explainTdee's entire numeric sentence. WeightTarget.tsx:109 ("62% of the way"), 133 ("(3 weeks)"), 163 (progress.message = "6.6 kg to go. About 3 weeks (21 days) to go — around 18 Aug."). progress.tsx:1084 and 1088-1090 do the same for the import preview counts. progress.tsx:844-848 shows the app's own established fix — `<StatValue size={13} tone="secondary">` spliced inside the `<Body>` sentence.

**User impact.** The rule is that every data number is Fraunces with tabular figures, so numbers are instantly recognisable as numbers. Half the numeric copy on the Goals screen and all of it on the weight card is Figtree, so the two screens read as if built by different apps — and the tabular alignment that keeps the accepted-plan macros scannable is lost.

**Fix.** Wrap each figure in `<StatValue size={13} tone="secondary">` inline, exactly as progress.tsx:844-848 does. For the long generated sentences (explainTdee, progress.message) either return structured parts instead of one string from the core helpers (upstream change) or split on the known numeric tokens at render time.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Spot-checked every cite. goals.tsx:592 (accepted-plan sentence with four fmtInt figures inside a plain <Body>), 506, 725, 865, 874, and 688-690 which renders explainTdee's whole numeric sentence. WeightTarget.tsx:109 (`${Math.round(progress.fraction * 100)}% of the way…`), 133 (`days left (${progress.etaWeeks} week…)`), 161-163 (progress.message). progress.tsx:1084 and 1088-1090. Text.tsx:33-58 confirms StatValue is the only path to Fraunces + tabular-nums, and progress.tsx:843-849 shows the app's own inline pattern.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Three of the four chart summaries, and every muscle-group volume row, announce no values to a screen reader</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:884</code> · accessibility · effort M</summary>

**Code.** ChartArea sets `accessible` on the wrapper (line 166), which collapses all children into one node carrying only `accessibilityLabel`. The macros label is `Stacked macro chart for the ${rangeWords}, in grams per day.` (884) — no numbers. The training label is `Training volume by muscle group over the ${rangeWords}, in ${unitLabel}.` (1155) — no numbers. The weight label gives a count only (991). Only the calorie chart (801-803) reads its average and goal. VolumeBars rows (435-477) and the PR rows (1194-1221) sit inside that same collapsed node with no per-row labels.

**User impact.** A VoiceOver/TalkBack user swipes to the macros chart and hears "Stacked macro chart for the last 7 days, in grams per day" and nothing else — the entire point of the screen is unreadable. The same user cannot hear a single muscle-group volume figure or a single PR.

**Fix.** Extend the macros label with avgProtein/avgCarbs/avgFat vs goals and the training label with the top volumeRows; drop `accessible` from the VolumeBars ChartArea (1154) and give each row in VolumeBars (435-477) its own `accessible` + accessibilityLabel. The PR list already reads correctly and needs no change.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The chart half is correct: ChartArea sets `accessible` on the wrapper (progress.tsx:166), collapsing children into one node; the macros label (884) and the training label (1155) carry no numbers, the weight label (991) carries only a count, and only the calorie label (801-803) reads its average and goal. VolumeBars rows (435-477) are indeed inside that collapsed node (1154-1158). But the PR rows are NOT: the Personal-records Surface (1184-1224) renders `recentPRs.map` in a plain <View> with no ChartArea and no `accessible`, so each lift name, date and e1RM is its own readable node. Drop that claim from the finding.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"Needs 2 weigh-ins" is shown to users who have dozens</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1026</code> · copy · effort S</summary>

**Code.** weightDelta (746-749) is null unless `weightPoints.length >= 2`, and weightPoints is already filtered to the selected window (736-742). Line 1026-1029 then renders `<Body size={13} tone="muted">Needs 2 weigh-ins</Body>` under the "Change" label.

**User impact.** Someone who weighs in weekly and is looking at the 7d range has exactly one point in the window and is told they need two weigh-ins, when they have fifty. It reads as the app not having their data rather than as a window that is too short.

**Fix.** Change the copy to name the window — "Needs 2 weigh-ins in the last 7 days" — and, when the log has ≥2 entries overall, offer the 30d range inline or fall back to the change since the most recent prior weigh-in with its date stated.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:746-749 sets weightDelta to null unless `weightPoints.length >= 2`, and weightPoints is already window-filtered (736-742). 1026-1029 then renders the flat `Needs 2 weigh-ins` with no mention of the window. A weekly weigher on the default 7d range hits this with a full log.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Between 10 and 13 logged days the screen shows a measured burn, tells the user to plan around it, and then ignores it</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:113</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** tdee.ts:171 assigns confidence 'low' at ≥10 qualifying days, at which point buildTdeeEstimate returns a non-null `measured`, rendered as a solid figure by goals.tsx:656-663 and explained at line 113: "The measured number is the better one to plan around." But useCoach.ts:173-178 sets `trustMeasured` only when confidence is 'medium' or 'high' (14+ days), so anchorSource stays 'predicted' and the plan card at line 506 reads "Built around your … predicted burn". MIN_DAYS_FOR_MEASURED in goals.tsx:64 is 10.

**User impact.** On day 10 the user is rewarded with a measured number and told it is the better one to plan around — then the plan directly above it says it was built on the predicted number, with no explanation. The user cannot tell whether the coach is ignoring their data or the copy is wrong.

**Fix.** Make explainTdee's closing sentence conditional on `coach.anchorSource === 'measured'`. When measured exists but is not trusted, say so: "Measured from 11 days — the plan still uses the formula until there are 14." Alternatively align MIN_DAYS_FOR_MEASURED and the confidence thresholds so the number only appears once it is actually used.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** tdee.ts:171 assigns 'low' at `qualifyingDays >= 10` and 185-192 returns a non-null `measured`, which goals.tsx:657-663 renders as a solid figure. useCoach.ts:173-178 gates trustMeasured on `confidence === 'medium' || 'high'` (14+ days per tdee.ts:171), so anchorSource stays 'predicted' and goals.tsx:506 reads "…predicted burn". goals.tsx:113 closes explainTdee with "The measured number is the better one to plan around." — unconditional in that branch. goals.tsx:64 MIN_DAYS_FOR_MEASURED = 10 as cited.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Sugar, sodium and water are rendered as personal targets but have no editor — they only change as a side effect of editing an unrelated Profile field</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:409</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `updateGoals` is called from exactly two places in the app: goals.tsx:409 (calories, protein, carbs, fat, fiber and the pct split) and onboarding.tsx:281 (calories + three macros). Diary renders `goals.sugar` and `goals.sodium` as targets (diary.tsx:276, 289) and the Dashboard renders `goals.water` (index.tsx:165). Because onboarding deliberately does not call recalculateGoals (onboarding.tsx:279-280), a new account keeps DEFAULT_GOALS forever: sugar 50 g, sodium 2300 mg, water 2500 ml (appState.ts:144-156). Meanwhile the manual section promises "Set anything you like here and press Save" (goals.tsx:702).

**User impact.** A 50 kg woman and a 110 kg man both get a 2,500 ml water goal and a 50 g sugar goal, presented in the Diary and Dashboard as personal targets, and neither can change them. The Goals screen — the only screen named for the job — does not list them.

**Fix.** Add sugar, sodium and water fields to the manual targets Surface in goals.tsx (MacroGoals already carries them and updateGoals takes a partial), and have onboarding.tsx:281 seed them from calculateMacroGoals so a new account is not on 2,500 ml / 50 g regardless of bodyweight.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** I grepped every call site: `updateGoals(` appears only at goals.tsx:409 and onboarding.tsx:281, and neither touches sugar/sodium/water — confirmed, as are the consumers (diary.tsx:276/289, index.tsx:165) and DEFAULT_GOALS (appState.ts:144-156). But the claim that they "can be set nowhere" is wrong: `recalculateGoals` (appState.ts:226-239) Object.assigns the full output of calculateMacroGoals, which returns sugar (calories*0.05/4), sodium (2300) and water (weightKg*35) — calculations.ts:42-44 — and profile.tsx calls it at 249, 477, 490 and 504. So they are recomputed as an invisible side effect of editing any Profile basic, and stick at DEFAULT_GOALS only until the user next touches Name/Age/Height/Goal/Activity/Gender. That is arguably worse, but the evidence must say so.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The "set a goal weight" line on the weight card has no control to set one</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:94</code> · empty-error-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `{progress.targetKg === null ? <Body tone="secondary">{progress.message}</Body> : (...)}`. The message comes from weightTarget.ts:182: "Set a goal weight to track whether you are heading the right way." There is no Pressable, Link or Button in that branch. The field that would satisfy it lives in profile.tsx:421-430, inside a Section that is only auto-opened when no target exists.

**User impact.** On the Dashboard the entire weight card collapses to one sentence telling the user to do something, with no control to do it. They have to guess that "goal weight" lives under Profile → Targets.

**Fix.** Add an action to that branch — a Button "Set goal weight" wrapped in `<Link href="/profile">` today, or `/goals` once the weight goal moves there (see the goals-screen-missing-weight-goal finding). The EmptyState component in Layout.tsx already takes an `action` slot for exactly this.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** WeightTarget.tsx:94-95 is exactly as quoted — `progress.targetKg === null ? <Body tone="secondary">{progress.message}</Body> : (...)` with no Pressable/Link/Button, message from weightTarget.ts:182, and the only editor is profile.tsx:421-430 inside a Section auto-opened only when no target exists (419). EmptyState does take an `action` slot (Layout.tsx:189, 212). But the impact is overstated: the weigh-in Field and Log button (196-228) sit OUTSIDE that conditional, so the card does not "collapse to one sentence" — it still logs weight. What is missing is any route to set the goal.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Progress shows the calorie goal and the goal line but offers no way to reach the Goals screen</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:835</code> · discoverability · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Line 835 renders `<StatTile label="Daily goal" value={withCommas(goals.calories)} unit="kcal" />` and 813-815 draws the goal as a dashed line on the calorie chart. `/goals` is linked from exactly one place in the entire app — index.tsx:350, the Dashboard coach card. progress.tsx imports no Link and no router.

**User impact.** A user opens Progress, sees a week of intake sitting 400 kcal above a dashed goal line, and concludes the goal is wrong — and there is no affordance on that screen to change it. They must return to the Dashboard and recognise the "Coach plan" card as the door to goals.

**Fix.** Make the "Daily goal" StatTile (or a small "Adjust goals" ghost button in the Summary card header) a `<Link href="/goals">` with accessibilityRole="link". Same treatment in the Macros tab's "Average vs goal" card header.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:835 renders the Daily goal StatTile and 814-815 draws goals.calories as the dashed goal line; the import block (1-47) contains no expo-router import, no Link and no useRouter. Grepping the app, `href="/goals"` appears exactly once, at index.tsx:350 (the Dashboard CoachCard).

</details>

<details><summary><b>A mistyped weigh-in cannot be corrected from the card that recorded it</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:196</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const loggedToday = weightLog.some(e => e.date === today)` (46) drives the branch at 196-228: once an entry exists for today the Field and Log button are replaced by the static line "Weighed in today. Daily weigh-ins make the trend far more reliable." appState.ts:319-329 would happily accept a corrected entry — it replaces the same-date row and rewrites currentWeightKg — but there is no control left to send one. The only recovery is Profile → Weight log → the Trash2 IconButton at profile.tsx:558-563, inside a collapsed Section.

**User impact.** Someone who types 8.0 instead of 80.0 sets currentWeightKg to 8, which immediately drives BMI on the Profile header (profile.tsx:219), the BMR/TDEE anchors on Goals (goals.tsx:344-348), coach protein g/kg (coachAlerts.ts:298) and the whole weight-target card. The card that took the number then refuses to take another one that day, and offers no hint that the fix lives in a collapsed accordion on a different screen.

**Fix.** Keep the Field rendered when loggedToday, pre-filled with today's entry and labelled "Update today's weigh-in", with the Log button reading "Update". addWeightEntry already de-duplicates by date, so no store change is needed.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Goals' Suggested figure and Cut/Bulk presets ignore the pace the user chose at setup</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:347</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** goals.tsx:347 `suggested: calculateCalorieGoal(tdee, profile.goal)` — calculations.ts:23-27 is a flat `tdee - 500` / `tdee + 300`. The presets at 418-422 hard-code the same `-500` / `+300`. Onboarding deliberately does not use that path: onboarding.tsx:279-280 comments "Not recalculateGoals(): that applies the shared flat +/-500 and would overwrite the pace-derived target the last step just showed", and onboarding.ts:197-208 caloriesForPace derives the deficit from the chosen kg/week (`Math.abs(kgPerWeek) * 7700 / 7`). profile.targetRateKgPerWeek (onboarding.ts:269) is never read on this screen.

**User impact.** A user who chose "Gentle — 0.25 kg a week" at setup was shown and given TDEE − 275. Opening Goals, the Suggested cell and the Cut preset both offer TDEE − 500, a pace they explicitly declined, presented as the app's recommendation. Tapping Cut doubles their deficit and nothing says so.

**Fix.** Derive `suggested` and the Cut/Bulk preset deltas from `profile.targetRateKgPerWeek` via caloriesForPace (onboarding.ts:197-208), falling back to the flat helper only when no pace is set, and label the presets with the rate they imply.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Google Fit import stamps weigh-ins with the UTC date, landing them on the wrong day west of UTC</b> — <code>src/lib/health/health.sync.ts:22</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const dateStr = record.time.split('T')[0]` — record.time is the Health Connect ISO instant in UTC, so the calendar day is UTC's, not the user's. The sibling reader does it correctly: healthConnect.ts:173-177 builds `const at = new Date(record.time)` then `getDateString(at)`, which is local (calculations.ts:148-150). progress.tsx:736-742 then matches those dates against getLast7Days/getLast30Days, which are local (calculations.ts:152-166), and weightTarget.ts:64-69 measures the trend span from them.

**User impact.** A user in Los Angeles who weighs in at 5pm gets an entry dated tomorrow; one in Auckland weighing at 8am gets yesterday. Same-day manual entries then fail to de-duplicate against the import, the trend is fitted over shifted x-values, and the newest imported reading can be dated in the future — where getLast7Days will never show it and weightTrendKgPerWeek's `p.day <= 0` filter (weightTarget.ts:87) silently drops it.

**Fix.** Use the local calendar day, matching healthConnect.ts:177: `const dateStr = getDateString(new Date(record.time))`. Better, delete the src/lib/health import path from progress.tsx and reuse useHealthSync().importWeightHistory(), which already gets this right.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile accepts a goal weight on the wrong side of the current weight; onboarding rejects the same value</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:252</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** commitTargetWeight (252-262) checks only `Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0` before writing targetWeightKg. Onboarding routes the identical field through validateTarget (onboarding.ts:160-174), which refuses it: "To lose weight the target has to be below where you are now" — and onboarding.ts:153-158 explains exactly why, because it "makes every 'you are on track' reading afterwards meaningless". getWeightTargetProgress derives direction from the sign of `targetKg - currentWeightKg` (weightTarget.ts:187-188), never from profile.goal.

**User impact.** A user on goal='lose' who fat-fingers 88 instead of 78 gets a card reading "On track: gaining 0.3 kg per week" while the coach on the next screen is still prescribing a cut and firing the rate_too_fast / rate_too_slow alerts for losing. The two screens disagree about which direction is correct and neither flags the impossible target.

**Fix.** Call validateTarget(profile.goal, currentWeightKg, kg) in commitTargetWeight before updateProfile and surface the returned message under the Field, exactly as onboarding.tsx:684-688 does.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (10)

<details><summary><b>Chart primitives and the duplicate Health Connect flow are inlined in the 1,268-line Progress route</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:49</code> · information-architecture · effort L</summary>

**Code.** Four distinct concerns share the file: (1) chart primitives, lines 49-480 — CHART_H/GUTTER geometry, niceMax, YAxis, AxisDates, Legend, ChartArea, LineChart, MacroBars, VolumeBars; (2) generic controls, 486-583 — Segmented, StatTile, CardHeader; (3) a Health Connect integration flow, 636-689 and 1073-1137, which duplicates the one in profile.tsx:360-409; (4) four dashboards — caloriesTab (782-853), macrosTab (864-968), weightTab (979-1139), trainingTab (1143-1226).

**User impact.** For the user it shows up as a Progress tab where nutrition history, bodyweight and a device-import settings flow are stacked behind one segmented control — the Google Fit sync card in particular is a settings task that only appears if you happen to select "Weight". For maintenance, none of the four charts can be reused on the Workout or Diary tabs where they'd help.

**Fix.** Extract 49-480 into src/components/charts/ (LineChart, MacroBars, VolumeBars, ChartAxis) and 486-583 into src/components (Segmented, StatTile). Move the Health Connect card out of the Weight tab and into Profile beside the existing one (deleting the duplicate). Move the Training tab's volume + PR cards next to the Workout tab, where the user is already thinking about lifting.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The inventory is accurate — chart primitives 49-480, controls 486-583, a Health Connect flow at 636-689/1073-1137 duplicating profile.tsx:360-409, and four dashboards — and the file is 1268 lines. But the only genuinely user-facing part of it (a device-import settings task buried inside the Weight tab, and its divergence from the Profile implementation) is already reported by gfit-import-overwrites-and-drops-data and health-card-shown-on-ios. What remains is code organisation with no additional user consequence, which the brief excludes from P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The plan's burn figure is frozen but its "predicted/measured" label is recomputed live</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:506</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `{`Built around your ${fmtInt(rec.tdeeUsed)} kcal/day ${coach.anchorSource} burn`}`. `rec.tdeeUsed` is baked into the Recommendation when it is built (localRecommendation.ts:144 for the offline plan, validated from the API at api.ts:403-416), while `coach.anchorSource` is derived from today's store state in useCoach.ts:170-178.

**User impact.** A plan built two weeks ago from a trusted measured burn of 2,610 is opened after the user stopped logging for a week, dropping confidence back to 'low'. The card now reads "Built around your 2,610 kcal/day predicted burn" — attributing a measured number to a formula. The user can't audit where their target came from.

**Fix.** Store the anchor source on the Recommendation alongside tdeeUsed (upstream change to src/core/types Recommendation and the API validator), and render `rec.anchorSource` here rather than the live `coach.anchorSource`.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The mechanism is real: goals.tsx:506 interpolates `coach.anchorSource`, derived live in useCoach.ts:170-178, next to `rec.tdeeUsed`, which is frozen into the Recommendation at localRecommendation.ts:144 and validated straight through from the API at api.ts:405/420 — and the Recommendation type carries no source field, so nothing records which anchor built it. But the mislabel only appears after confidence crosses the 14-day boundary in either direction since the plan was built, and it changes one adjective in a caption. That is nit territory, not noticeable friction.

</details>

<details><summary><b>ETA days and ETA weeks are rounded independently and contradict each other in one sentence</b> — <code>src/core/utils/weightTarget.ts:129</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `horizonFrom` computes `etaWeeks = Math.max(1, Math.round(weeksExact))` and `etaDays = Math.max(1, Math.round(weeksExact * 7))` from the same weeksExact. WeightTarget.tsx:131-134 renders both together: `<StatValue size={20}>{String(progress.etaDays)}</StatValue>` then `days left (${progress.etaWeeks} week${...})`. describeEta (weightTarget.ts:143-151) does the same inside the status message.

**User impact.** At weeksExact = 1.43 the countdown reads "10 days left (1 week)"; at 2.6 it reads "18 days left (3 weeks)". The single most-read number on the card is immediately undercut by the parenthetical beside it, which makes the whole projection look unreliable.

**Fix.** Upstream web-repo change to src/core/utils/weightTarget.ts: derive etaWeeks from etaDays (`Math.max(1, Math.round(etaDays / 7))`) so the two can never disagree. Or drop etaWeeks entirely and let the mobile card show days only.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** weightTarget.ts:134-136 is as quoted: `etaDays = Math.max(1, Math.round(weeksExact * 7))` and `etaWeeks = Math.max(1, Math.round(weeksExact))` from the same weeksExact, rendered together at WeightTarget.tsx:131-134 and inside describeEta (143-151). The worked examples are right (1.43 → "10 days left (1 week)"). But both roundings are individually defensible and the parenthetical is a coarse restatement, not a contradictory claim — no number the user acts on is wrong. Nit.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The projected goal date is a day early for users east of UTC+12</b> — <code>src/core/utils/weightTarget.ts:121</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `addDays` builds `new Date(`${from}T12:00:00`)` (local noon, correct, DST-safe), calls setDate, then returns `base.toISOString().slice(0, 10)` — which is UTC. At UTC+13 (New Zealand in DST, Samoa, Tonga) local noon is 23:00 UTC on the previous calendar day, so the returned string is one day earlier. describeEta (147-151) then formats that string back through `new Date(`${etaDate}T12:00:00`).toLocaleDateString`. Every other date helper in the file is careful to pin to noon (dayDiff at 64-69) — this one function drops back to UTC.

**User impact.** A user in Auckland reads "About 3 weeks (21 days) to go — around 17 Aug" when the 21-day count lands on 18 Aug. Small, but it is the one date on the card and it disagrees with the day count sitting next to it.

**Fix.** Upstream web-repo change: return `${base.getFullYear()}-${pad(base.getMonth()+1)}-${pad(base.getDate())}` instead of toISOString().slice(0,10) — the same local-date formatting getDateString already uses in calculations.ts:148-150.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** weightTarget.ts:121-126: `new Date(`${from}T12:00:00`)` (local noon), setDate, then `base.toISOString().slice(0, 10)` — UTC. At UTC+13 local noon is 23:00 UTC on the previous calendar day, so the string is a day early; describeEta (147-151) then formats that string back. dayDiff (64-69) in the same file is noon-pinned throughout, and calculations.ts:148-150 getDateString already does the local formatting the fix needs.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The dashboard's "compact" weight card is the full card, duplicated on two screens</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:167</code> · information-architecture · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `compact` is used exactly once, at line 167: `{!compact && progress.trendKgPerWeek !== null && (...)}` — it suppresses only the Trend / Based-on row. The 40pt weight figure (99), progress bar (107), ETA box (116-142), status box (144-165) and the weigh-in Field + Log button (204-227) all render regardless. index.tsx:171-173 comments "Compact here; the full breakdown lives on Profile", and profile.tsx:358 renders the identical card in full.

**User impact.** The Dashboard is a today-at-a-glance screen and gets a five-block weight panel with a text input in the middle of it, pushing the streak/burned tiles below the fold. A user who scrolls Dashboard then opens Profile sees the same card twice and cannot tell which one is authoritative.

**Fix.** Make compact mean compact: current weight, one status line with its icon, and the weigh-in field — dropping the progress bar, the ETA box and the fraction caption, which belong on the Profile/Progress breakdown.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** WeightTarget.tsx:167 is the sole use of `compact`: `{!compact && progress.trendKgPerWeek !== null && (…)}`, suppressing only the Trend / Based-on row. The 40pt figure (99), ProgressTrack (107), ETA box (116-142), status box (144-165) and the weigh-in Field + Log (204-227) all render unconditionally. index.tsx:171-173 comments "Compact here; the full breakdown lives on Profile" and profile.tsx:358 renders `<WeightTargetCard />` — the same card minus one row.

</details>

<details><summary><b>kg↔lb conversion is reimplemented inside the component instead of using the core helper</b> — <code>src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:16</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `const LBS_PER_KG = 2.20462` at line 16 and `const toDisplay = (kg) => Math.round((unit === 'lbs' ? kg * LBS_PER_KG : kg) * 10) / 10` at 54-55, duplicating `kgToLbs` in calculations.ts:126-128. profile.tsx:216-217 has a third copy of the same pair. progress.tsx:693 does it correctly: `(kg) => (weightUnit === 'lbs' ? kgToLbs(kg) : kg)`. The design rule is "Business logic lives in src/core — never reimplement a formula in a component."

**User impact.** No visible divergence today (both round to one decimal), but three independent copies of the conversion mean a future correction to the constant or the rounding fixes one screen and not the others — which is precisely the class of bug that produces a chart and a stat tile disagreeing about the same weight.

**Fix.** Import kgToLbs from @core/utils/calculations in WeightTarget.tsx (and profile.tsx) and delete the local LBS_PER_KG constants, matching progress.tsx:693.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** WeightTarget.tsx:16 `const LBS_PER_KG = 2.20462` and 54-55 `toDisplay`; profile.tsx:44 and 216-217 are a verbatim second copy; calculations.ts:126-128 exports kgToLbs. progress.tsx:693 does it correctly through the core helper. Rounding happens to agree today (both land on one decimal), so this is a maintenance hazard rather than a live divergence — P3 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>"kcal to lose" — a raw enum value rendered as user-facing copy</b> — <code>app/goals.tsx:762</code> · copy · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<FigureCell label="Suggested" value={fmtInt(formula.suggested)} caption={`kcal to ${profile.goal}`} />` — profile.goal is the WeightGoal union 'lose' | 'maintain' | 'gain'. index.tsx:66-71 and profile.tsx both maintain a GOAL_LABELS map for exactly this.

**User impact.** The caption renders "kcal to lose" / "kcal to gain" / "kcal to maintain", which reads as an unfinished string rather than a sentence, on the one cell that is meant to justify the suggested number.

**Fix.** Add a GOAL_CAPTION map in goals.tsx ('lose' → 'kcal to lose weight', 'gain' → 'kcal to gain weight', 'maintain' → 'kcal to hold steady') and use it, or import the shared GOAL_LABELS.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** goals.tsx:758-763 `<FigureCell … caption={`kcal to ${profile.goal}`} />` with profile.goal typed 'lose' | 'maintain' | 'gain'. index.tsx:66-71 (PHASE_LABELS) and profile.tsx:54-58 (GOAL_LABELS) both maintain display-name maps for exactly this.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Weight-chart y-axis ticks are arbitrary padded values like 81.6 / 80.0 / 78.4</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:222</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** In the `fillToZero === false` branch (222-228) min and max are the raw data bounds padded by 18% of the spread, and ticks are `[max, (max + min) / 2, min]` (235) formatted with oneDecimal. `niceMax` (81-86), which exists specifically to make tick labels readable, is only applied in the fillToZero branch (line 220).

**User impact.** The weight chart's axis reads 81.6 / 80.0 / 78.4 instead of round steps, so the user cannot quickly judge where a point sits. The MacroBars and calorie charts, which do use niceMax, read cleanly by comparison — the inconsistency is visible when switching tabs.

**Fix.** Apply a nice-step routine to the non-zero-baseline branch too: pick a step from {0.2, 0.5, 1, 2, 5} that covers the padded span, then floor min and ceil max onto it before computing ticks.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:218-228: niceMax (81-86) is applied only in the `fillToZero` branch (220); the else branch sets min/max to the raw data bounds padded by 18% of the spread, and 235 builds ticks as `[max, (max + min) / 2, min]`, formatted by `oneDecimal` for the weight chart (1003). MacroBars (358) and the calorie chart do use niceMax.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Haptic fires on "Sync from Google Fit", which starts a network read rather than committing anything</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1125</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `<Button label="Sync from Google Fit" onPress={handleHealthSync} loading={healthSyncLoading} variant="secondary" icon={...} haptic />`. Button.tsx:26 documents the prop as "Fire a haptic tap. Reserve for meaningful commits, never plain navigation." handleHealthSync (636-670) only reads permissions and fetches records; the actual commit is "Confirm & Import" at 1093-1098, which is correctly haptic.

**User impact.** The user feels the same confirmation buzz for starting a read as for the write that follows, which devalues the signal that is supposed to mean "something was recorded".

**Fix.** Remove `haptic` from the Sync button at progress.tsx:1125 and keep it on Confirm & Import.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** progress.tsx:1119-1126 — the "Sync from Google Fit" Button carries `haptic` (1125). Button.tsx:26 documents the prop as "Reserve for meaningful commits, never plain navigation" and fires Haptics.impactAsync at 69. handleHealthSync (636-670) only checks status, requests permission and fetches; the commit is Confirm & Import (1093-1098), which also carries haptic.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Changing the goal in Profile leaves the pace from the old goal driving every ETA</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:475</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The Goal Choice handler (475-478) calls `updateProfile({ goal: v })` and recalculateGoals(), and never touches profile.targetRateKgPerWeek. That field is written only by answersToProfile (onboarding.ts:269) via signedRate, which encodes direction in the sign. weightTarget.ts:208-209 reads it back as `Math.abs(profile.targetRateKgPerWeek)` for plannedRate, which then sets the projected ETA (216-228) and the 'ahead' threshold (261).

**User impact.** Someone who set up as "lose, quick, 0.75 kg/week" and later switches to Build muscle keeps 0.75 kg/week as their planned pace. The card projects a bulk at a loss-phase rate and calls anything under 1.1 kg/week gained "on track" rather than "faster than planned".

**Fix.** When the goal changes, clear or re-default targetRateKgPerWeek with defaultPaceFor(v) through signedRate (onboarding.ts:90-97) — or expose the pace as an editable field so the user resets it themselves (see goals-screen-missing-weight-goal).

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

### Profile, auth & onboarding

#### P1 (6)

<details><summary><b>The splash screen is dismissed before auth resolves, leaving up to 8 seconds of blank screen at cold start</b> — <code>app/_layout.tsx:100</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `const ready = (fontsLoaded || !!fontError) && hydrated` (line 100) does not include the auth `loading` flag, and both the effect at line 107 and onLayout at line 103 call `SplashScreen.hideAsync()` as soon as `ready`. AuthProvider is only mounted after `if (!ready) return null` (line 110), so getSession() has not even started when the splash goes away. Meanwhile app/index.tsx:22 does `if (loading) return null` under a comment asserting 'the native splash is still up at that point, so a spinner here would only flash a second loading state' — which is false given _layout.tsx.

**User impact.** On every cold start the user watches an empty canvas-coloured screen with no logo, no spinner and no text for as long as the session check plus the 8-second data fetch takes. On a slow connection that reads as a hung or broken app, and the natural response is to force-quit, which aborts the data load mid-flight.

**Fix.** Either keep the splash up until auth settles — hoist AuthProvider above the `!ready` early return and gate hideAsync on `ready && !loading` — or render a real branded loading state in app/index.tsx instead of `null`, and correct the stale comment at app/index.tsx:15-17.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** app/_layout.tsx:100 `const ready = (fontsLoaded || !!fontError) && hydrated` omits auth entirely, and both the effect (106-108) and onLayout (102-104) call hideAsync on `ready`. AuthProvider mounts inside the tree gated by `if (!ready) return null` (line 110), and app/index.tsx:22 renders null while loading, under a Stack whose contentStyle is theme.canvas — so the user sees a flat coloured screen with nothing on it until getSession plus loadUserData (up to the 8s cap at AuthProvider.tsx:65) settles. One sub-claim is slightly off: child effects run before parent effects, so AuthProvider's getSession has in fact started a tick before hideAsync fires — but the splash still comes down before auth resolves, which is the actual defect. P1 stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>There is no forgot-password path anywhere, so a forgotten password permanently locks the user out</b> — <code>app/login.tsx:204</code> · broken-flow · effort L</summary>

**Code.** The login card renders only the Sign in / Create account toggle, an email Field, a password Field, the submit Button and a conditional 'Resend confirmation email' button (lines 161-221). A repo-wide search for resetPasswordForEmail, updateUser, 'forgot' and 'Forgot' returns no matches in app/ or src/lib/. AuthProvider exposes only signIn, signUp, resendConfirmation and signOut (lines 14-18).

**User impact.** A user who forgets their password sees the error 'Wrong password, or there is no account for you@example.com yet' (authErrors.ts:55-59) and has no route forward. Tapping Create account returns 'already has an account. Switch to Sign in'. They are stuck in a two-message loop with their entire nutrition and training history locked behind a password they cannot reset, and no support link.

**Fix.** Add `resetPassword(email)` to AuthProvider wrapping supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail with a `macrofit://` redirect, add a 'Forgot password?' ghost button under the submit Button in login.tsx (visible only in mode 'in'), and a deep-linked app/reset-password.tsx that calls supabase.auth.updateUser. Reuse describeAuthError for its failures.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** I read app/login.tsx in full: the card (lines 161-222) contains the mode toggle, email Field, password Field, submit Button and the conditional resend Button — nothing else. AuthProvider's context type (lines 10-29) exposes only signIn, signUp, resendConfirmation, signOut. A repo-wide grep for resetPasswordForEmail and auth.updateUser across src/ and app/ returns zero hits. describeAuthError's invalid_credentials copy (src/core/utils/authErrors.ts:54-59) and signUp's 'already has an account. Switch to Sign in' (AuthProvider.tsx:178) are exactly the two-message loop described. Confirmed.

</details>

<details><summary><b>An expired refresh token boots the user to login mid-task with no explanation and drops the last unsaved edits</b> — <code>src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:121</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** onAuthStateChange sets `setUser(nextSession?.user ?? null)` and `userRef.current = nextSession?.user ?? null` for every event including SIGNED_OUT (lines 121-124). RootNavigator's effect then fires `if (!user) { if (!onLoginScreen) router.replace('/login') }` (app/_layout.tsx:49-52). Any pending debounced save is now a no-op because flushSave starts with `if (!userRef.current || isHydratingRef.current) return` (line 101). Neither AuthProvider nor _layout.tsx surfaces any message about why the session ended.

**User impact.** A user is halfway through logging a workout when the refresh token expires (phone was in a pocket, supabase.ts:40-47 only refreshes in the foreground). The screen is replaced by the login form with no toast, no 'your session expired' copy and no return path to what they were doing. The last up-to-1.5s of edits never sync, and on re-login loadUserData overwrites the local store with the older server snapshot.

**Fix.** In onAuthStateChange, capture `event === 'SIGNED_OUT'` when it was not user-initiated (track a `signingOutRef` set by signOut) and pass a reason through to the login screen — render a Message with kind 'notice' saying the session expired and that nothing was lost. Flush the pending save with the still-valid userRef before clearing it.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** onAuthStateChange (AuthProvider.tsx:121-124) clears user and userRef for every event, including SIGNED_OUT and a failed refresh, with no reason propagated. app/_layout.tsx:49-52 then does `router.replace('/login')` unconditionally, and login.tsx reads no params and renders no session-expired message. A debounced save still pending is dropped because flushSave returns early on `!userRef.current` (line 101). On re-login, hydrateStore (appState.ts:603-617) assigns the server snapshot over the local keys, so the un-synced edits are gone. Mechanism verified in full; the trigger (refresh-token failure, foreground-only refresh at supabase.ts:40-51) is real but not everyday, which is why P1 rather than P0 is the right level.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Blurring any Profile field silently replaces the pace-derived calorie target with the flat plus/minus 500</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:249</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** commitBasics ends with `recalculateGoals()` (line 249) and runs onBlur of Name, Age and Height (lines 442, 449, 461). The Goal, Activity level and Gender Choice handlers also each call `recalculateGoals()` (lines 477, 490, 504). recalculateGoals uses `calculateCalorieGoal(tdee, profile.goal)` (appState.ts:230), which is `if (goal === 'lose') return Math.round(tdee - 500)` / `if (goal === 'gain') return Math.round(tdee + 300)` (calculations.ts:23-27). onboarding.tsx:279-281 explicitly refuses to call it: 'Not recalculateGoals(): that applies the shared flat +/-500 and would overwrite the pace-derived target the last step just showed.'

**User impact.** A user picked 'Gentle, 0.25 kg a week' in setup and was promised 2,150 kcal. Later they open Profile just to fix the spelling of their name; tapping out of the Name field silently rewrites their target to 1,875 kcal. Nothing on screen announces the change. Their dashboard 'kcal left today' and every on-track reading is now built on a plan they never chose, and the same tap discards an accepted coach plan's calories.

**Fix.** Stop calling recalculateGoals() from Profile. Recompute using `caloriesForPace(tdee, profile.goal, Math.abs(profile.targetRateKgPerWeek ?? 0))` from src/core/utils/onboarding.ts so the stored pace is honoured, and only when a field that actually feeds BMR/TDEE changed. Show a confirmation line ('Target updated to 2,150 kcal') rather than mutating silently.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** commitBasics (profile.tsx:238-250) ends with recalculateGoals() and is wired to onBlur of Name (442), Age (450) and Height (460); the Goal/Activity/Gender Choice handlers call it again at 477, 490 and 504. recalculateGoals (appState.ts:226-239) computes `calculateCalorieGoal(tdee, profile.goal)` and does `Object.assign(state.goals, macros)` where macros includes `calories` (calculations.ts:36-38), so the stored target is replaced. calculateCalorieGoal is the flat -500/+300 (calculations.ts:23-27) and ignores profile.targetRateKgPerWeek entirely. onboarding.tsx:279-281 documents the exact reason it refuses to call it. It also silently discards an accepted coach plan's calories (appState.ts:456-459). Confirmed at P1.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile accepts any age or height with no range check, while onboarding enforces 13-100 and 120-230 cm</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:243</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** commitBasics only guards positivity: `age: Number.isFinite(parsedAge) && parsedAge > 0 ? Math.round(parsedAge) : profile.age` and the same shape for heightCm (lines 241-248). Onboarding routes the same two fields through validateBasics, which enforces AGE_RANGE {min:13,max:100} and HEIGHT_CM_RANGE {min:120,max:230} (src/core/utils/onboarding.ts:123-151). calculateBMR does no clamping either: `10 * weightKg + 6.25 * heightCm - 5 * age + 5` (calculations.ts:7).

**User impact.** A user fat-fingers height as 17 instead of 170 and taps away. The value is accepted, recalculateGoals fires immediately, and their calorie target drops by roughly 950 kcal/day. Nothing flags it. The Profile header still prints '30 · 17 cm' as if it were a fact, and BMI on the hero row becomes a meaningless four-digit number.

**Fix.** Import validateBasics from src/core/utils/onboarding.ts into profile.tsx, run it in commitBasics with the current weight, and on failure keep the previous value AND render the returned message under the field instead of silently reverting. Same treatment for commitTargetWeight, which currently accepts any positive number.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** commitBasics (profile.tsx:238-250) guards only `Number.isFinite(x) && x > 0`; 17 cm, 3 cm and age 200 all pass and land in the profile, then recalculateGoals fires on the same blur. Onboarding runs the same two fields through validateBasics with AGE_RANGE 13-100 and HEIGHT_CM_RANGE 120-230 (src/core/utils/onboarding.ts:123-151, called at onboarding.tsx:265). calculateBMR does no clamping (calculations.ts:3-10) and calculateBMI (48-51) will happily print a four-digit number. Worth adding: when the parse does fail the field silently keeps showing the bad text while the store keeps the old value, so the screen and the state disagree with no message either way. Confirmed at P1.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Progress tab statically imports an Android-only TurboModule, which throws at module load on iOS</b> — <code>src/lib/health/health-connect.client.ts:2</code> · crash · effort M</summary>

**Code.** health-connect.client.ts:2-8 imports getSdkStatus/initialize/requestPermission/readRecords from 'react-native-health-connect' at module scope. That package's entry eagerly requires NativeHealthConnect.js, whose entire body is `TurboModuleRegistry.getEnforcing('HealthConnect')` (node_modules/react-native-health-connect/lib/commonjs/NativeHealthConnect.js), which throws when the native module is not registered. The package ships an `android/` directory and no iOS implementation or podspec (only `"ios": {}` in codegenConfig). src/lib/health/index.ts re-exports it and app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:28-32 imports it directly, so evaluating the Progress route on iOS throws. package.json declares an `ios: expo run:ios` script and app.json ships a full ios block, so iOS is a declared target. The sibling client avoids exactly this with a lazy `await import(...)` inside a try/catch (src/lib/healthConnect.ts:36-44, commented 'Not linked into this binary — e.g. running in Expo Go').

**User impact.** On an iOS build (and in Expo Go on either platform) opening the Progress tab dies at module evaluation instead of rendering — the user loses the entire progress/charts area of the app with a red screen or a silent tab crash. Android release builds are unaffected, which is why it can survive CI that only builds Android.

**Fix.** Delete src/lib/health/ and route the Progress sync through useHealthSync, which already lazy-loads the module behind a try/catch. If the directory must stay for now, convert health-connect.client.ts to the same `const hc = await import('react-native-health-connect')` pattern guarded by `Platform.OS === 'android'`, so nothing is required at module scope.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P2 (16)

<details><summary><b>Health Connect shows 'Connected' on a partial grant, and a refusal produces no message — then the weight import claims there is nothing new</b> — <code>src/lib/healthConnect.ts:72</code> · feedback-state · effort M</summary>

**Code.** requestPermissions asks for `[{accessType:'read',recordType:'Steps'},{accessType:'read',recordType:'Weight'}]` but returns `Array.isArray(granted) && granted.length > 0` (line 72) — any single grant counts as success. hasPermissions does the same (line 85). useHealthSync.connect does `setGranted(ok)` with no error branch (src/hooks/useHealthSync.ts:75-85). profile.tsx:399-407 renders the same 'Connect' button whether ok was true or false, and importWeightHistory reports 0 results as 'No new weigh-ins found — everything on file is already logged.' (line 384).

**User impact.** A user grants Steps but declines Weight. Profile flips the subtitle to 'Connected'. They tap 'Import weight history' and are told everything on file is already logged — a flat untruth; the read was denied. They conclude Health Connect has no weight data and stop trying, and the app never offers a way back to the permission screen.

**Fix.** Have requestPermissions/hasPermissions return the granted recordType set rather than a boolean, expose `grantedSteps` and `grantedWeight` from useHealthSync, and in profile.tsx render per-capability state. On denial show an explicit message with a button that opens Health Connect's app settings, and never print 'no new weigh-ins' when the weight permission is missing.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code is exactly as described: requestPermissions returns `Array.isArray(granted) && granted.length > 0` (src/lib/healthConnect.ts:71) and hasPermissions the same (line 83), so one of the two reads counts as connected; useHealthSync.connect just does setGranted(ok) with no failure branch (src/hooks/useHealthSync.ts:75-85); profile.tsx:399-407 re-renders the identical 'Connect' block on refusal with no message; and importWeightHistory prints 'No new weigh-ins found — everything on file is already logged.' (profile.tsx:383-384) when readWeightHistory returns [] from its catch. All verified. Severity is the problem: this is a secondary import convenience, not a core task — nobody is blocked from logging food, finishing a set or reading their targets. It is misleading feedback in an optional feature, which is P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Chat history vanishes on close, failures cannot be retried, and error text is fed back to the model as conversation</b> — <code>app/chat.tsx:169</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** messages is plain component state seeded with the welcome entry (lines 155-157) and chat is a modal (app/_layout.tsx:82), so router.back() unmounts it. Nothing persists it. On failure the catch pushes `{ role: 'assistant', text: message, failed: true }` (lines 299-304) with no retry affordance, and the user's typed text was already cleared by `setInput('')` (line 175). The next send builds history with only `messages.filter(m => m.id !== WELCOME_ID)` (line 170), so the error string — e.g. '/api/chat timed out after 45s. Check your connection and try again.' — is sent to the model as a genuine assistant turn.

**User impact.** A user describes a full meal, the request times out, and they must retype the whole sentence from memory because the input was cleared and there is no Retry. If they do retype it, the model now believes it previously said it timed out, and follow-ups like 'yes log that' resolve against a corrupted transcript. Closing the sheet to check a number in the Diary wipes the conversation entirely.

**Fix.** Persist messages to the store (or AsyncStorage) keyed by day so reopening restores the thread, add a 'Retry' Button on failed entries that re-sends the preceding user turn, keep the user's text in a lastAttempt ref until the request succeeds, and exclude `failed` entries when building the history array at line 169.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** messages is component state seeded with the welcome entry (chat.tsx:153-157) and chat is a Stack modal (app/_layout.tsx:82), so router.back() from the close IconButton (chat.tsx:316) unmounts and discards it; nothing writes it to the store or AsyncStorage. setInput('') runs at line 175 before the request, and the catch at 299-304 pushes the error text as a normal assistant entry with `failed: true`; I read the failed-message renderer at 375-389 and it is an AlertTriangle plus text — there is no retry control anywhere. The history built at 169-171 filters only WELCOME_ID, so the error string is replayed to the model as an assistant turn. All confirmed at P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Two independent Health Connect clients with different permission sets are surfaced as two different buttons on two screens</b> — <code>src/lib/health/health-connect.client.ts:11</code> · information-architecture · effort L</summary>

**Code.** src/lib/health/ requests REQUIRED_PERMISSIONS of Weight, Height, BodyFat and Steps (lines 11-16) and is consumed only by app/(tabs)/progress.tsx ('Sync from Google Fit', line 1120). src/lib/healthConnect.ts requests only Steps and Weight (line 26-29) and is consumed by useHealthSync, which powers Profile's 'Connect' / 'Import weight history' (profile.tsx:360-408). The two also disagree on availability vocabulary ('available'|'unsupported'|'not_installed'|'unavailable' vs 'available'|'not_installed'|'not_supported') and on lazy vs top-level import of react-native-health-connect. Profile hides its whole section behind `health.availability === 'available'` (line 360), so a user without Health Connect installed gets no prompt there, while Progress offers an 'Install Health Connect' button (line 1128).

**User impact.** A user connects Health Connect from Profile, then finds a second, differently-worded 'Sync from Google Fit' on Progress that asks for permissions again — including Height and Body Fat that Profile's copy never mentioned. Whichever they use first determines whether their manual weigh-ins are protected, and if Health Connect is not installed the Profile section is simply absent with no explanation.

**Fix.** Delete src/lib/health/ and make useHealthSync the single client. Extend it with the Height/BodyFat reads Progress needs and with an 'install' state that surfaces openHealthConnectPlayStore. Render one Health Connect control (Profile) and have Progress link to it rather than duplicating the flow. Align the requested recordTypes with the four declared in app.json:52-58.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** src/lib/health/health-connect.client.ts:11-16 requests Weight, Height, BodyFat and Steps and is re-exported through index.ts into app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:28-32 ('Sync from Google Fit', line 1120). src/lib/healthConnect.ts:26-29 requests Steps and Weight only and feeds useHealthSync, which powers profile.tsx:360-408. The availability vocabularies differ exactly as stated (healthConnect.ts:18 vs health-connect.client.ts:28), one lazy-imports the native module with a try/catch (healthConnect.ts:36-44) and one imports it statically (health-connect.client.ts:2-8). profile.tsx:360 hides the whole section unless availability === 'available' while progress.tsx:1127-1133 offers an install button. Confirmed at P2 — and see the missed iOS crash finding, which is a direct consequence of the duplicate client.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Password field has no visibility toggle, no iOS textContentType and no submit-on-return</b> — <code>app/login.tsx:191</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The password Field passes only `secureTextEntry`, `autoCapitalize="none"` and `autoComplete={mode === 'in' ? 'current-password' : 'new-password'}` (lines 191-199). There is no textContentType, no returnKeyType, no onSubmitEditing, and no reveal control. The email Field likewise has no returnKeyType/onSubmitEditing (lines 181-190). The codebase already knows the pattern: src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:216-217 sets `returnKeyType="done"` and `onSubmitEditing={submitWeighIn}`.

**User impact.** A user typing a 14-character password on a phone keyboard has no way to check it before submitting, so a single mistyped character produces 'Wrong password, or there is no account for you@example.com yet' — an error that also suggests their account may not exist. Pressing Return after the password does nothing; they must dismiss or reach past the keyboard to hit Sign in.

**Fix.** Add an eye IconButton inside the password Field that toggles secureTextEntry, add `textContentType={mode === 'in' ? 'password' : 'newPassword'}` and `textContentType="emailAddress"` on the email field for iOS autofill, and wire `returnKeyType="next"` + onSubmitEditing focus-forward on email and `returnKeyType="go"` + `onSubmitEditing={submit}` on password.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** login.tsx:191-199 passes only label/value/onChangeText/placeholder/secureTextEntry/autoCapitalize/autoComplete; the email Field at 181-190 has no returnKeyType or onSubmitEditing either. Field spreads ...props onto TextInput (src/components/Layout.tsx:157-180), so all of these are one-line additions. The pattern is already used elsewhere — WeightTarget.tsx:216-217, diary.tsx:392-394, goals.tsx:717 — so this is an omission on the app's gateway screen, not a platform limitation. P2 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Onboarding progress is held only in component state, so quitting mid-flow restarts at step 1 with every field blank</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:178</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** Every answer lives in useState: step, name, gender, age, heightUnit, heightCmText, heightFt, heightIn, weightUnit, weightText, activityLevel, goal, pace, targetText (lines 178-193). Nothing is written to the store until finish() runs updateProfile/addWeightEntry/updateGoals/completeOnboarding at the very end (lines 270-284). name/age/height/weight are initialised to '' rather than from the existing profile, so even a partially configured account starts empty. The gate keeps routing back here while `onboardedAt === null` (app/index.tsx:28, app/_layout.tsx:53-56).

**User impact.** A user gets a phone call on step 4 of 5. The OS reclaims the app. On relaunch they are back on the Welcome card and must re-enter their sex, age, height, weight, activity level, goal, pace and target weight from scratch. There is no indication anything was saved, which is the classic point at which people abandon setup.

**Fix.** Persist the draft after each successful `next()` — either write partial answers straight into profile via updateProfile (leaving onboardedAt null), or store a `onboardingDraft` slice — and rehydrate all useState initialisers from it, including `step`. Show 'Picking up where you left off' on resume.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Every answer is useState (onboarding.tsx:178-193) with name/age/heightCmText/weightText/targetText initialised to '' and nothing persisted until finish() runs updateProfile/addWeightEntry/updateGoals/completeOnboarding (270-284). next() only calls setStep (293). The gate keeps routing back to step 0 while onboardedAt is null (app/index.tsx:28, app/_layout.tsx:53-56). So a process kill on step 4 costs all five steps. Confirmed at P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>KeyboardAvoidingView is a no-op on Android across login, onboarding and chat</b> — <code>app/chat.tsx:325</code> · broken-flow · effort M</summary>

**Code.** All three screens use `behavior={Platform.OS === 'ios' ? 'padding' : undefined}` (chat.tsx:325, login.tsx:127, onboarding.tsx:320). With behavior undefined KeyboardAvoidingView applies no offset and is a passthrough View. app.json declares no `android.softwareKeyboardLayoutMode`, and Expo SDK 57 / RN 0.86 run Android edge-to-edge by default, where the window is not resized for the IME. In chat the composer sits in a non-scrolling flex container at the bottom of the KeyboardAvoidingView (lines 517-567) and has no way to move.

**User impact.** On Android the chat composer — the only input on the screen — can sit under the keyboard, so the user types blind and cannot see the Send button. On login and onboarding the ScrollView lets them scroll the field back into view, but the primary action button is pushed under the keyboard and reachable only after an extra scroll.

**Fix.** Adopt react-native-keyboard-controller's KeyboardAvoidingView (or set `behavior="height"` plus `useAnimatedKeyboard` from Reanimated, which is already a dependency) so the composer and primary buttons lift on Android too. Verify on an Android 15 device with edge-to-edge enabled.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified all three call sites — chat.tsx:323-325, login.tsx:125-127, onboarding.tsx:318-320 — each `behavior={Platform.OS === 'ios' ? 'padding' : undefined}`, which makes KeyboardAvoidingView a plain View on Android. app.json (read in full) declares no android.softwareKeyboardLayoutMode and no edge-to-edge opt-out, on Expo SDK 57 / RN 0.86 where Android edge-to-edge is the default and the window is not resized for the IME. In chat the composer sits below a flex:1 ScrollView inside the KAV with nothing to lift it. P2 is right — login and onboarding are recoverable by scrolling; the chat composer is the genuinely affected one.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The final onboarding step promises a timeline that ignores the 1200 kcal floor applied to the very target it shows</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:262</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `const horizon = describeHorizon(weeksToTarget(weightKg, targetKg, pace))` (line 262) divides the remaining distance by the chosen pace only. The calorie number on the same screen comes from caloriesForPace, which ends `return Math.round(Math.max(MIN_DAILY_CALORIES, raw))` with MIN_DAILY_CALORIES = 1200 (src/core/utils/onboarding.ts:184, 207). weeksToTarget has no knowledge of that clamp (lines 214-223).

**User impact.** A small user picks 'Quick — 0.75 kg a week' and a 12 kg target. The screen tells them 'About 16 weeks at this pace' while the prescribed 1,200 kcal actually supports roughly half that rate. Sixteen weeks later the app reports them as stalled against a schedule it silently could never deliver, and the weight-goal card on Profile shows 'Stalled' for a plan the app itself floored.

**Fix.** Have the last step derive the effective pace from the calories it is about to save — `(tdee - plan.calories) * 7 / KCAL_PER_KG` — and pass that to weeksToTarget. When the floor bit, say so in one line ('At 1,200 kcal the safe pace is about 0.4 kg a week, so about 30 weeks'). The clamp-aware helper belongs upstream in src/core/utils/onboarding.ts.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** onboarding.tsx:262 `describeHorizon(weeksToTarget(weightKg, targetKg, pace))`, and weeksToTarget (src/core/utils/onboarding.ts:214-223) is pure `distance / kgPerWeek` with no knowledge of calories. The number rendered beside it comes from caloriesForPace (onboarding.tsx:256), which ends `Math.round(Math.max(MIN_DAILY_CALORIES, raw))` with MIN_DAILY_CALORIES = 1200 (lines 184, 207).
