So whenever the floor bites, the screen shows a timeline the prescribed calories cannot deliver. Confirmed at P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Onboarding says 'Other' averages both formulas; the calorie formula uses the female constant and the body-fat formula uses the male one</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:455</code> · copy · effort S</summary>

**Code.** onboarding.tsx:454-456 renders 'Body-fat and calorie formulas differ by sex. “Other” uses the average of both.' calculateBMR is `if (gender === 'male') { ... + 5 } return 10 * weightKg + 6.25 * heightCm - 5 * age - 161` (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:6-9) — 'other' and 'female' take the identical branch. The gap between the two constants is 166 kcal, and the average would be -78.

**User impact.** A non-binary user picks 'Other' on the strength of that sentence and receives a BMR 78 kcal/day lower than promised, carried through TDEE and into their calorie target. The app has told them in writing what it does and then done something else, which is the kind of thing that destroys trust in every other number on screen.

**Fix.** Fix the mobile copy at onboarding.tsx:455 and the Profile label at profile.tsx:495 to state what actually happens. Upstream web repo change if the promise is to be kept: calculateBMR should use the mean constant (-78) for 'other' (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:6-9) and bodyComposition.ts:38-41 should stop silently choosing the male equation for 'other' — or the UI should ask which formula the user wants.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The copy at onboarding.tsx:454-456 is wrong, but in a different and larger way than reported. calculateBMR (src/core/utils/calculations.ts:6-9) sends 'other' down the female branch (-161), while estimateBodyComposition (src/core/utils/bodyComposition.ts:38-41) explicitly sends 'other' down the MALE branch with a comment saying so. So the sentence 'Body-fat and calorie formulas differ by sex. Other uses the average of both' is false for both formulas it names, and in opposite directions — neither averages anything. The 78 kcal arithmetic in the finding is right for BMR. Severity stays P2; the fix has to cover both files.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Onboarding says every number is editable under Profile, but calorie and macro targets are only editable at /goals</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:670</code> · copy · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The last onboarding step promises 'Nothing is locked in — every number is editable under Profile.' (line 670). Profile's sections are Health Connect, Targets (goal weight only), About you, Weight log, Body measurements, Custom foods, Meal templates and Appearance — none of them exposes goals.calories/protein/carbs/fat, and profile.tsx contains no link to /goals. A search for '/goals' across app/ returns exactly one navigation site: the CoachCard Link on the dashboard (app/(tabs)/index.tsx:350).

**User impact.** A user who wants to raise their protein target from 150 g to 180 g goes to Profile because the setup flow told them to, scrolls eight collapsed sections, finds nothing, and gives up. The screen that actually does it is behind a card on the Dashboard labelled 'Coach plan' — which reads as an AI feature, not as settings.

**Fix.** Add a 'Daily targets' Section to profile.tsx that either edits goals inline or is a Pressable row navigating to /goals with a ChevronRight, matching the other rows. Then the onboarding copy becomes true.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** onboarding.tsx:670 promises 'Nothing is locked in — every number is editable under Profile.' I read profile.tsx in full: its sections are Health Connect (360), Targets — goal weight only (411-435), About you (437), Weight log (535), Body measurements (569), Custom foods (645), Meal templates (679), Appearance (714) and the account block (739). Nothing touches goals.calories/protein/carbs/fat and there is no link to /goals. A grep for '/goals' across app/ returns one navigation site, the CoachCard Link at app/(tabs)/index.tsx:350, whose label is 'Coach plan' / 'Coach: no plan yet. Open goals to set one up.' (index.tsx:343-347). So the screen is reachable but never from where the setup flow says to look. P2 stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>EXPO_PUBLIC_USDA_API_KEY is documented, CI-injected and exported, but never read — food search always uses the shared DEMO_KEY</b> — <code>src/lib/env.ts:107</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** src/lib/env.ts:107 exports `USDA_API_KEY` from EXPO_PUBLIC_USDA_API_KEY, and nothing imports it (repo-wide search finds only its own definition). app/food-search.tsx:25 imports searchUSDA from @core/utils/usdaApi, whose key is `const API_KEY = (import.meta as any).env?.VITE_USDA_API_KEY ?? 'DEMO_KEY'` (src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:5) — Vite-only syntax, so on Hermes it optional-chains to undefined and falls back to DEMO_KEY every time. The variable is nonetheless in .env.example:41, README.md:40 and wired as a GitHub secret in .github/workflows/android-release.yml:55.

**User impact.** Every install shares the public DEMO_KEY (30 req/min, 1000 req/day per IP). Once that budget is spent, food search returns nothing for everyone on that network and the app reports it as no results rather than as rate limiting. The operator set the secret in CI and has no signal that it is inert.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change: make src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts take the key as an argument (or read from an injected config object) instead of import.meta. Then pass USDA_API_KEY from src/lib/env.ts at the call site in app/food-search.tsx, and drop the dead export if the wiring is not done. Env.ts already fails loudly for Supabase vars; do the same class of check here.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** src/lib/env.ts:107 exports USDA_API_KEY; a repo-wide grep for USDA_API_KEY returns only that definition plus .env.example:41, README.md:40 and .github/workflows/android-release.yml:55 — no consumer anywhere in src/ or app/. app/food-search.tsx:25 imports searchUSDA from @core/utils/usdaApi and calls it at line 286; that module's key is `const API_KEY = (import.meta as any).env?.VITE_USDA_API_KEY ?? 'DEMO_KEY'` (src/core/utils/usdaApi.ts:5), Vite-only syntax that cannot resolve under Metro/Hermes, so DEMO_KEY is always used. The CI secret is inert. Confirmed at P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile's height input is cm-only regardless of the user's chosen height unit</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:459</code> · broken-flow · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The About-you row renders `<Field numeric label="Height (cm)" value={heightCm} keyboardType="number-pad" />` (lines 456-464) with no ft/in variant, while the section subtitle prints both: `${profile.age} · ${profile.heightCm} cm (${cmToFeetInches(profile.heightCm)})` (line 440) and the screen offers a Height unit toggle right below (line 522). Onboarding by contrast has a full UnitToggle with paired feet/inches Fields and conversion on switch (onboarding.tsx:468-510).

**User impact.** A user who chose ft/in during setup, and who sees ft/in everywhere else, must convert their own height to centimetres by hand to correct it. Doing the conversion wrong is silent — commitBasics accepts any positive number (see the validation finding) — so '5' or '511' lands in the profile and rewrites their calorie target.

**Fix.** Reuse the onboarding pattern: when profile.heightUnit === 'ft' render paired feet/inches Fields backed by feetInchesFromCm/cmFromFeetInches from src/core/utils/onboarding.ts, and label the field with the active unit. Store stays cm, exactly as the weight comment at profile.tsx:510 already establishes.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** profile.tsx:456-464 renders a single `Field numeric label="Height (cm)" keyboardType="number-pad"` with no ft/in variant, while the section subtitle at line 440 prints `${profile.heightCm} cm (${cmToFeetInches(profile.heightCm)})` and a Height unit toggle sits at 522-530 that changes nothing about this input. Onboarding does it properly with paired feet/inches Fields and conversion on switch (onboarding.tsx:468-510, switchHeightUnit at 220-234). Combined with the missing range check in commitBasics, a botched hand conversion is accepted silently. Confirmed at P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Sign-out fires the final save without awaiting it, so the last edits can race the session teardown and never reach the server</b> — <code>src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:201</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** signOut is `flushSave(); await supabase.auth.signOut()` (AuthProvider.tsx:201-204). flushSave is synchronous and discards the promise — `void saveUserData(userRef.current.id, collect())` (line 106) — and saveUserData's upsert (83-85) resolves its auth token asynchronously inside supabase-js. supabase.auth.signOut() runs concurrently and clears the session, so the in-flight upsert can be sent without a valid access token and be rejected by RLS. saveUserData's catch (88-90) only sets syncStatus to 'error', which is on a screen the user is being navigated away from. Meanwhile profile.tsx:291 tells them 'Your data stays synced to your account.'

**User impact.** Anything the user changed in the ~1.5s before tapping Sign out — the set they just logged, the meal they just added — can be silently dropped from the server while the confirmation dialog promises the opposite. They see it again on this device only because nothing clears local state; on their next device it is gone.

**Fix.** Make flushSave return the save promise, and in signOut `await flushSave()` (and await any in-flight save) before calling supabase.auth.signOut(). If the save fails, tell the user before signing out rather than after.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile claims 'Sync failed — will retry' but nothing ever retries</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:303</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** profile.tsx:297-304 maps syncStatus 'error' to the string 'Sync failed — will retry'. In AuthProvider the only things that ever call saveUserData are the debounced store subscription (137-144) and the AppState background handler (149-154); saveUserData's own failure path just does `setSyncStatus('error')` (86, 89) with no timer, no backoff and no re-queue. If the user makes no further edit and does not background the app, the failed save is never attempted again.

**User impact.** A user who sees the red CloudOff line trusts the app to recover and closes it. The write is never retried, so whatever failed to upload is missing on their other device — a promise in the copy that the code does not keep, on the one screen that exists to reassure them their data is safe.

**Fix.** Either implement it — retry with backoff on failure and on regaining connectivity — or change the copy to what actually happens ('Not saved. Make another change or reopen the app to retry.') plus a manual 'Retry now' button that calls flushSave.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile's About-you fields are captured at mount and never resynced, so blurring one field writes stale values back over the others</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:227</code> · data-correctness · effort M</summary>

**Code.** name/age/heightCm/targetWeight/stepGoal are `useState(...)` initialised from the profile once at mount (profile.tsx:227-235) with no effect resyncing them. commitBasics (238-250) writes all three of name, age and heightCm together on every blur of any one of them. The profile can change underneath that snapshot from other code paths: progress.tsx:683-685 does `updateProfile({ heightCm: healthSyncResult.latestHeightCm })`, and AuthProvider's loadUserData → hydrateStore (appState.ts:603-617) replaces the whole profile object after a sign-in that happens while the app is running. Tab screens stay mounted once visited, so the stale snapshot survives.

**User impact.** User visits Profile, goes to Progress and imports their height from Health Connect, comes back to Profile and fixes a typo in their name. Blurring the Name field silently rewrites heightCm back to the pre-import value and re-runs recalculateGoals on it, so their calorie target moves for a reason nothing on screen explains.

**Fix.** Derive the field values from the store and sync them on change (useEffect on profile.age/heightCm/name, or key the section off the profile), and have commitBasics patch only the field that was actually edited rather than all three.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Switching the weight unit on Profile leaves the goal-weight field showing the old unit's number, which is then committed as the new unit</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:518</code> · data-correctness · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The Weight unit Choice does `onChange={v => updateProfile({ weightUnit: v })}` (profile.tsx:511-519) and nothing else — targetWeight state, initialised once from `toDisplay(profile.targetWeightKg)` at line 230-232, is not reconverted. The field's label recomputes to `Goal weight (${unit})` (423) and the section subtitle recomputes to the converted value (417), so the field and the label immediately disagree. commitTargetWeight then reads the stale text and converts with the NEW unit: `const kg = unit === 'lbs' ? value / LBS_PER_KG : value` (260). Onboarding does this correctly — switchWeightUnit rewrites the text on toggle (onboarding.tsx:208-218).

**User impact.** A 70 kg goal user switches the display to lbs. The field still reads '70' while the subtitle says 'Goal 154.3 lbs'. The moment they touch the field and tap away, their goal weight becomes 70 lbs — 31.8 kg — and the weight-target card, countdown and every 'on track' verdict are computed against a target no human set.

**Fix.** In the Weight unit onChange, convert targetWeight (and any other displayed weight text) the way onboarding.tsx:208-218 does before storing the new unit, or re-derive the field text from profile.targetWeightKg whenever unit changes.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>After a successful sign-in the login screen sits idle with a re-tappable button for as long as the data fetch takes</b> — <code>app/login.tsx:95</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** run() clears busy as soon as signInWithPassword resolves (login.tsx:87-97, setBusy(false) at 95). onAuthStateChange then sets hydrating true and starts loadUserData (AuthProvider.tsx:127-130), and RootNavigator deliberately refuses to route while hydrating (`if (loading || hydrating) return`, app/_layout.tsx:48). login.tsx never reads `hydrating`, so between those two moments the form is fully interactive with no spinner and no message, for up to the 8s cap in loadUserData (AuthProvider.tsx:65).

**User impact.** On a slow connection the user taps Sign in, the button un-spins, and nothing happens for several seconds. The natural response is to tap Sign in again, firing a second signInWithPassword that can trip GoTrue's rate limiter and produce 'Too many attempts. Wait a minute before trying again.' (authErrors.ts:68) immediately after a sign-in that actually succeeded.

**Fix.** Expose `hydrating` to the login screen and keep the submit button in its loading/disabled state while it is true, with a line saying the account's data is being restored.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (4)

<details><summary><b>Auth error mapper has no message-based fallback, so an error arriving without a `code` degrades to GoTrue's raw prose</b> — <code>src/core/utils/authErrors.ts:77</code> · feedback-state · effort S</summary>

**Code.** describeAuthError branches on status 0/502/504 and then `switch (error.code)` with `default: return error.message` (lines 53-79). There is no message-based fallback for the credentials case, even though the whole point of the mapper (per the commit 'Explain auth failures instead of showing Invalid login credentials') is that GoTrue's prose says 'Invalid login credentials' for both a wrong password and a non-existent account. Any AuthError instance that arrives without a populated `code` — an older GoTrue deployment, a non-AuthApiError, or a proxied response — falls through to `default` and prints that exact string again.

**User impact.** On a project whose auth server does not populate error codes, the user sees the bare 'Invalid login credentials' the mapper exists to eliminate, with no hint that the app and website share one login or that Create account is the next step. The mapper appears to work in testing and silently degrades in production.

**Fix.** Upstream web repo change in src/core/utils/authErrors.ts: before the default branch, add a message-regex fallback mapping /invalid login credentials/i to the invalid_credentials copy, /email not confirmed/i to the email_not_confirmed copy, and /user already registered/i to the exists copy. Keep code matching as the primary path.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code reads as claimed: describeAuthError (src/core/utils/authErrors.ts:40-79) does message-regex matching only for the network and timeout cases, then switches on error.code with `default: return error.message` at line 78, so an error without a populated code prints GoTrue's raw prose. But the impact is hypothetical for this build: package.json pins @supabase/supabase-js ^2.110.9, and GoTrue error codes have been populated by default for a long time, so on this project's own auth server the invalid_credentials branch is what actually fires. There is no observed user affected; it is defence-in-depth against a deployment this app does not run against. That is a P3 hardening nit, not P2 feedback breakage.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile's Goal, Activity, Gender and unit selectors are 40pt tall, below the 44pt minimum</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:164</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The Choice component's Pressable sets `minHeight: 40` (line 164). It is used five times on Profile: Goal (468), Activity level (481), Gender (494), Weight unit (511) and Height unit (522). The project's own token is HIT_SIZE, used correctly by Button (src/components/Button.tsx:75), IconButton (line 133) and Field (src/components/Layout.tsx:166).

**User impact.** Five rows of horizontally scrolling pills that change the user's calorie formula are 4pt under the minimum target size, and they sit inside a horizontal ScrollView, so a slightly low tap scrolls the row instead of selecting. Users with reduced motor precision mis-set their activity level, which shifts TDEE by up to 35%.

**Fix.** Add HIT_SIZE to the existing tokens import at profile.tsx:37 and change `minHeight: 40` (line 163) to `minHeight: HIT_SIZE`, trimming paddingVertical if the pill looks heavy.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The measurement is right — profile.tsx:163 sets `minHeight: 40` inside Choice's Pressable, used at 468, 481, 494, 511 and 522, against HIT_SIZE = 44 (src/theme/tokens.ts:124) which Button (Button.tsx:75), IconButton (132-133) and Field (Layout.tsx:166) all honour. Two parts of the finding are wrong: profile.tsx:37 imports only `{ radius, spacing }` from @/theme/tokens, so HIT_SIZE is NOT already imported; and the 'a slightly low tap scrolls the row instead of selecting' claim does not follow — a 4pt shortfall in the vertical axis has no bearing on a horizontal ScrollView's responder. What is left is a 4pt token violation with no demonstrated failure, which is a nit.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Profile mixes settings with food-library management and duplicates the Dashboard's weight card</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:358</code> · information-architecture · effort L</summary>

**Code.** Profile renders, in order: an identity + Weight/BMI/Streak stat card (309-355), WeightTargetCard (358) which is also on the Dashboard (app/(tabs)/index.tsx:173), Health Connect (360), Targets (411), About you (437), Weight log listing 20 entries (535), Body measurements with a Navy body-fat estimate (569), Custom foods (645), Meal templates (679), Appearance (714) and the account block (739). Progress already owns weight charting and health sync; Diary owns foods and templates.

**User impact.** A user who opens Profile to sign out or switch to dark mode has to scroll past a weigh-in form, a body-fat estimator, a 20-row weight table and two food-management lists. A user who wants to delete a bad custom food would never guess it lives under Profile. The one thing every user eventually needs — Sign out — is dead last on the longest screen in the app.

**Fix.** Move Weight log and Body measurements to Progress (which already has the weight chart), move Custom foods and Meal templates behind the Diary's food-search sheet, drop the duplicate WeightTargetCard, and let Profile be identity, targets, about-you, health connections, appearance and account. Pin the account/sign-out block to the top or make it reachable from the header.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The inventory is accurate (identity card 309-355, duplicate WeightTargetCard at profile.tsx:358 and index.tsx:173, then eight Sections and the account block at 739), but the stated impact is not what the code produces: Section defaults to `defaultOpen = false` (profile.tsx:74), so the weigh-in list, body-fat estimator, custom foods and meal templates are all collapsed 56pt rows — nobody scrolls past a 20-row weight table to reach Sign out. Only Targets (419) and Health Connect (365) auto-open, and only when unset. The real residual issue is discoverability — food-related management living under Profile rather than Diary, and a card duplicated from the Dashboard — which is a structural nit with no measurable user cost, not P2 friction.

</details>

<details><summary><b>A signed-in user cannot change their password, change their email or delete their account anywhere in the app</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:756</code> · broken-flow · effort L</summary>

**Code.** The account block at profile.tsx:739-762 contains the sync status line, the email as plain text (754) and a Sign out button — nothing else. AuthProvider's context (src/lib/AuthProvider.tsx:10-29) exposes no update or delete operation, and a repo-wide grep for `auth.updateUser`, `deleteUser`, 'Delete account' and 'Change password' across app/ and src/ returns zero hits.

**User impact.** A user who suspects their password is compromised, or who wants their health data removed, has no path at all inside the app — and with no forgot-password flow either, the only way to change a password is to find the website. Account deletion is also a standard store-review and privacy expectation for an app holding weight and body-measurement history.

**Fix.** Add an Account section to Profile with 'Change password' (supabase.auth.updateUser after re-authentication) and 'Delete account' (an edge function that removes the auth user and the user_data row), both routed through describeAuthError for failures.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

### Design system & accessibility

#### P1 (1)

<details><summary><b>textMuted (stone-500) fails 4.5:1 on every dark surface — it is the colour of every caption, every unit, and the tab bar labels</b> — <code>src/theme/tokens.ts:103</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** Both themes set `textMuted: stone[500]` (#78716C) — light at tokens.ts:71, dark at tokens.ts:103 — and `Label` (src/components/Text.tsx:105) hardcodes `color: theme.textMuted`. Measured ratios for #78716C: dark surface stone-900 #1C1917 = 3.65:1; dark canvas stone-950 #0C0A09 = 4.12:1; dark surfaceRaised #221F1D = 3.42:1; light glass (0.55 white over the Backdrop jade wash) ≈ 4.12:1; dark glass (0.55 stone-900 over the dark wash) ≈ 3.41:1. It only clears 4.5:1 on plain white (4.80:1) and light canvas (4.59:1). This colour carries every `<Label>` ("Eaten today", "kcal today", "Sets", "Elapsed", "est. 1RM kg", the set-grid column heads at app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:568-581) and every `Body tone="muted"` unit and denominator, often at size 10–12 (app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:302, app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:368).

**User impact.** In dark mode — the mode the app ships a persisted toggle for — every caption and every unit next to a number is below the AA floor. A user checking whether the 182 next to their protein ring is grams or kcal cannot read the caption in a gym with glare or with any degree of low vision.

**Fix.** In src/theme/tokens.ts raise dark `textMuted` to stone[400] (#A8A29E, 6.9:1 on stone-900) and light `textMuted` to stone[600] (#57534E, 7.6:1 on white; 6.6:1 on light glass). If a third step below textSecondary is genuinely needed, add `textFaint` and restrict it to non-essential decoration — not to `Label`, which names data.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Code is exactly as described: tokens.ts:71 and :96 both set `textMuted: stone[500]` (#78716C), and Text.tsx:105 hardcodes `color: theme.textMuted` inside `Label`. I recomputed the WCAG ratios myself: #78716C L=0.16915 → 4.79:1 on #FFFFFF, 4.59:1 on stone-50 canvas, 3.65:1 on stone-900 surface, 4.12:1 on stone-950 canvas, 3.42:1 on #221F1D surfaceRaised. So dark mode genuinely fails AA for every `<Label>` and every `Body tone="muted"` unit/denominator. Two extras the finding missed that make it worse: the tab bar's inactive labels are textMuted at fontSize 10 (app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:114,120), and onboarding's UnitToggle renders the *unselected* unit in textMuted on `theme.border` — 3.82:1 light, 3.16:1 dark (onboarding.tsx:117,134), i.e. an interactive control label below AA in both modes. Severity is the only thing wrong: nothing here blocks a task, causes data loss or crashes, so this is not P0 by the stated rubric. It is a real, app-wide AA failure → P1.

</details>

#### P2 (16)

<details><summary><b>Field never forwards its label to the input — the seven Profile inputs with no placeholder announce as a bare 'text field'</b> — <code>src/components/Layout.tsx:162</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `Field` (src/components/Layout.tsx:157-183) renders `{label ? <Label>{label}</Label> : null}` and then `<TextInput ... {...props} />`. The `label` prop is never passed as `accessibilityLabel`, and React Native has no `htmlFor` equivalent — the visible Label is a separate, unassociated node. Callers that pass only `label`: app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:388 (step goal), 421 (goal weight), 442 (Name), 445 (Age), 456 (Height), 592 & 606 (four measurement fields); app/onboarding.tsx:413 (name), 459 (Age), 483/492/500 (height cm, feet, inches — these have no `label` at all, only a `FieldHeader` sibling), 526 (weight), 589 (goal weight); app/login.tsx:181 (Email), 191 (Password).

**User impact.** A VoiceOver/TalkBack user tabbing through first-run setup or Profile lands on a row of edit boxes announced only as "text field, double tap to edit" — no way to tell age from height from weight. Onboarding cannot be completed non-visually, and it is a mandatory gate before the tabs.

**Fix.** Layout.tsx `Field`: `accessibilityLabel={props.accessibilityLabel ?? label}`. That fixes profile.tsx:442/445/456/592/606 (the genuinely unnamed ones) and upgrades the placeholder-only announcements elsewhere from a hint to a name.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Layout.tsx:157-183 is as quoted: `{label ? <Label>{label}</Label> : null}` then `<TextInput ... {...props} />` with no `accessibilityLabel` default. The call sites listed all exist. But the impact is overstated: React Native maps `placeholder` to the native placeholder/hint, which both VoiceOver (UITextField falls back to placeholder) and TalkBack (EditText hint) announce. So onboarding.tsx:459 ('e.g. 28'), 483 ('e.g. 175'), 492/500 ('feet'/'inches'), 526 ('e.g. 72.5'), login.tsx:181/191 ('you@example.com'/'At least 6 characters') and profile.tsx:388 ('8000'), 421 (placeholder = current weight) all announce something usable — 'Onboarding cannot be completed non-visually' is not supported by the code. The genuinely nameless inputs are the ones with a label and no placeholder: profile.tsx:442 (Name), 445 (Age), 456 (Height), 592 and 606 (the four measurement fields) — seven inputs announced only as 'text field' plus their current value. The one-line fix is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The onboarding plan's headline calorie number is Figtree, not Fraunces — the one screen that sells the app breaks the type signature</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:630</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** app/onboarding.tsx:630-640 renders the daily target as `<Body style={{ fontSize: 40, lineHeight: 46, fontVariant: ['tabular-nums'], color: theme.status.good }} weight="semibold">{`${plan.calories} kcal`}</Body>` — Figtree_600SemiBold, not `StatValue`/Fraunces. Same file: 657-662 renders each macro as `<Body weight="semibold" style={{ fontSize: 20, color: m.color, fontVariant: ['tabular-nums'] }}>{`${m.value}g`}</Body>`; 612 renders `You burn roughly ${plan.tdee} kcal a day` in Body; 576-580 renders the pace figure in an OptionRow label. `StatValue` is imported nowhere in onboarding.tsx. The doc (docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:72) states "Every data number wears Fraunces… Use `<StatValue>` rather than styling text ad hoc."

**User impact.** The number the user is asked to commit to — their daily calorie target — renders in a different typeface and weight than the identical number everywhere else in the app. Land on the Dashboard hero two seconds later and the same 2,140 is a visibly different shape, which reads as two different systems rather than one.

**Fix.** Replace the hand-styled Body at app/onboarding.tsx:630-640 with `<StatValue size={40} color={theme.brandText}>{plan.calories}</StatValue>` plus a separate `<Body tone="muted">kcal</Body>`, and the macro figures at 657-662 with `<StatValue size={20} color={m.color}>`. Drop the manual `fontVariant` — StatValue already applies tabular-nums.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Verified line for line. onboarding.tsx:630-640 renders the daily target as a `Body` with `fontSize: 40, fontVariant: ['tabular-nums'], color: theme.status.good, weight="semibold"` — Figtree_600SemiBold, not Fraunces. 657-662 does the same for the three macro figures at fontSize 20, 612 puts the TDEE in a Body sentence, 576-580 puts the pace figure in an OptionRow label. The import at line 20 is `{ Body, Label, SectionTitle }` — StatValue is genuinely never imported in this file. Real binding-rule violation (docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:72-77). Severity only: a typeface mismatch on one screen is something a user notices but nothing is broken, misplaced or unusable → P2, not P1.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Glass used on four surfaces the design doc forbids, three of them wrapping body paragraphs</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:357</code> · design-system · effort M</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:16-26 allows GlassSurface only for the tab bar/FAB, scroll-under headers, modals-sheets-pickers, the Dashboard hero, the Coach plan card, and badges over imagery — and says "Never … for long lists, dense stat tables, body paragraphs". `GlassSurface` is used outside that list at: app/onboarding.tsx:357 (the whole five-step wizard card — it contains every paragraph of setup copy, e.g. 374-377, 409-411, 454-456, plus all the form fields); app/login.tsx:161 (the sign-in form); app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:309 (the identity card, which also holds a three-column stat table at 331-345); app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:719 (the live session card, which holds a three-column stat table at 748-764). app/goals.tsx:437 is an allowed coach card but stuffs five `FigureCell` stat cells (511-550) and three body paragraphs (445-447, 552-554, plus `Notice` blocks) inside the blur.

**User impact.** Paragraph copy and dense figures sit on a translucent, low-contrast, luminance-varying backdrop — the exact case the rule exists to prevent. Combined with the textMuted finding, muted captions on these glass cards measure ~3.4:1 in dark mode. It is also four extra offscreen blur passes on screens that scroll.

**Fix.** Swap `GlassSurface` for `Surface` at app/onboarding.tsx:357, app/login.tsx:161, app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:309 and app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:719. On app/goals.tsx keep the glass but move the FigureCell grid and the rationale/Notice paragraphs out into a sibling `Surface` beneath it, leaving only the headline, phase pill and calorie figure on glass.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** All four sites verified: onboarding.tsx:357 wraps the entire five-step wizard — every setup paragraph (374-377, 409-411, 454-456) and every form field — in GlassSurface; login.tsx:161 puts the sign-in form on glass; profile.tsx:309 puts the identity card plus a three-column stat table (331-345) on glass; workout.tsx:719 puts the live-session card plus a three-column stat table (748-764) on glass (its own comment calls it 'the one glass card here'). goals.tsx:437 is an allowed coach card but holds five FigureCells (511,519,527,538,544) and three paragraphs (445-447, 552-554) plus Notices. docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:24-26 forbids exactly this. Severity: legibility is degraded, not destroyed — the content is still readable and every task still completes, and the contrast half of the impact is already counted in the textMuted finding. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Goal denominators, counts and percentages render in Figtree beside Fraunces values — the two typefaces sit in the same row</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:185</code> · design-system · effort M</summary>

**Code.** The achieved value goes through StatValue while its denominator does not, in the same flex row: app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:182-185 `<StatValue size={18}>{Math.round(value)}</StatValue><Body size={12} tone="muted">{`/ ${Math.round(goal)} g`}</Body>`; same pattern at diary.tsx:208-209, 219, 223, 276, 289. Other data numbers rendered in Body: src/components/WeightTarget.tsx:101 (the goal weight), :109 (`${Math.round(progress.fraction * 100)}%`), :133 (weeks); src/components/StepsCard.tsx:79 (`/ ${goal.toLocaleString()}`), :87 (7-day average); app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:349 (body-fat %), :351 (lean mass kg), :440 (age and height), :538, :648, :665, :682, :700 (counts and per-serving kcal); app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1084 (`Found {totalRecordsFound} historical entries`), :1088-1090 (weigh-in count, height cm, body-fat %); and every macro chip in app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:104/107/110 and app/food-search.tsx:96/99/102, where `Pill` renders its string child through `Body` (src/components/Layout.tsx:141). Roughly 25 sites.

**User impact.** "148 / 180 g" renders with the 148 in Fraunces tabular figures and the 180 in Figtree proportional figures, at different widths and different digit shapes, 4pt apart. Columns of these across the diary and the progress summary do not line up, which is exactly what tabular figures were adopted to prevent.

**Fix.** Wrap the figure inside those Body strings in `<StatValue size={…} tone="muted">` (the pattern already used correctly at app/(tabs)/index.tsx:231 and app/chat.tsx:109), and give `Pill` a numeric variant — or have the MacroChips pass `children` rather than a template string so the grams go through StatValue.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Spot-checked every cited site and they are all real: diary.tsx:182-185 (StatValue 18 beside `Body size={12}` '/ 180 g'), 208-209, 219, 223, 276, 289; WeightTarget.tsx:101, 109, 133; StepsCard.tsx:79, 87; profile.tsx:349 (Pill body-fat %), 351, 440, 538, 648, 665, 682, 700; progress.tsx:1084, 1088-1090; and Pill routes its string child through `Body` at Layout.tsx:141, which is what puts the grams in diary.tsx:104/107/110 and food-search.tsx:96/99/102 in Figtree. The counter-examples cited (index.tsx:231, chat.tsx:109, progress.tsx:932) do exist, so the codebase is internally inconsistent rather than uniformly wrong. Mixed digit shapes and non-tabular widths in the same baseline row is a real, visible defect at P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Protein and carbs used as small text colour fail AA in light mode (3.20:1 on the chip tint)</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:104</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** The macro palette was validated for marks, not for type — but it is used as text colour at small sizes. `Pill` renders its label in the passed colour on a `${color}1A` (10%) tint: app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:104/107/110 and app/food-search.tsx:96/99/102 render `P 12g` / `C 30g` at `size={12}`. Measured on the light tint over white: protein #168BE1 = 3.20:1, carbs #C97004 = 3.20:1 (on plain white both are 3.61:1). Fat #9B204A (6.9:1) and fiber #924BAC (5.5:1) pass. The same two colours are also used for text below the large-text threshold at app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:182 (`StatValue size={18}` = 13.5pt) and app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:927 (`size={16}`). Dark mode passes (protein 5.13:1, carbs 4.90:1).

**User impact.** On the diary's food rows — the densest, most-read list in the app — the protein and carb figures are the two least legible things on the screen in light mode, which is the default. A user scanning "did this meal have carbs?" gets the answer in the lowest-contrast text on the card.

**Fix.** Do not substitute the palette (it is validated). Instead, in `Pill` (src/components/Layout.tsx:141) render the label in `theme.text`/`theme.textSecondary` and let the colour live in a 8pt dot swatch — the pattern already used at app/chat.tsx:105 and app/goals.tsx:136. For diary.tsx:182 and progress.tsx:927 either raise the size past 18.7px semibold or drop to `tone="primary"` with a colour dot.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** I recomputed the ratios rather than trusting them. Protein #168BE1 L=0.24067 → 3.61:1 on white; over the Pill's 10% tint (Layout.tsx:133 `${color}1A`, composited to ~#E7F3FC) → 3.22:1. Carbs #C97004 L=0.23844 → 3.64:1 on white, 3.24:1 on the tint. Both fail AA for 12px text, and the chips at diary.tsx:104/107/110 and food-search.tsx:96/99/102 render at `size={12}`. Fat #9B204A is 7.8:1 and fiber passes, exactly as claimed. progress.tsx:927 (`StatValue size={16}` in row.color) and diary.tsx:182 (size 18) are also under the large-text threshold. Dark mode does pass. The proposed fix correctly refuses to touch the validated palette and points at the dot-swatch pattern already used at chat.tsx:105 and goals.tsx:136/155. P2 is right — the chips carry a spelled-out accessibilityLabel wrapper (diary.tsx:103), so this is legibility friction, not a blocker.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Haptics fire on tab switching and on opening chat (both plain navigation) but not on logging water</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:88</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:86 — "Haptics on meaningful commits only (set completed, plan accepted, food logged) — never on scroll or plain navigation." app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:88 fires `void Haptics.selectionAsync()` inside the tab bar's `onPress` before `navigation.navigate(route.name)`, and :150 fires `void Haptics.impactAsync(ImpactFeedbackStyle.Light)` before `router.push('/chat')`. Both are pure navigation. Meanwhile the water quick-add buttons at app/(tabs)/index.tsx:558-577 call `addWater(date, amount)` — a real write to the diary — with no haptic, and the −250 IconButton at :580 likewise.

**User impact.** Every tab tap buzzes, so the taptic signal carries no information; the user stops noticing it. Then the one dashboard control that actually commits data (quick-add water, the most-tapped write on the home screen) gives no physical confirmation at all, so it is unclear whether the tap registered.

**Fix.** Delete `Haptics.selectionAsync()` at app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:88, the `impactAsync` at :150, and drop `haptic` from the 'Ask AI' Button at app/(tabs)/index.tsx:146. Add `void Haptics.impactAsync(Haptics.ImpactFeedbackStyle.Light)` to the water quick-add at index.tsx:562 and the decrement at :583.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Both sites confirmed: _layout.tsx:88 `void Haptics.selectionAsync()` fires inside the tab `onPress` before `navigation.navigate`, and :150 fires `impactAsync(Light)` before `router.push('/chat')`. The water quick-add at index.tsx:557-577 calls `addWater(date, amount)` with no haptic, and the −250 IconButton at 580-586 likewise, while the codebase does haptic real commits correctly elsewhere (workout.tsx:234 set completed, food-search.tsx:414 food logged, diary.tsx:452/625 template saved/applied). The finding missed a third navigation haptic: index.tsx:144-150 renders `<Button label="Ask AI" haptic onPress={() => router.push('/chat')} />` — the same plain navigation, buzzing again. Severity P2 stands; the fix needs the extra site.

</details>

<details><summary><b>useReducedMotion() is not imported anywhere; every animation and screen transition ignores the OS setting</b> — <code>src/components/Button.tsx:63</code> · accessibility · effort M</summary>

**Code.** A repo-wide grep for `useReducedMotion` returns zero hits. The only Reanimated animation in the app is `Button`'s press scale — src/components/Button.tsx:45-46 `useSharedValue(1)` / `useAnimatedStyle(() => ({ transform: [{ scale: scale.value }] }))`, driven at :63 and :66 by `withTiming(0.97, { duration: 120 })`. It is an unconditional transform. The FAB also transforms on press without Reanimated (app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:167 `transform: [{ scale: pressed ? 0.96 : 1 }]`), and app/_layout.tsx:68/75/79 sets `animation: 'slide_from_right'` and `'slide_from_bottom'` on the stack. The doc (docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:84-85) specifies 150ms press and "Honour `useReducedMotion()` — skip transforms, keep opacity"; the implementation uses 120ms and never checks.

**User impact.** A user with Reduce Motion enabled — often because motion triggers nausea or vestibular symptoms — still gets every button scaling under their thumb and every screen sliding in. Nothing in the app respects the setting.

**Fix.** In src/components/Button.tsx read `const reduced = useReducedMotion()` and gate the transform: animate `opacity` to 0.85 instead of `scale` when reduced, and raise the duration to 150ms per spec. Do the same for the FAB transform at app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:167, and switch the stack to `animation: reduced ? 'fade' : 'slide_from_right'` in app/_layout.tsx.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** A repo-wide grep for `useReducedMotion|reduceMotion|AccessibilityInfo|allowFontScaling` returns exactly one hit — the sentence in docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:85. A grep for reanimated/withTiming/Animated confirms Button.tsx is the only animated component in the app (lines 11, 31, 63, 66), and its `withTiming(0.97, { duration: 120 })` is an unconditional transform at 120ms against the spec's 150ms. The FAB transform at _layout.tsx:167 and the stack animations at app/_layout.tsx:68/75/79 are also unconditional. Nothing in the app reads the OS setting. P2 is right.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Two control sets are below the 44pt minimum, including the unit toggle in first-run setup</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:127</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `UnitToggle` in app/onboarding.tsx:122-137 styles each option with `paddingHorizontal: 12, paddingVertical: 5` around a `<Body size={12}>` (lineHeight 12 × 1.45 ≈ 17.4) — total height ≈ 27pt, with no `minHeight`. It is used twice on the busiest setup step (height units at 472-479, weight units at 516-523). `Choice` in app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:156-170 sets `minHeight: 40` — used five times (Goal, Activity level, Gender, Weight unit, Height unit). `HIT_SIZE = 44` is exported from src/theme/tokens.ts:124 and used correctly almost everywhere else.

**User impact.** The kg/lbs switch in onboarding is a 27pt target sitting next to the weight field — a user who wanted to enter 160 lbs and misses the toggle enters 160 kg instead, which then seeds their first weigh-in, their BMR and every calorie target downstream. The Profile choice rows are equally fiddly with a thumb.

**Fix.** Add `minHeight: HIT_SIZE` to the Pressable at app/onboarding.tsx:127 (keeping the visual pill compact via an inner View if the smaller look is wanted) and raise `minHeight: 40` to `HIT_SIZE` at app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:163.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** onboarding.tsx:122-137: each UnitToggle option is `paddingHorizontal: 12, paddingVertical: 5` around `<Body size={12}>` (lineHeight 12*1.45 = 17.4) with no minHeight and no hitSlop → ~27pt tall, used at 472-479 (cm / ft-in) and 516-523 (kg / lbs) on the busiest setup step. profile.tsx:163 is `minHeight: 40` on `Choice`, used five times (468, 481, 494, 511, 522). HIT_SIZE = 44 is exported at tokens.ts:124 and honoured nearly everywhere else (onboarding's own gender buttons use it at :432). The mis-entry scenario is plausible: the toggle governs the field directly beneath it and the entered number seeds the first weigh-in (finish() at 270-284) and every downstream target. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>ProgressTrack announces a bare percentage with no name — the diary alone has five nameless focus stops</b> — <code>src/components/MacroRing.tsx:96</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** src/components/MacroRing.tsx:95-97 sets `accessibilityRole="progressbar"` and `accessibilityValue={{ min: 0, max: 100, now: Math.round(clamped * 100) }}` but no `accessibilityLabel` and no way to pass one — the props are `{ progress, color, height, over }`. In app/(tabs)/diary.tsx the label wrapper (`accessible accessibilityLabel={...}` at 179-181) closes before the track is rendered at 187, so each of the four MacroStats plus the calorie track (211) plus the four in progress.tsx (939) become separate, unnamed stops. The `over` prop's own doc comment claims it is "announced, not color-only", but nothing announces it — the critical fill at :110 is colour-only.

**User impact.** Swiping through the diary with a screen reader, the user hears "Protein, 148 of 180 grams" then immediately "82 percent" with no idea what the 82 percent belongs to — four times in a row, then a fifth for calories. The over-goal state is conveyed only by the bar turning red.

**Fix.** Add a required `label` prop to `ProgressTrack` and set `accessibilityLabel={over ? `${label}, over goal` : label}`. Pass it at every call site (diary.tsx:187/211, progress.tsx:939, index.tsx:238/536, StepsCard.tsx:83, WeightTarget.tsx:107, RestTimer.tsx:109), or alternatively set `accessibilityElementsHidden` where the parent already carries the full sentence.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The main claim holds: MacroRing.tsx:86-97 sets `accessibilityRole="progressbar"` with min/max/now and has no `label` prop at all, and in diary.tsx the `accessible` wrapper closes at :186 before the track renders at :187, so each of the four MacroStats plus the calorie track at :211 becomes its own nameless 'N percent' stop; same at progress.tsx:939, index.tsx:238/536, StepsCard.tsx:83, WeightTarget.tsx:107, RestTimer.tsx:109. The sub-claim about `over` is wrong, though: `over` is only ever passed at index.tsx:238, and the sibling block at index.tsx:240-249 is `accessible accessibilityLabel={status}` with an AlertTriangle and the words 'N kcal over your goal' — so the over state is announced with icon and words, it is not colour-only. Drop that half; severity stays P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The assistant FAB floats 30pt above where scroll content ends, covering the bottom-right of the last card</b> — <code>src/components/Layout.tsx:103</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `Screen` pads the scroll view with `paddingBottom: TAB_BAR_SPACE + insets.bottom + spacing.xl` = 76 + insets + 24 = 100 + insets (src/components/Layout.tsx:20 and :103). The FAB is positioned at `bottom: Math.max(insets.bottom, 8) + 74` with `height: 56` (app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:157-161), so it occupies insets+74 through insets+130 — its top edge is 30pt above where content stops. `TAB_BAR_SPACE` accounts for the tab bar only; nothing in Layout.tsx knows the FAB exists.

**User impact.** Scrolled to the bottom of Progress or Workout, the FAB sits on top of the last row's right-hand column — exactly where the est. 1RM and volume figures are right-aligned (app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1271-1274, app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1212-1219). The user has to scroll back up to read a number that is nominally on screen.

**Fix.** Raise `TAB_BAR_SPACE` in src/components/Layout.tsx:20 to clear the FAB (76 → 140), or better, export a `FAB_SPACE` constant from app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx and add it to the paddingBottom at Layout.tsx:103 so the two stay in sync.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Geometry checks out. Layout.tsx:20 `TAB_BAR_SPACE = 76`; Layout.tsx:103 `paddingBottom: TAB_BAR_SPACE + insets.bottom + spacing.xl` = insets + 100. _layout.tsx:157-160 places the FAB at `bottom: Math.max(insets.bottom, 8) + 74`, `height: 56`, so it spans insets+74 to insets+130 — its top 30pt sit inside the content area, over the right-hand 74pt column. Scrolled to the bottom of Workout that column holds the right-aligned est. 1RM figures (workout.tsx:1271-1274) and on Progress the e1RM figures (progress.tsx:1212-1219). Nothing in Layout.tsx knows the FAB exists. P2.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The set-grid inputs use a fixed 44pt height and the chart axis a hardcoded tick offset — both clip at large OS font sizes</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:132</code> · accessibility · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `numberInputStyle` (app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:131-143) sets `height: HIT_SIZE` — a hard 44, not `minHeight` — with `fontSize: 15` and `paddingHorizontal: 2`, and the row's columns are fixed at `COL_SET = 44`, `COL_UNIT = 22`, `COL_REPS = 52` (145-149). Nothing in the app sets `allowFontScaling={false}` (grep returns zero hits), so at 200% Dynamic Type the 15pt text inside those 44pt boxes clips vertically and "225.5" cannot fit a 52pt reps column. Similarly app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:58-63 fixes `CHART_H = 152` and `GUTTER = 44`, and `YAxis` (:96-104) absolutely positions each tick at `top: tick.y - 7`, a hardcoded half-line-height for an 11pt figure.

**User impact.** A user with large text who logs a set sees the weight and reps they typed cut off mid-glyph, in the one input that must be right, mid-workout, with a rest timer running. The Progress y-axis labels overlap each other and spill outside the 44pt gutter.

**Fix.** Change `height: HIT_SIZE` to `minHeight: HIT_SIZE` at workout.tsx:132 and let COL_REPS/COL_UNIT flex; in progress.tsx derive the YAxis tick offset from onLayout instead of the hardcoded -7 at :98 and scale GUTTER with PixelRatio.getFontScale().

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The core defect is real: workout.tsx:131-143 `numberInputStyle` sets `height: HIT_SIZE` — a hard 44, not minHeight — with `fontSize: 15`, and a repo-wide grep confirms `allowFontScaling` and `maxFontSizeMultiplier` appear nowhere, so at 200% OS font scale a ~30px glyph is boxed in 44px. progress.tsx:58-63 (CHART_H 152, GUTTER 44) and the `top: tick.y - 7` hardcoded half-line-height at :98 are also exactly as described. One detail is wrong: '225.5 cannot fit a 52pt reps column' — the weight input is `[numberInputStyle, { flex: 1 }]` at :298 and only the reps input takes COL_REPS = 52 (:316), so the weight has room; the reps column is the one that will clip. Severity P2 stands.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Health Connect status line is painted in the protein data colour (3.6:1) with no icon, and success and failure share the treatment</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1113</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:1112-1116 renders `<Body size={13} tone="muted" style={{ color: theme.macro.protein }}>{healthSyncMessage}</Body>`. That same state holds error strings ("Health Connect is not installed…" :643, "Permission … was not granted" :656, `syncRes.error` :665) and the success string "Successfully imported N weigh-in entries!" (:687) — one colour, no icon, no words distinguishing kind. The colour used is `theme.macro.protein` (#168BE1 light), a validated data-series hue, on a plain white Surface = 3.61:1 at 13px, below the 4.5:1 floor. docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:56-58 reserves status colours and requires "an icon and words — never color alone".

**User impact.** After tapping "Sync from Google Fit", failure and success look identical: same blue, same size, no icon. A user whose permission was denied reads a blue line in the colour the charts above use for protein and cannot tell at a glance whether the import worked.

**Fix.** Split the state into `{ kind: 'error' | 'success', text }` and render it with the existing pattern from app/food-search.tsx:495-512 — `TriangleAlert` + `theme.status.critical` for failures, `Check` + `theme.status.good` for the import confirmation. Never `theme.macro.*` for status.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** progress.tsx:1112-1116 does render `<Body size={13} tone="muted" style={{ color: theme.macro.protein }}>{healthSyncMessage}</Body>`, and the same state holds the failures (:643, :649, :656, :665) and the success string (:687) — and since :688 clears healthSyncResult, the success text lands in that same blue Body. `theme.macro.protein` is #168BE1 = 3.61:1 on a white Surface at 13px, below AA. Both doc rules (56-58) are broken: a data-series hue used for status, with no icon. One overstatement: 'failure and success look identical … cannot tell at a glance whether the import worked' — the sentences themselves differ plainly ('Permission … was not granted' vs 'Successfully imported N weigh-in entries!'), so the meaning is available, just not the treatment. P2 stands on the contrast failure plus the palette misuse.

</details>

<details><summary><b>No screen title or section heading exposes accessibilityRole="header"</b> — <code>src/components/Text.tsx:115</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `SectionTitle` (src/components/Text.tsx:115-125) renders a plain `<Text>` with no `accessibilityRole`, and the `Screen` header renders it the same way (src/components/Layout.tsx:87). A grep for `accessibilityRole="header"` across app/ and src/ returns nothing. Screens stack many sections — app/(tabs)/progress.tsx has four `CardHeader`s per tab, app/(tabs)/diary.tsx renders six `MealCard` titles.

**User impact.** Screen-reader users cannot use heading navigation to jump between Breakfast, Lunch and Dinner in the diary, or between the chart cards on Progress; they must swipe through every figure in between.

**Fix.** Add `accessibilityRole="header"` to the Text in `SectionTitle` (src/components/Text.tsx:121) and to the screen title in src/components/Layout.tsx:87.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Confirmed: Text.tsx:115-125 `SectionTitle` renders a bare `<Text>` with no accessibilityRole, the screen title goes through it at Layout.tsx:87, and a repo-wide grep for `accessibilityRole="header"` returns zero hits. The screens really do stack many sections — diary.tsx renders six MealCard `SectionTitle`s (461) plus Saved meals (580), progress.tsx has multiple CardHeaders per tab (574-583), profile.tsx has nine Sections. I am raising this rather than lowering it: heading roles are the standard mobile mechanism for skipping content (WCAG 1.3.1 Info and Relationships, level A), and without them a screen-reader user must swipe through every figure between Breakfast and Dinner. That is noticeable friction on the app's most-used list, not a nit. The one-line fix is correct.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Validation and error messages appear silently — no live region or alert role anywhere except one spot in goals.tsx</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:684</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** onboarding.tsx:684-688 renders `{error ? (<Body size={13} style={{ color: theme.status.critical }}>{error}</Body>) : null}` — the message that appears when `next()` fails `validateStep` (286-295). It has no `accessibilityLiveRegion`, no `accessibilityRole="alert"`, and focus stays on the Continue button, so nothing is spoken. The same pattern repeats at login.tsx:59-71 (`Message`, rendered at 201-202 for every auth failure), diary.tsx:535-542 ('Give this meal a name…'), food-search.tsx:602-609 ('Enter an amount greater than zero.'), lift-picker.tsx:370-377 (nameError), workout.tsx:397-413 (the 'Tap the bin again' confirmation) and goals.tsx:850-877 (the Incomplete/Mismatch/Balanced line). The app already knows the API: goals.tsx:583-586 wraps the plan-accepted confirmation in `accessibilityLiveRegion="polite"`.

**User impact.** A screen-reader user presses Continue in first-run setup, nothing is announced, and the wizard appears to have frozen on step 2 — the reason ('Enter an age between 13 and 100') is rendered off-focus below the button. Same for a failed sign-in and for the 'enter an amount greater than zero' block in food logging.

**Fix.** Add `accessibilityLiveRegion="polite"` (Android) plus `accessibilityRole="alert"` to each error container, or better: give `Layout.tsx` a shared `<InlineError>` that carries both, and use it at onboarding.tsx:685, login.tsx:63, diary.tsx:536, food-search.tsx:603, lift-picker.tsx:371 and goals.tsx:851/860.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>The Dashboard hero maps a continuous ratio onto the reserved status hues — hitting your calorie goal exactly renders red with an alert triangle</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/index.tsx:204</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** index.tsx:204 `const tone: Tone = progress >= 1 ? 'critical' : progress >= 0.9 ? 'warning' : 'good'`, :205 `const color = theme.status[tone]`, :207 `const StatusIcon = tone === 'good' ? Check : AlertTriangle`. That colour then drives both the sentence (246) and the ProgressTrack fill (238). At eaten === goal, `remaining` is 0 and `over` is false, so the card reads '0 kcal left today' in `status.critical` (#B91C1C) beside an AlertTriangle; from 90% to 99.9% — landing on target — it reads amber warning with the same triangle. docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:56 reserves status colours and forbids reusing them as a data series.

**User impact.** The dashboard exists to answer 'am I on track today'. A user who eats 1,950 of a 2,000 kcal goal — the best possible outcome — is shown a warning triangle in amber, and one who lands exactly on 2,000 gets a red alert. The one screen that should confirm success reports it as a problem.

**Fix.** Decouple hue from the ratio: keep `status.good`+Check for anything at or under goal, `status.warning`+AlertTriangle only once `over` is true, and let the bar carry the magnitude (it already flips to critical via the `over` prop at MacroRing.tsx:110). Reserve `status.critical` for a genuine overshoot threshold rather than for >= 100%.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

<details><summary><b>Inactive tab bar labels are 10px in textMuted on glass — the app's primary navigation is its smallest, lowest-contrast text</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:117</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** _layout.tsx:112-123 renders each inactive tab as `<Icon size={22} color={theme.textMuted} strokeWidth={1.8} />` and `<Body size={10} weight="medium" style={{ color: focused ? theme.brandText : theme.textMuted }}>`. fontSize 10 is the smallest type anywhere in the app (the next smallest is 11, used for `Label` and captions), and #78716C over the glass overlay measures ~4.1:1 in light and ~3.4:1 in dark — below AA — on top of the blurred, luminance-varying backdrop the tab bar sits on (Backdrop.tsx:60-72 puts a jade bloom directly behind it).

**User impact.** Four of the five destination names in the app's main navigation are illegible at a glance in dim light or for anyone with mild low vision; the user navigates by icon shape and colour alone, and the active/inactive distinction leans on brandText vs textMuted, both small.

**Fix.** Raise the tab label to size 11 and use `theme.textSecondary` (stone-600 light / stone-400 dark, both >= 6.5:1) for the inactive state; the selected state already carries an indicator bar and a weight change, so contrast is free to rise on both.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

#### P3 (7)

<details><summary><b>Onboarding uses the reserved status.good token as its brand accent in 14 places (same hex as brand today, so no visual difference — a latent coupling, not a visible one)</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:65</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** `theme.status.good` is used as a UI accent throughout app/onboarding.tsx: OptionRow border and fill (65, 66, 77, 78), OptionRow label colour (86), the step name (337), the progress segments (350), the feature-icon tiles (393, 396), the horizon sentence (597), the plan panel border and fill (625, 626), the headline calorie figure (635) and the closing bullet icons (674). `theme.brand`/`theme.brandText` — used for selection on every other screen (app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:168, app/food-search.tsx:133, app/lift-picker.tsx:59, app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:531) — is never referenced in this file. docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:56 says status is "reserved and never reused as a data series".

**User impact.** Onboarding looks like a different product than the app it hands off to: its selected chips are status green while every selected chip afterwards is jade brand. Worse, the plan panel and its calorie figure wear the same green the app later uses to mean "on track" / "goal reached", so a first-time user learns green = selected, then meets green = you are doing well.

**Fix.** In app/onboarding.tsx swap every `theme.status.good` used for selection or emphasis to `theme.brand` (fills) / `theme.brandText` (text and icons), matching profile.tsx's `Choice` and food-search's `ChoiceChip`. Reserve `status.good` for the one genuine status moment on this screen — none currently exists.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** The code claim is true — `theme.status.good` is the accent at onboarding.tsx:65,66,77,78,86,337,350,393,396,597,625,626,635,674, and a grep for `theme.brand` in that file returns zero hits. But the stated impact is wrong, and I checked the tokens: light `status.good = jade[600]` which is *the identical hex* to light `theme.brand` (#0C8261), and dark `status.good = jade[400]` which is the identical hex to dark `theme.brandText` (#38BC8D). So onboarding does NOT render a different colour from the rest of the app — 'its selected chips are status green while every selected chip afterwards is jade brand' is false; they are the same pixels. What remains is genuine token-semantics misuse (a reserved status token doing brand duty, so a later change to status.good silently restyles onboarding) with no perceivable difference today. That is a P3, not a P1. The fix as written is still the right change.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Four blur surfaces are hand-rolled instead of using GlassSurface, and none of them has the required top highlight</b> — <code>src/components/Layout.tsx:69</code> · design-system · effort M</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:28 requires every glass surface to carry "a hairline border and a subtle top highlight". `GlassSurface` implements that highlight (src/components/Glass.tsx:62-70). Four surfaces re-implement the BlurView + overlay pair by hand and omit the highlight entirely: src/components/Layout.tsx:69-75 (screen header), app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:61-67 (tab bar), app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:176-183 (assistant FAB), app/chat.tsx:528-534 (composer). Each repeats the same four lines — `tint={theme.glass.tint}`, `intensity={theme.glass.intensity + 20}`, `experimentalBlurMethod`, `<View style={[StyleSheet.absoluteFill, { backgroundColor: theme.glass.overlay }]} />`. Additionally app/food-search.tsx:425 and app/lift-picker.tsx:224 pass `bordered={false}` to GlassSurface, dropping its hairline in favour of a single bottom border.

**User impact.** The four most persistent chrome elements — header, tab bar, FAB, composer — read as flat translucent rectangles rather than as the same material as the hero and coach cards, and the intensity/overlay recipe has to be kept in sync by hand across four files. On Android, where the blur can degrade, they lose the edge treatment that makes the surface look deliberate.

**Fix.** Same fix (an `edge` prop on GlassSurface, then replace the four hand-rolled stacks). Note food-search.tsx:425 / lift-picker.tsx:224 only drop the border, not the highlight, so they need nothing beyond the shared component.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Accurate. Glass.tsx:62-70 is the LinearGradient top highlight, and the four hand-rolled stacks (Layout.tsx:69-75, _layout.tsx:61-67, _layout.tsx:176-181, chat.tsx:528-534) each repeat tint/intensity+20/experimentalBlurMethod/overlay with no highlight; all four do carry a hairline border, so only the highlight is missing. food-search.tsx:425 and lift-picker.tsx:224 do pass `bordered={false}` (they keep the highlight, since `highlight` defaults true — the finding's wording implies otherwise). Severity: a missing 1.5px gradient line on chrome has no consequence for any user task; the sync-by-hand argument is maintenance, not user impact. P3.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Nine hardcoded colour literals bypass useTheme(), including the whole Backdrop palette</b> — <code>src/components/Backdrop.tsx:25</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:38 — "Always read them through `useTheme()`; never hardcode a hex in a component." Violations: src/components/Backdrop.tsx:25 `'rgba(120,113,108,0.16)'` / `'rgba(214,211,209,0.55)'` (stone-500, stone-300), :31 `'rgba(18,161,117,0.22)'` / `'rgba(56,188,141,0.30)'` (jade-500, jade-400), :61 `'rgba(12,130,97,0.20)'` / `'rgba(113,213,175,0.28)'` (jade-600, jade-300) — six palette steps re-encoded by hand, while Backdrop.tsx:5 imports `jade` and never uses it; src/components/Glass.tsx:99 and app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:169 `shadowColor: '#1C1917'`; app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:184 `<Sparkles size={24} color="#FFFFFF" />`; app/onboarding.tsx:370 `<Sparkles size={28} color="#FFFFFF" />`; app/login.tsx:21 `color: '#FAFAF9'` and :36 `stroke="rgba(250,250,249,0.25)"`.

**User impact.** The ambient wash behind every screen is defined in literals that no longer track the token file, so a palette change updates the cards and leaves the background behind them stale. The two `#FFFFFF` icons will stay white if `brandOn` ever moves off white for the dark theme, and the shadow colour will not follow a canvas change.

**Fix.** In src/components/Backdrop.tsx use the already-imported `jade` and `stone` scales with an alpha helper instead of the six rgba strings. Replace `#FFFFFF` with `theme.brandOn` at app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:184 and app/onboarding.tsx:370, `#FAFAF9`/`rgba(250,250,249,…)` with `stone[50]` in app/login.tsx:21/36, and add a `shadow` token to src/theme/tokens.ts for the two `#1C1917` shadow colours.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Every literal is where the finding says: Backdrop.tsx:25 (two rgba stone values), :31 and :61 (four rgba jade values) with `import { jade } from '@/theme/tokens'` at line 5 that is never used in the file; Glass.tsx:99 and _layout.tsx:169 `shadowColor: '#1C1917'`; _layout.tsx:184 and onboarding.tsx:370 `color="#FFFFFF"`; login.tsx:21 `'#FAFAF9'` and :36 `stroke="rgba(250,250,249,0.25)"`. Real violation of docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:38. But the user-facing consequence today is nil: `brandOn` is '#FFFFFF' in both themes so the two icon literals render identically, and the shadow is only applied in light mode where #1C1917 is correct. This is a latent-drift/maintenance defect, which is a P3 by the stated rubric.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Sheet is missing from src/components and seven ad-hoc copies of the chip/segmented pattern have drifted apart</b> — <code>src/components/Layout.tsx:117</code> · design-system · effort L</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:95-98 lists `Sheet` as a component to build against — it does not exist in src/components/ (the directory holds only Backdrop, Button, Glass, Layout, MacroRing, RestTimer, StepsCard, Text, WeightTarget). The doc's rule "if a pattern appears three times, it belongs here" is broken by: (1) selectable chip/segmented — app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:491 `Segmented` (minHeight 44, radius.tight, brand fill), app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:136 `Choice` (minHeight 40, radius.pill, brand fill), app/onboarding.tsx:100 `UnitToggle` (≈27pt, radius.pill, surface fill), app/onboarding.tsx:425 inline gender buttons (44pt, radius.control, status.good fill), app/lift-picker.tsx:45 `Chip` (44pt, radius.pill), app/food-search.tsx:112 `ChoiceChip` (44pt, radius.pill), app/goals.tsx:239 `PresetCell` (44pt, radius.control) — seven implementations, three heights, three radii, two accent colours; (2) macro chip trio — app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:95, app/food-search.tsx:87 (byte-identical), app/chat.tsx:87 (different markup, and the only one using StatValue); (3) label-plus-figure tile — app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:548 `StatTile`, app/(tabs)/index.tsx:616 `GlanceTile`, app/goals.tsx:141 `FigureCell`; (4) icon-plus-text notice box — app/goals.tsx:183 `Notice`, app/chat.tsx:463-483, app/food-search.tsx:495-512, app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:831-839 and :1058-1064, app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:579-587; (5) search field — app/food-search.tsx:149 `SearchBar` vs app/lift-picker.tsx:256-301 inline.

**User impact.** This duplication is the mechanism behind most other findings in this report: the sub-44pt targets, the status-green accent, and the missing-Fraunces figures all live in one of these forks. A user moving between Profile, Progress and setup sees the same control at three different sizes with three different corner radii.

**Fix.** Add `Chip`/`Segmented`, `StatTile`, `Notice` and `SearchField` to src/components (Layout.tsx or new files), built on the tokens, then delete the seven chip forks, three tile forks, six notice forks and the duplicate SearchBar. Add the missing `Sheet` and use it for the food-search serving step (app/food-search.tsx:524-673), which is currently a full-screen state swap.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Inventory verified. `Sheet` really is absent — src/components/ holds exactly Backdrop, Button, Glass, Layout, MacroRing, RestTimer, StepsCard, Text, WeightTarget. The seven chip/segmented forks all exist as described (progress.tsx:491 minHeight 44 + radius.tight + brand; profile.tsx:136 minHeight 40 + radius.pill; onboarding.tsx:100 ~27pt + radius.pill; onboarding.tsx:425 44pt + radius.control + status.good; lift-picker.tsx:45 44pt + radius.pill; food-search.tsx:112 44pt + radius.pill; goals.tsx:239 44pt + radius.control). Tile forks (progress.tsx:548, index.tsx:616, goals.tsx:141) and notice forks (goals.tsx:183, chat.tsx:463-483, food-search.tsx:495-512, workout.tsx:830-839 and :1057-1064, profile.tsx:578-587) confirmed, as is the SearchBar duplication (food-search.tsx:149 vs lift-picker.tsx:256-301). This is a code-architecture finding: its user-visible consequences (the sub-44pt target, the status-green accent, the missing Fraunces) are already reported as their own findings, so on its own it is a P3.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Three onboarding controls have accessibilityRole but no accessibilityLabel</b> — <code>app/onboarding.tsx:54</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:102 requires every touchable to have both. `OptionRow` (app/onboarding.tsx:54-58) sets `accessibilityRole="radio"` and `accessibilityState={{ selected }}` with no label — it relies on RN collapsing its two child `Body` nodes (label + detail). `UnitToggle`'s option (:122-126) and the gender buttons (:425-429) do the same. These are the only touchables in app/ missing an explicit label; every other Pressable in the codebase carries one.

**User impact.** An OptionRow announces its whole detail sentence ("Lightly active — exercise 1 to 3 days a week…") as its name, and the unit toggle announces just "cm" with no indication it switches the field beside it, so it is unclear what the control does before activating it.

**Fix.** Add `accessibilityLabel={label}` plus `accessibilityHint={detail}` to app/onboarding.tsx:54, `accessibilityLabel={`Use ${o.label}`}` at :122, and `accessibilityLabel={g}` at :425.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** onboarding.tsx:54-58 sets `accessibilityRole="radio"` and `accessibilityState={{ selected }}` with no label; :122-126 (UnitToggle option) and :425-429 (gender) do the same. I checked the rest of app/ and src/ — every other Pressable does carry an explicit accessibilityLabel (e.g. _layout.tsx:80, diary.tsx:341/618, workout.tsx:265/322/349/506/523, food-search.tsx:123/204, lift-picker.tsx:51/239/287/413, goals.tsx:250, profile.tsx:84/160, index.tsx:432/561), so the claim that these are the only three holds. Announcement still works via child-text collapsing, which is why this is correctly a P3.

</details>

<details><summary><b>Three ad-hoc corner radii bypass the token scale</b> — <code>app/lift-picker.tsx:426</code> · design-system · effort S</summary>

**Code.** docs/MOBILE-DESIGN.md:81 fixes radii at cards 24, controls 16, pills 999 (tokens.ts:111 also exports `tight: 12`). Off-scale values: app/lift-picker.tsx:426 `borderRadius: 8` on the compound-lift checkbox, app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:144 `borderRadius: 3` on the legend swatch, app/login.tsx:145 `borderRadius: 26` on the 96×96 app-mark tile. (The circle radii — profile.tsx:315, _layout.tsx:160, onboarding.tsx:75 — are correct geometry for their sizes but would read more clearly as `radius.pill`.)

**User impact.** Minor visual drift: the login mark's corner does not match any card on the screens that follow it, and the checkbox in the lift creator is rounder than every other control around it.

**Fix.** Use `radius.tight` (12) for the lift-picker checkbox and the progress legend swatch, and `radius.card` (24) for the login tile; use `radius.pill` for the elements intended as circles.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** lift-picker.tsx:426 `borderRadius: 8` on the 24x24 compound-lift checkbox, progress.tsx:144 `borderRadius: 3` on the 12x12 legend swatch, login.tsx:145 `borderRadius: 26` on the 96x96 app mark — none of these is 12/16/24/999 from tokens.ts:111. The circle radii called out as fine (profile.tsx:315, _layout.tsx:160, onboarding.tsx:75) are indeed correct geometry. P3 is right; the visual consequence is negligible.

</details>

<details><summary><b>The same assistant destination is announced under two different names on different screens</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/diary.tsx:690</code> · accessibility · effort S</summary>

**Code.** diary.tsx:690 and workout.tsx:1145 label the header button `accessibilityLabel="Open AI Assistant"`, while index.tsx:125 and the global FAB at _layout.tsx:148 label the identical `router.push('/chat')` action `accessibilityLabel="Open the nutrition assistant"`. index.tsx:146 adds a third visible name for it, 'Ask AI'.

**User impact.** A screen-reader user learns one name for the control on the Dashboard and hears a different one for the same control on the Diary and Workout tabs, so it does not read as the same destination.

**Fix.** Pick one string — 'Open the nutrition assistant' matches the FAB and the screen's own title ('Assistant', chat.tsx:313) — and use it at diary.tsx:690 and workout.tsx:1145.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** found by verifier, not original auditor

</details>

### Performance

#### P1 (7)

<details><summary><b>Every store write re-serializes the entire store to AsyncStorage; in the workout set grid that is once per keystroke</b> — <code>src/store/useStore.ts:24</code> · performance · effort M</summary>

**Code.** `persist(immer(createAppState), { name: 'macrofit-storage', storage: createJSONStorage(() => AsyncStorage), onRehydrateStorage: ... })` — no `partialize`, no throttle/debounce. zustand 4.5.7's persist (node_modules/zustand/middleware.js:500-507) wraps the setter: `api.setState = function (state, replace) { savedSetState(state, replace); void setItem() }` and the inner config setter does `set.apply(...); void setItem()`. `setItem` is `options.partialize(_extends({}, get()))` (default partialize = identity, so it shallow-copies the whole state incl. ~50 action closures) then `storage.setItem` → `JSON.stringify(newValue)`. So EVERY `set()` synchronously stringifies the full state on the JS thread. The hot paths that call `set()` per keystroke/tap: `updateSet` (app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:216 `onChange({ weightKg: ... })` fires on every character), `updateFoodEntry` (diary.tsx:318 `applyServings`), `addWater` (index.tsx:562 `onPress={() => addWater(date, amount)}`), and `renameWorkout` (workout.tsx:658) which additionally does `useStore.setState({ workoutLog: ...map(...) })` per character.

**User impact.** A user typing "102.5" into a weight field triggers five full-store JSON.stringify + AsyncStorage writes. With a realistic 6-month payload (see persist-no-partialize) that is ~30-80ms of blocked JS thread per character on a mid-range Android, on top of the React re-render cascade — characters appear late, get dropped, or the cursor jumps. Logging a set mid-workout, the app's core task, becomes visibly broken.

**Fix.** Same fix (wrap the storage handed to createJSONStorage so setItem coalesces on a ~1s trailing timer, flush on AppState background as AuthProvider.tsx:149-154 already does, and add partialize). Drop the diary/water claims from the rationale — the only true per-keystroke writers are workout.tsx:216 (weight), :222 (reps), :228 (RPE) and :658 (rename). Moving those three to onEndEditing/onBlur, mirroring diary.tsx:323 commitDraft, removes the hot path entirely.

**Verifier (ADJUSTED).** Mechanism verified end to end. src/store/useStore.ts:24-32 passes no partialize/throttle; node_modules/zustand/middleware.js:493-506 wraps both api.setState and the inner set() with `void setItem()`, and setItem -> createJSONStorage.setItem (middleware.js:303-305) does a synchronous `JSON.stringify(newValue)` on the JS thread. So every set() does serialize the whole persisted state. The workout hot path is real: app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:213-217 `handleWeight` calls `onChange({weightKg})` from `onChangeText`, which reaches `updateSet` (appState.ts:537), and workout.tsx:658-665 `renameWorkout` calls `useStore.setState` per character. BUT two of the four cited hot paths are wrong: (a) diary.tsx:317 `applyServings` is NOT called per keystroke — the servings Field at diary.tsx:387-397 uses `onChangeText={setDraft}` (local state) and only commits via `onBlur`/`onSubmitEditing` -> `commitDraft` (323-330); (b) index.tsx:562 `addWater` is one tap, not a keystroke. Severity: this makes typing progressively laggier on a large log, but the user can still complete the log — that is P1, not P0 (no crash, no data loss, task still completable).

</details>

<details><summary><b>Typing one character in a set field re-renders every workout ever logged</b> — <code>app/(tabs)/workout.tsx:1116</code> · performance · effort L</summary>

**Code.** `const workoutLog = useStore(s => s.workoutLog)` (line 1116) subscribes WorkoutScreen to the whole array. immer's `updateSet` (src/core/store/appState.ts:537) replaces the set → sets array → exercise → exercises array → session → `workoutLog` reference, so a single character retypes the whole selector.
